{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{104.56387pt}{40.97197pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{104.56387pt}{-40.97197pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}
{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 144.69 55.72 L 144.69 -55.72}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@fill\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 126.69 16.55 h 35.98 v 12.25 h -35.98 Z}{stroke:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{92.86075pt}{15.20798pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

(74)

where the dashed line denotes the product of interfering diagrams.
Here, we account for the fact that various mother particles
ij
or
ai

may occur in the diagrams (
67
) by
labeling them with
ij/ij^{\prime}
in the
FS
case
and
ai/ai^{\prime}
in the
IS
case.
The corresponding multiplet indices and masses are written
as
l_{ij}/l_{ij}^{\prime}
,
l_{ai}/l_{ai}^{\prime}

and
m_{ij}/m_{ij}^{\prime}
,
m_{ai}/m_{ai}^{\prime}
, respectively.
For example, in the
EWSM
, a
\gamma\mathrm{W}^{\pm}
-pair can
originate from the splitting of a W or its associated would-be Goldstone
boson, while a
\mathrm{W}^{\pm}\mathrm{W}^{\mp}
-pair can be produced via
the splitting of a photon, Higgs boson, Z, or the corresponding would-be
Goldstone boson.
In (
74
), all possible interferences may
occur.
Accordingly, we denote the contributions from the individual diagrams of
type I with
fixed
intermediate
\varphi_{ij}

or
\varphi_{ai}
as
partial matrix elements

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\varphi_{ij})}\Big]_{\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}},\qquad\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\varphi_{ai})}\Big]_{\varphi_{a}\varphi_{i}X}^{\kappa_{a}\kappa_{i}}.

(75)

While being more involved than the consideration of individual
amplitudes, this approach has several advantages.
First, the obtained results may be directly applied to shower
formulations or
NLO
subtraction algorithms, avoiding the necessity
of non-trivial combinations of formulas for individual amplitudes.
Second, external wave functions
\mathcal{W}
always occur in pairs
in (
74
), which allows for their
replacement via (spin-projected) completeness relations of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}^{\kappa}_{\varphi}(p)\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{\kappa}_{\varphi}(p)=C_{\varphi}(p^{\mu},r^{\mu},\kappa),

(76)

where
C_{\varphi}
is a tensor-valued function (in Lorentz or Dirac
space) and the bar denotes conjugation, i.e.
\bar{\psi}=\psi^{\dagger}\gamma^{0}
for spinors
\psi
and complex conjugation otherwise.
The light-like gauge vector
r^{\mu}
is defined as
in (
61
) and provides the precise definition of the
spin reference axis for the considered particle.
For vector bosons, the role of
r^{\mu}
has been discussed in Section
2.7
,
and the

transverse
polarization sum for an on-shell vector boson of
momentum
p
and mass
M_{V}
(
p^{2}=M_{V}^{2}
) reads

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda=\pm}\varepsilon^{\mu\ast}_{\lambda}(p)\varepsilon^{\nu}_{\lambda}(p)

\displaystyle=-g^{\mu\nu}-\frac{p^{\mu}r^{\nu}+p^{\nu}r^{\mu}}{M_{V}}-r^{\mu}r^{\nu},\qquad pr=-M_{V},\qquad r^{2}=0.

(77)

The spinors for on-shell fermions and antifermions of
momentum
p
and mass
m_{f}
(
p^{2}=m_{f}^{2}
) fulfill

\displaystyle u_{\kappa}(p)\bar{u}_{\kappa}(p)

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\kappa}(\not{p}+m_{f}),\qquad v_{\kappa}(p)\bar{v}_{\kappa}(p)=\Sigma_{\kappa}(\not{p}-m_{f}),

(78)

respectively, with the spin projection matrix

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+\kappa\gamma^{5}\not{s}}{2},\qquad s^{\mu}=\frac{p^{\mu}}{m_{f}}+r^{\mu},\qquad pr=-m_{f},\qquad r^{2}=0.

(79)

We recall that
\Sigma_{\pm}
projects onto
the fermion and antifermion spinors
u_{\pm}
and
v_{\pm}
, which both correspond to
states with spin projection
\pm 1/2

in the reference direction.
In the rest frame of
p^{\mu}
, this direction is
provided by the unit vector

\hat{\boldsymbol{r}}
.
In any frame with

\boldsymbol{p}\parallel\boldsymbol{r}
,
these spinors correspond to states with helicity
\pm 1/2
.
In summary, the use of (
76
) bypasses
the need for explicit parametrizations of spinors and polarization
vectors and thereby enables us to work in a way that is both manifestly
independent of the Lorentz frame and the precise definition of
polarization.
In our approach, the latter
is reflected by the fact that the
vectors
r^{\mu}
are found to drop out of our results, without making any
further assumptions.

Plugging propagators, vertices, and wave functions from
Section
2.4

into (
74
), and employing completeness
relations like (
76
), we obtain
asymptotic expressions

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}_{\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}X}\right|^{2}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathop{\resizebox{1423121}{0}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\textstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1423121}{0}{\hbox{$\textstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1256560}{0}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1212868}{0}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}}e^{2}\sum_{\varphi_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}},\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\frac{1}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}

\displaystyle\cdot T^{(n)}_{\varphi_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}X}(p_{ij})\otimes\tilde{\mathcal{P}}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}(p_{i},p_{j},r_{i},r_{j},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j})\otimes\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}X}(p_{ij}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\kappa_{a}\kappa_{i}}_{\varphi_{a}\varphi_{i}X}\right|^{2}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathop{\resizebox{1423121}{0}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\textstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1423121}{0}{\hbox{$\textstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1256560}{0}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1212868}{0}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}}e^{2}\sum_{\varphi_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}},\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{ai}^{2}-m_{ai}^{2}}\frac{1}{p_{ai}^{2}-m_{ai}^{\prime\,2}}

\displaystyle\cdot T^{(n)}_{\varphi_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}X}(p_{ai})\otimes\tilde{\bar{\mathcal{P}}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{a}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{i}}(p_{a},p_{i},r_{a},r_{i},\kappa_{a},\kappa_{i})\otimes\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}X}(p_{ai})

(80)

in terms of some functions
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}

and
\tilde{\bar{\mathcal{P}}}
,
which are precursors of the splitting function we are after.
These may carry Lorentz or Dirac indices, are completely independent of
the underlying
n
-particle process, and only depend on the splitting
type and kinematics.
All process dependence is contained in the
stripped matrix
elements

T
, defined by factoring wave functions off full matrix
elements
\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\kappa_{ij}}_{\varphi_{ij}X}

\displaystyle=T_{\varphi_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\otimes\mathcal{W}_{\varphi_{ij}}^{\kappa_{ij}}(p_{ij}),\qquad

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\kappa_{ai}}_{\varphi_{ai}X}

\displaystyle=T_{\varphi_{ai}X}(p_{ai})\otimes\mathcal{W}_{\varphi_{ai}}^{\kappa_{ai}}(p_{ai}).

(81)

The symbolized product
\otimes
represents multiplication, including
contraction of Lorentz and Dirac indices, depending on the types of the
particles
ij
and
ai
.
Whenever
\otimes
appears, the order of factors does not necessarily
represent the position of the various (Lorentz and Dirac) indices.
This must be inferred from the specific case.
For example, if
ij
and
ai
are vector
bosons, (
81
) reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\lambda_{ij}}_{V_{ij}X}

\displaystyle=T^{(n)\mu}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\,\varepsilon_{\lambda_{ij},\mu}^{\ast}(p_{ij}),\qquad

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\lambda_{ai}}_{V_{ai}X}

\displaystyle=T^{(n)\mu}_{V_{ai}X}(p_{ai})\,\varepsilon_{\lambda_{ai},\mu}(p_{ai}).

(82)

If they are fermions, we instead have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\kappa_{ij}}_{f_{ij}X}

\displaystyle=\bar{u}_{\kappa_{ij}}(p_{ij})\,T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}(p_{ij}),\qquad

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\kappa_{ai}}_{f_{ai}X}

\displaystyle=\bar{T}^{(n)}_{f_{ai}X}(p_{ai})\,u_{\kappa_{ai}}(p_{ai}),

(83)

and in the case of antifermions

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\kappa_{ij}}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}

\displaystyle=\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\,v_{\kappa_{ij}}(p_{ij}),\qquad

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\kappa_{ai}}_{\bar{f}_{ai}X}

\displaystyle=\bar{v}_{\kappa_{ai}}(p_{ai})\,T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ai}X}(p_{ai}).

(84)

Here and in the following, we include a bar in the definition of
T
for
outgoing antifermions
f_{ij}
and incoming fermions
f_{ai}

to indicate the Dirac structure.

In order to evaluate (
80
) in the quasi-collinear
limits, we employ the Sudakov parametrizations given
in (
69
) and (
70
).
We assign auxiliary invariant masses
\tilde{m}_{ij}
and
\tilde{m}_{ai}

to the collinear four-momentum
\bar{p}
to properly account for the cases

m_{ij}\neq m^{\prime}_{ij}
and
m_{ai}\neq m^{\prime}_{ai}
.
The auxiliary masses are consistently counted as
\mathcal{O}\left(m\right)
by adding

\tilde{m}_{ij}\to\lambda\tilde{m}_{ij}
and

\tilde{m}_{ai}\to\lambda\tilde{m}_{ai}

to the rescaling procedure (
73
).
Special care has to be taken when any of the intermediate particles is a
vector boson
V
.
In this case, (
80
)
involves contractions of
\bar{p}^{\mu}
with

T^{(n)\mu}_{VX}(p)
.
Via the
WI
(
46
), this becomes proportional to

M_{V}T^{(n)}_{GX}(p)
, which is of
\mathcal{O}\left(m\right)
.
8
8

8

The
WI
requires the contraction with
p=\bar{p}+\mathcal{O}\left(m\right)
,
but that does not change this argument.

Therefore,
\bar{p}^{\mu}
must be counted as
\mathcal{O}\left(m\right)
when contracted
with the stripped underlying amplitude.
We stress that no assumption on
r^{\mu}
other than
r^{\mu}=\mathcal{O}\left(m\right)
,
and thereby no constraints on the precise definition of polarizations,
are made when evaluating the quasi-collinear limits.
Keeping only terms of lowest order in
\lambda

from (
80
) finally provides expressions
of
\mathcal{O}\left(m^{-2}\right)
.
Similar to
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
and
\tilde{\bar{\mathcal{P}}}
, we can
read off the
FS
and
IS
splitting functions

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big),\qquad\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{a}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{a},\kappa_{i}\big),

(85)

respectively, which are of
\mathcal{O}\left(m^{2}\right)
.
These correspond to the splitting processes

\displaystyle[\varphi_{ij}^{\ast}/\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}^{\ast}]\to\varphi_{i}\,\varphi_{j},\qquad\varphi_{a}\to\varphi_{i}\,[\varphi_{ai}^{\ast}/\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}^{\ast}],

(86)

according to the corresponding subdiagrams
in (
74
).
The square brackets denote interfering mother particles, while the
asterisk indicates off-shellness.
The procedure of taking the quasi-collinear limits is demonstrated more
explicitly in Section
4.2.1
.

As indicated by the arguments of the splitting functions
\mathcal{P}

and
\bar{\mathcal{P}}
in (
85
), they are found to be
independent of auxiliary quantities such as the vectors
n^{\mu}
,

r^{\mu}
, and the masses
\tilde{m}_{ij}
,
\tilde{m}_{ai}
.
As mentioned above, this implies that our results are
independent of the precise definition of spin reference axes for the
individual external particles.
Moreover, working in general
R_{\xi}
gauge, we observe that all
dependence on the gauge parameters
\xi_{V}
drops out.

Our computations are performed within
Mathematica
13, employing

FeynArts
3.11
Hahn:2000kx
for the generation of diagrams of
type (
75
) and their translation into analytical
expressions.
For the handling of Lorentz and Dirac algebra, we
use
FeynCalc
9.3.1
Shtabovenko:2016sxi
;
Shtabovenko:2020gxv
;
Kublbeck:1990xc
.
Most of the results have been checked in a second, independent calculation
with inhouse
Mathematica
routines.

4
Derivation of splitting functions

4.1
Symmetries of splitting functions

The splitting functions are subject to certain symmetry and crossing
relations, which are detailed in this section.
Moreover, there are additional relations under charge conjugation for
splitting functions involving fermions.
This is detailed in Section
4.3
.

4.1.1
Symmetries under

\boldsymbol{i\leftrightarrow j}

and

\boldsymbol{a\leftrightarrow i}

The diagrams that determine
the
FS
splitting
functions
\mathcal{P}
are invariant under simultaneous exchange of the
fields
\varphi_{i}
,
\varphi_{j}
, polarization labels
\kappa_{i}
,

\kappa_{j}
, and momenta
p_{i}
,
p_{j}
.
Via (
69
), this means that
\mathcal{P}
is
symmetric under

\displaystyle i\leftrightarrow j\equiv\big\{\varphi_{i}\leftrightarrow\varphi_{j},\ m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j},\ \kappa_{i}\leftrightarrow\kappa_{j},\ z\to(1-z),\ k_{\perp}^{\mu}\to-k_{\perp}^{\mu},\dots\big\},

(87)

i.e.

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{j}}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{i}}\big(1-z,-k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{j},\kappa_{i}\big)\Big|_{m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j}}.

(88)

The dots in (
87
) indicate that there might be more properties
of
i
and
j
relevant to characterize the splitting functions, such as
the phases of would-be Goldstone-boson
fields, which are suppressed in the notation here (see Section
4.2.7
).
To avoid confusion, we stress that we understand the fields and
polarization labels in the definition (
85
) of

\mathcal{P}
and
\bar{\mathcal{P}}
to be assigned to the particles of
the splitting processes (
86
) by their
position
in
the respective lists of arguments, regardless of what
indices
the
fields and polarization labels may carry themselves.
In contrast, the masses
m_{a}
,
m_{i}
, and
m_{j}
(and all
remaining parameters such as phases of would-be Goldstone-boson fields)
are assigned
implicitly by their indices
.
Specifically, in the functions

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\alpha}_{\gamma}{}^{\beta}_{\delta}(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}),\qquad\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{\alpha}_{\gamma}{}^{\beta}_{\delta}(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}),

(89)

the fields
\gamma
and
\delta
, the polarizations
\kappa_{1}
and

\kappa_{2}
, and the masses
m_{i/a}
and
m_{j/i}
correspond to the
first and second external particle in the
FS
and
IS
splitting process of (
86
), respectively.
Therefore,
m_{i/a}
and
m_{j/i}

always
belong
to the fields
\gamma
and
\delta
, with polarizations
\kappa_{1}
and

\kappa_{2}
.
Thus, before the interchange
m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j}
,
the mass
m_{i}
in the splitting function on the r.h.s. of (
88
) corresponds to the
field
\varphi_{j}
,
not
to
\varphi_{i}
.
9
9

9

To spell this out even more explicitly
in a specific example, in the
FS
splitting function

\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{j}}{}^{f_{ij^{\prime}}}_{f_{i}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\lambda,\kappa\big)

the vector boson
V_{j}
carries the mass
m_{i}
,
polarization
\lambda
, and momentum fraction
z
, while the fermion

f_{i}
carries the mass
m_{j}
, polarization
\kappa
, and
momentum fraction
1-z
.

,
10
10

10

In the following, similar issues do not arise for the polarization
labels
\kappa_{a/i/j}
.
Their position in the argument lists of splitting functions is always
consistent with the position of the fields with the respective index.

The splitting functions on the r.h.s. of (
88
) may, finally, be related to splitting functions
for fields with indices in conventional order by modification of
couplings, see (
125
)
and (
210
) below.

Analogously, the diagrams corresponding to the
IS
splitting
functions
\bar{\mathcal{P}}
are symmetric under
crossing
of the
fields
\varphi_{a}
and
\varphi_{i}
, reassigning as well as
flipping the polarizations
\kappa_{a}\leftrightarrow-\kappa_{i}
, and
swapping the momenta
p_{a}
and
-p_{i}
.
Crossing involves charge conjugation of the fields,
i.e. fermions turn
into antifermions and vice versa.
Via (
70
), this corresponds to the symmetry
of
\bar{\mathcal{P}}
under

\displaystyle a\leftrightarrow i\equiv\bigg\{

\displaystyle m_{a}\leftrightarrow m_{i},\ \varphi_{a}\leftrightarrow\bar{\varphi}_{i},\ \kappa_{a}\leftrightarrow-\kappa_{i},

\displaystyle x\to\frac{x}{x-1},\ k^{\mu}_{\perp}\to\frac{-1}{x-1}k^{\mu}_{\perp},\ \bar{p}^{\mu}\to\bar{p}^{\mu}-\frac{x}{x-1}k^{\mu}_{\perp},\dots\bigg\}.

(90)

The kinematic part of these substitutions results from the interchange

p_{a}\leftrightarrow-p_{i}
neglecting some
mass-suppressed terms that are not relevant in the following
symmetry relation of the splitting function expressing crossing.
11
11

11

Denoting the invariant mass of
\bar{p}^{\mu}

in (
70
) with
\tilde{m}_{ai}
, according to
Section
3.2
, the mapping
p_{a}\leftrightarrow-p_{i}
also requires the replacement
\tilde{m}_{ai}^{2}\rightarrow\tilde{m}_{ai}^{2}+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}x^{2}}{(x-1)^{2}}
.
This can be neglected in (
90
) as well because splitting
functions are independent of the auxiliary mass parameter

\tilde{m}_{ai}
.
The physical mass
m_{ai}
corresponding to
\varphi_{ai}
, on the
other hand, is obviously not affected by the crossing
a\leftrightarrow i
.

Note that the transformation of
\bar{p}^{\mu}
and
k^{\mu}_{\perp}

maps
\bar{p}^{\mu}-k^{\mu}_{\perp}
onto itself, so that

p_{ai}^{\mu}=\bar{p}^{\mu}-k^{\mu}_{\perp}+\mathcal{O}\left(m^{2}\right)
is unchanged.
For the splitting functions, we get

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{a}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{a},\kappa_{i}\big)

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\bar{\varphi}_{i}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}}_{\bar{\varphi}_{a}}\Big(\frac{x}{x-1},\frac{-1}{x-1}k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu}-\frac{x}{x-1}k^{\mu}_{\perp},-\kappa_{i},-\kappa_{a}\Big)\Big|_{m_{a}\leftrightarrow m_{i}}.

(91)

4.1.2
Symmetry under

\boldsymbol{ij\leftrightarrow ij^{\prime}}

and

\boldsymbol{ai\leftrightarrow ai^{\prime}}

The
FS
splitting functions are invariant under complex conjugation
combined with exchange of the intermediate particles and the associated
parameters.
Together, the symmetry of
\mathcal{P}
reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)

\displaystyle=\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\ast}_{m_{ij}\leftrightarrow m_{ij}^{\prime}}.

(92)

In the same way, the
IS
splitting functions possess the symmetry
relation

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{a}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{a},\kappa_{i}\big)

\displaystyle=\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{a}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{a},\kappa_{i}\big)\bigg]^{\ast}_{m_{ai}\leftrightarrow m_{ai}^{\prime}}.

(93)

4.1.3
Crossing symmetry between FS and IS splitting functions

The
FS
and
IS
splitting functions can be related to each
other via the crossing symmetry of the associated matrix elements,
combined with a mapping between the kinematic
parametrizations (
69
)
and (
70
).
In other words, crossing relates the

FS
splitting functions
\mathcal{P}
for
the process

\bar{\varphi}_{ai}^{\ast}/\bar{\varphi}_{ai^{\prime}}^{\ast}\to\bar{\varphi}_{a}\varphi_{i}

to the

IS
splitting functions
\bar{\mathcal{P}}
for

\varphi_{a}\to\varphi_{i}[\varphi_{ai}^{\ast}/\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}^{\ast}]
.
This expressed by

\displaystyle\mathrm{FS}\to\mathrm{IS}\equiv\bigg\{

\displaystyle\varphi_{ij}\to\bar{\varphi}_{ai},\ \varphi_{ij^{\prime}}\to\bar{\varphi}_{ai^{\prime}},\ \varphi_{i}\to\bar{\varphi}_{a},\ \varphi_{j}\to\varphi_{i},

\displaystyle m_{i}\to m_{a},\ m_{j}\to m_{i},\ m_{ij}\to m_{ai},\ m_{ij}^{\prime}\to m_{ai}^{\prime},\ \kappa_{i}\to-\kappa_{a},\ \kappa_{j}\to\kappa_{i},

\displaystyle z\to\frac{1}{x},\ k_{\perp}^{\mu}\to\frac{-k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{x},\ \bar{p}^{\mu}\to-\bar{p}^{\mu}+k_{\perp}^{\mu},\dots\bigg\},

(94)

where the kinematic substitutions derive from

p_{i}\to-p_{a}
and
p_{j}\to p_{i}
up to mass-suppressed terms that are not
relevant in the following crossing relation of the splitting functions.
Explicitly, we have

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{a}}{}^{\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{a},\kappa_{i}\big)

\displaystyle=s_{\varphi_{a}}\mathcal{P}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\bigg(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\bigg)\bigg|_{\eqref{eq:fs-to-is-rules}}

\displaystyle=s_{\varphi_{a}}\mathcal{P}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\bigg(\frac{1}{x},-\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{x},-\bar{p}^{\mu}+k_{\perp}^{\mu},-\kappa_{a},\kappa_{i}\bigg)\bigg|_{\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}\to\varphi_{\mathrm{IS}},m_{\mathrm{FS}}\to m_{\mathrm{IS}}},

(95)

where
the substitutions
\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}\to\varphi_{\mathrm{IS}}
and

m_{\mathrm{FS}}\to m_{\mathrm{IS}}
are the ones
for fields and masses in (
94
),
respectively, and
the sign
s_{\varphi_{a}}
is due to the crossing of the
field
\varphi_{a}
between the
FS
and
IS
of the
process (
67
).
It takes the value
s_{\varphi_{a}}=-1
if
\varphi_{a}
is a
fermion or antifermion and
s_{\varphi_{a}}=+1
otherwise.

4.2
Bosonic FS splitting functions

We now turn to the explicit derivation of
FS
splitting functions
for specific splitting processes (
86
), applying
the procedure described in Section
3.2
.
We work in a generic gauge theory with scalars, fermions, and vector
bosons, in general
R_{\xi}
gauge.
First, we focus on splitting processes that involve
bosonic fields
only.
For selected
situations, we demonstrate the application of the symmetries
from Section
4.1
.
Similarly, symmetry relations may be used to obtain all splitting
functions that are not explicitly detailed in the following.
We work in
D=4
space–time dimensions throughout, assuming that
collinear singularities are regularized by the masses of the particles
taking part in the splitting process.

4.2.1
\boldsymbol{V^{\ast}\to VV}

splitting functions

First, we consider the splitting functions for the splitting process

\displaystyle[V_{ij}^{\ast}/V_{ij^{\prime}}^{\ast}]\to V_{i}\,V_{j}.

(96)

The diagrams (
74
) involving such
a subprocess are always accompanied by corresponding diagrams with
internal would-be Goldstone-boson lines, i.e. with the splitting
processes

\displaystyle[V_{ij}^{\ast}/G_{ij^{\prime}}^{\ast}]\to V_{i}\,V_{j},\qquad[G_{ij}^{\ast}/V_{ij^{\prime}}^{\ast}]\to V_{i}\,V_{j},\qquad[G_{ij}^{\ast}/G_{ij^{\prime}}^{\ast}]\to V_{i}\,V_{j}.

(97)

We combine all of these contributions into a common splitting
function.
As detailed below, this proceeds by relating the
amplitudes
T^{(n),\mu}_{VX}
and
T^{(n)}_{GX}

via
WIs
and simplifying the sum
of contributions
upon collecting terms according to their propagator structure.
In this way we merge the contributions from
intermediate vector-bosons and would-be Goldstone-bosons
to (
80
) into a single term

\displaystyle e^{2}\frac{1}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\frac{1}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}\ T^{(n)}_{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}X,\mu}(p_{ij})\left[\tilde{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}(p_{i},p_{j},\lambda_{i},\lambda_{j})\right]^{\mu\nu}T^{(n)\ast}_{V_{ij^{\prime}}X,\nu}(p_{ij}).

(98)

In this procedure, a meaningful combination of the vector-boson and
would-be Goldstone-boson parts is facilitated by the coupling relations
from Section
2.6
.

As already mentioned in Section
3.2
, the
presence of mass-singular factors in longitudinal polarization vectors
poses a severe issue for the diagram selection procedure.
Care must be taken to consistently capture all contributions that are
relevant at the lowest order in
\lambda
and to restore the gauge
cancellations destroyed by naively dropping diagrams of
type II.
The easiest way to deal with this problem is to turn the argument from
Section
2.7
around and employ the
exact

version (
63
) of the
GBET
to
enforce

all gauge cancellations of mass-singular terms from the
outset.
Specifically, we employ this formula to rewrite the
real-emission matrix elements (
66
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}_{V_{i}V_{j}X}

\displaystyle=\left[T^{(n+1)}_{V_{i}V_{j}X}\right]_{\mu_{i}\mu_{j}}\varepsilon_{\lambda_{i}}^{\mu_{i}\ast}(p_{i})\varepsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\mu_{j}\ast}(p_{j})

(99)

with
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{L}
and/or
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{L}
in terms of

scalar
amplitudes.
It is then obvious that diagrams of type II do not contribute at
leading power in
\lambda
.
This is very similar to the approach taken in
Cuomo:2019siu
.

For
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{L}
and
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
,
employing (
63
) for the outgoing field
V_{i}

amounts to

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\mathrm{L}\mathrm{T}}_{V_{i}V_{j}X}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\ast}_{V_{i}}\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\mathrm{G}\mathrm{T}}_{G_{i}V_{j}X}+\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\mathrm{R}\mathrm{T}}_{V_{i}V_{j}X},

(100)

where we defined the partial polarization vector

\varepsilon_{\mathrm{R}}^{\mu}(p)=r^{\mu}(p)
and labeled it with

\lambda=\mathrm{R}
.
Then, we
separately
carry out the procedure from
Section
3.2
for the two amplitudes on the
r.h.s. of (
100
).
This means that we evaluate the sum of the partial matrix elements

\displaystyle\eta^{\ast}_{V_{i}}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\varphi_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{T}}+\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\varphi_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{T}}

(101)

instead of the corresponding longitudinally polarized partial matrix element.
12
12

12

Note that this explicitly retains the R-polarized parts, which are
considered as subleading in the

GBET
(
64
).
However, as we explicitly observe below, both parts contribute at the
same order in the quasi-collinear limit.

While (
100
) is an identity,
switching to its r.h.s. effectively
modifies
the diagram selection procedure for
the terms
\propto p^{\mu_{i}}_{i}/M_{V_{i}}
from the
amplitude with longitudinally polarized
V_{i}
, because
scalar
diagrams of type
I instead of the corresponding
vector-boson
diagrams
are kept.

For two longitudinally polarized external vector bosons
(
\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{L}
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\mathrm{L}\mathrm{L}}_{V_{i}V_{j}X}=\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\mathrm{R}\mathrm{R}}_{V_{i}V_{j}X}+\eta^{\ast}_{V_{i}}\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\mathrm{G}\mathrm{R}}_{G_{i}V_{j}X}+\eta^{\ast}_{V_{j}}\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\mathrm{R}\mathrm{G}}_{V_{i}G_{j}X}+\eta^{\ast}_{V_{i}}\eta^{\ast}_{V_{j}}\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\mathrm{G}\mathrm{G}}_{G_{i}G_{j}X},

(102)

as starting point that is manifestly free of mass-singular contributions.
Again, the diagram selection is now performed for each term separately,
which amounts to the evaluation of

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\varphi_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{R}}+\eta^{\ast}_{V_{i}}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\varphi_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{R}}+\eta^{\ast}_{V_{j}}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\varphi_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{G}}+\eta^{\ast}_{V_{i}}\eta^{\ast}_{V_{j}}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\varphi_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{G}}.

(103)

In order to follow this procedure, we have to consider the following types of diagrams,

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle{}={}

(104)

After employing momentum conservation, the
WI
(
46
) for the
underlying would-be Goldstone amplitude (with outgoing
V_{ij},G_{ij}
),

\displaystyle T^{(n)}_{G_{ij}X}(p_{ij})=\eta_{V_{ij}}\frac{p_{ij}^{\mu}}{m_{ij}}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X,\mu}(p_{ij}),

(105)

and inserting the coupling relations (
53
)
and (
54
),
they translate into the expressions

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}T^{(n)\,\sigma}_{V_{ij}X}\left(p_{ij}\right)G^{\bar{V}_{ij}V_{ij}}_{\sigma\delta}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})g^{\delta\alpha}\varepsilon_{\lambda_{i}}^{\beta\ast}(p_{i})\varepsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\gamma\ast}(p_{j})

\displaystyle\cdot\left[g_{\beta\gamma}\left(p_{i}-p_{j}\right)_{\alpha}+g_{\alpha\gamma}\left(p_{i}+2p_{j}\right)_{\beta}+g_{\alpha\beta}\left(-2p_{i}-p_{j}\right)_{\gamma}\right],

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}={}

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\frac{m_{j}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}}{m_{ij}^{2}}G^{\bar{G}_{ij}G_{ij}}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})T_{V_{ij}X}^{(n)\,\alpha}\left(p_{ij}\right)p_{ij,\alpha}g_{\beta\gamma}\varepsilon_{\lambda_{i}}^{\beta\ast}(p_{i})\varepsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\gamma\ast}(p_{j}),

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\lambda_{j}}={}

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\eta_{V_{i}}\frac{m_{j}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}{m_{i}}T^{(n)\,\sigma}_{V_{ij}X}\left(p_{ij}\right)G^{\bar{V}_{ij}V_{ij}}_{\sigma\gamma}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\varepsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\gamma\ast}(p_{j}),

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\lambda_{j}}={}

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\eta_{V_{i}}\frac{m_{j}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}}{2m_{ij}^{2}m_{i}}

\displaystyle\cdot G^{\bar{G}_{ij}G_{ij}}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})T_{V_{ij}X}^{(n)\,\alpha}\left(p_{ij}\right)p_{ij,\alpha}\varepsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\gamma\ast}(p_{j})\left(2p_{i}+p_{j}\right)_{\gamma},

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}G_{j}X}^{\lambda_{i}\mathrm{G}}={}

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\eta_{V_{j}}\frac{m_{ij}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}}{m_{j}}T^{(n)\,\sigma}_{V_{ij}X}\left(p_{ij}\right)G^{\bar{V}_{ij}V_{ij}}_{\sigma\beta}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\varepsilon_{\lambda_{i}}^{\beta\ast}(p_{i}),

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}G_{j}X}^{\lambda_{i}\mathrm{G}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\eta_{V_{j}}\frac{m_{i}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}-m_{j}^{2}}{2m_{ij}^{2}m_{j}}

\displaystyle\cdot G^{\bar{G}_{ij}G_{ij}}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})T_{V_{ij}X}^{(n)\,\alpha}\left(p_{ij}\right)p_{ij,\alpha}\varepsilon_{\lambda_{i}}^{\beta\ast}(p_{i})\left(p_{i}+2p_{j}\right)_{\beta},

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{G}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\eta_{V_{i}}\eta_{V_{j}}\frac{m_{ij}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}-m_{j}^{2}}{2m_{i}m_{j}}T^{(n)\,\sigma}_{V_{ij}X}\left(p_{ij}\right)G^{\bar{V}_{ij}V_{ij}}_{\sigma\alpha}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\left(p_{j}-p_{i}\right)_{\alpha},

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{G}}={}

\displaystyle 0.

(106)

The last equality is due to (
59
).
Here, we left the propagators symbolic, which are the only places where
the gauge parameters
\xi_{V}
appear.
The independence of the
splitting functions from gauge parameters serves
as an important check on our derivation.
Therefore, we split

\displaystyle G^{\bar{V},V}_{\mu\nu}(p,-p)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p^{2}-M_{V}^{2}}\left(-g_{\mu\nu}+\frac{(1-\xi_{V})}{p^{2}-\xi_{V}M_{V}^{2}}p_{\mu}p_{\nu}\right)=G^{\bar{V}V}_{\mathrm{tHF},\,\mu\nu}(p,-p)+G^{\bar{V}V}_{\xi,\,\mu\nu}(p,-p),

\displaystyle G^{\bar{G}G}(p,-p)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p^{2}-\xi_{V}M_{V}^{2}}=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p^{2}-M_{V}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{(\xi_{V}-1)M_{V}^{2}}{p^{2}-\xi_{V}M_{V}^{2}}\right)=G^{\bar{G}G}_{\mathrm{tHF}}(p,-p)+G^{\bar{G}G}_{\xi}(p,-p)

(107)

into their expressions in ’t Hooft–Feynman gauge
and remainder terms that explicitly depend on
\xi_{V}
,

\displaystyle G^{\bar{V}V}_{\mathrm{tHF},\,\mu\nu}(p,-p)

\displaystyle=\frac{-\mathrm{i}g_{\mu\nu}}{p^{2}-M_{V}^{2}},

\displaystyle G^{\bar{V}V}_{\xi,\,\mu\nu}(p,-p)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p^{2}-M_{V}^{2}}\frac{(1-\xi_{V})p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{p^{2}-\xi_{V}M_{V}^{2}},

\displaystyle G^{\bar{G}G}_{\mathrm{tHF}}(p,-p)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p^{2}-M_{V}^{2}}

\displaystyle G^{\bar{G}G}_{\xi}(p,-p)

\displaystyle=\frac{-\mathrm{i}}{p^{2}-M_{V}^{2}}\frac{(1-\xi_{V})M_{V}^{2}}{p^{2}-\xi_{V}M_{V}^{2}}.

(108)

According to this, we decompose the partial matrix elements
from (
106
) into

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}=\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}+\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\xi)},\qquad\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij})}=\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}+\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij},\xi)}.

(109)

The second, gauge-parameter dependent terms do not vanish.
They do, however, cancel in the sum of the two objects
from (
109
).
This implies that the final results of our splitting functions are free
from gauge parameters.

For two transversely polarized external vector bosons with

\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
, this follows straightforwardly from

\displaystyle G^{\bar{V}V}_{\xi,\,\mu\nu}(p,-p)=-\frac{p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{M_{V}^{2}}G^{\bar{G}G}_{\xi}(p,-p)

(110)

and the application of the
WI
(
46
)
to both
T^{(n)}_{V,\mu}
and the splitting vertex from the first
diagram in (
104
).
If at least one of the external vector bosons is longitudinal, the
cancellation of gauge parameters is more intricate.
This is because the partial matrix elements in
(
101
)
and (
103
) have
unphysical
external
would-be Goldstone bosons and R-polarized gauge bosons.
The corresponding splitting vertices do not fulfill the
requirements of the
WI
(
46
).
However, the gauge-parameter dependent parts cancel within the sum of

all
unphysical partial amplitudes
from (
101
)
and (
103
), due to the

WIs
(
46
), (
47
),
and (
48
).
In our explicit calculations, this cancellation is made possible by the
fact that these
WIs
are comprised by the coupling relations from
Section
2.6
.
This provides self-consistency checks for the implementation of these
relations and the decompositions
(
100
),
(
102
), in particular.

Naively, the presence of masses in the denominators
of (
106
) suggests bad behavior in the various massless
limits, despite the treatment of longitudinal polarization vectors.
These factors, however, stem either from the application of the

WI
(
105
)
or the insertion of the coupling
relations (
53
)
and (
54
).
In both cases, the gauge structure of the considered theory implies that
the
various massless limits
m\to 0
are well defined, as discussed in
Sections
2.6
and
2.7
.
We provide further comments on this when presenting our final results
for
V^{\ast}\to VV
splitting functions.

The following computations are supported by
Appendix
C.1
, where our alternative strategy for
the treatment of mass-singular factors in longitudinal polarization
vectors is discussed.
We find full agreement between the results from the two different methods.

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{T}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
.

The TT configuration is free of problems regarding longitudinal
polarization vectors, allowing for a straightforward evaluation of the
quasi-collinear limit.
As mentioned, the fact that the
sum of the

\xi
-dependent parts from the
decompositions (
109
) vanishes in
our explicit computation,

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\xi)}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}}+\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij},\xi)}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}}=0,

(111)

provides an important check.
For the
\xi
-independent contributions in ’t Hooft–Feynman gauge, we get

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}}+\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}}

\displaystyle=\frac{eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\bigg\{

\displaystyle 2\big(p_{i}\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})\big)\Big(\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{i})T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)-2\big(p_{j}\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{i})\big)\Big(\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle+\big(\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{i})\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})\big)\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\Big(p_{j}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle-\big(\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{i})\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})\big)\rho(m_{ij},m_{j},m_{i})\Big(p_{i}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)\bigg\},

(112)

where terms of higher orders
in
\lambda
have not been neglected yet and
we defined the shorthand

\displaystyle\rho(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=\frac{m_{1}^{2}+m_{2}^{2}-m_{3}^{2}}{m_{1}^{2}},

(113)

which fulfills the relations

\displaystyle m_{1}^{2}\,\rho(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=m_{2}^{2}\,\rho(m_{2},m_{1},m_{3}),

\displaystyle\rho(m_{1},m_{3},m_{2})=2-\rho(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3}).

(114)

The form of
\rho
is linked to the factors in the coupling
relations (
53
)
and (
54
).
In particular, the discussion at the end of Section
2.6

implies that

\displaystyle I_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}^{V_{a}}m_{1}\rho(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3}),\qquad I_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}^{V_{a}}\frac{m_{1}}{m_{2}}\rho(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})

(115)

are well behaved in the limits
m_{1}\to 0
and
m_{2}\to 0
, by virtue of
the gauge symmetry of the theory.
Here,
m_{1},m_{2},m_{3}
represent any permutation
of
M_{V_{a}},M_{V_{b}},M_{V_{c}}
.

We proceed by multiplying (
112
) with its
conjugate, with

V_{ij}
and
G_{ij}

replaced by

V_{ij^{\prime}}
and
G_{ij^{\prime}}
, and insert
the transverse polarization sum (
77
) for

\varepsilon^{\mu}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{i})
and
\varepsilon^{\mu}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})
,
with separately defined

r^{\mu}_{i}
and
r^{\mu}_{j}
.
13
13

13

The factors
\propto p^{\mu}/M_{V}
in (
77
)
do not pose a problem for the naive factorization procedure.
While this is not straightforward to see at the level of squared amplitudes,
it can be inferred by considering individual
amplitudes, as transverse polarization vectors are free of spurious
factors
\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{-1}\right)
.

This is followed by applying the Sudakov
parametrization (
69
) and inserting explicit
expressions for known scalar products, such as on-shell relations and
transversality conditions.
Finally, in order to identify contributions beyond the leading order
in
\lambda
, we evaluate the mass order of all remaining scalar
products, entirely using

\displaystyle k_{\perp}^{\mu}=\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right),\quad p_{i}p_{j}=\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right),\quad\bar{p}^{\mu}\,T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X,\mu}(p_{ij})=\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right),\quad r^{\mu}_{i/j}=\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right).

(116)

In particular, no other assumptions on the scaling of scalar products
involving the gauge vectors
r^{\mu}
and thereby on their precise
definition are made.
Finally, we employ

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=p_{i}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}(p_{i})=m_{i}+\big(z\bar{p}-A_{i}n+k_{\perp}\big)r_{i}=m_{i}+z\,\big(\bar{p}r_{i}\big)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right),

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=p_{j}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}(p_{j})=m_{j}+\big((1-z)\bar{p}-A_{j}n-k_{\perp}\big)r_{j}=m_{j}+(1-z)\,\big(\bar{p}r_{j}\big)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right),

(117)

with
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
defined in (
69
),
to replace
\bar{p}r_{i}
and
\bar{p}r_{j}
at leading mass order.
We arrive at the transverse splitting function

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}={}

\displaystyle I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\bigg\{2\bigg(\frac{1}{(z-1)z}+2\bigg)\bigg(2z-\rho(m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i},m_{j})\bigg)\bar{p}^{\nu}k_{\perp}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\quad{}+2\bigg(\frac{1}{(z-1)z}+2\bigg)\bigg(2z-\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\bigg)\bar{p}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\quad{}+2\bigg(2z-\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\bigg)\bigg(2z-\rho(m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i},m_{j})\bigg)\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\quad{}+8k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}+4\bigg(\frac{1}{z^{2}}+\frac{1}{(z-1)^{2}}\bigg)k_{\perp}^{2}g^{\mu\nu}\bigg\}.

(118)

The assignment of the Lorentz indices is clarified
by (
98
).
This result is well behaved for
m_{ij}\to 0

and
m_{ij}^{\prime}\to 0
, due to the properties of
\rho
discussed
with (
115
) and the

WI
(
105
).
The disappearance of

r^{\mu}_{i}
and
r^{\mu}_{j}
from this result, together with
the fact that no constraints on these gauge vectors were placed, implies
independence from the frames in which the transverse polarization
vectors are defined.

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{L}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
.

Next, we turn to the LT case.
Again, the gauge-parameter-dependent parts cancel,

\displaystyle 0=\eta_{V_{i}}^{\ast}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\xi)}\Big]_{G_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{T}}+\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\xi)}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{T}}+(V_{ij}\rightarrow G_{ij}).

(119)

For the tHF contributions to (
101
), we get

\displaystyle\eta_{V_{i}}^{\ast}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{G_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{T}}+(V_{ij}\rightarrow G_{ij})

\displaystyle=\frac{eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\Bigg\{

\displaystyle\frac{m_{j}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}{m_{i}}\Big(\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle{}+\frac{\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})}{m_{i}}\big(p_{i}\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})\big)\Big(p_{i}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})+p_{j}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)\Bigg\},

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{T}}+(V_{ij}\rightarrow G_{ij})

\displaystyle=\frac{eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\Bigg\{

\displaystyle\Big(m_{i}-2\big(p_{j}r_{i}\big)\Big)\Big(\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)+2\big(p_{i}\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})\big)\Big(r_{i}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle{}-\rho(m_{ij},m_{j},m_{i})\Big(r_{i}\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})\Big)\Big(p_{i}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle{}+\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\Big(r_{i}\varepsilon^{\ast}_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})\Big)\Big(p_{j}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)\Bigg\}.

(120)

As before, terms of higher order in
\lambda
have not been neglected at
this point.
The convention-dependent phase factors
\eta_{V}
have dropped out.
Summing these two contributions and proceeding as for the TT case by
forming the interference contributions with
their counterparts for intermediate fields
V_{ij^{\prime}}

and
G_{ij^{\prime}}
, employing the transverse polarization
sum for
V_{j}
, and dropping terms of subleading mass order
produces the LT splitting function

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}={}

\displaystyle I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\bigg\{\frac{\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})}{(z-1)z}\left(2-z\rho(m_{i},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{j})\right)\bar{p}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\quad{}+\frac{\rho(m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i},m_{j})}{(z-1)z}\left(2-z\rho(m_{i},m_{ij},m_{j})\right)\bar{p}^{\nu}k_{\perp}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\quad{}-k_{\perp}^{2}\frac{\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\rho(m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i},m_{j})}{(z-1)^{2}m_{i}^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\quad{}-\frac{m_{i}^{2}}{z^{2}}\left(2-z\rho(m_{i},m_{ij},m_{j})\right)\left(2-z\rho(m_{i},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{j})\right)g^{\mu\nu}\bigg\}.

(121)

Once more, the properties of
\rho
and (
105
)
imply that this result is well behaved for
m_{ij}\to 0
,

m_{ij}^{\prime}\to 0
, and
m_{i}\to 0
, which becomes apparent by
rewriting

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\frac{m_{i}}{m_{ij}}\rho(m_{i},m_{ij},m_{j})\bigg(\frac{2m_{i}}{(z-1)z}-\frac{m_{i}}{z-1}\rho(m_{i},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{j})\bigg)\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}}{m_{ij}}k_{\perp}^{\nu}

\displaystyle{}+\frac{m_{i}}{m_{ij}^{\prime}}\rho(m_{i},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{j})\bigg(\frac{2m_{i}}{(z-1)z}-\frac{m_{i}}{z-1}\rho(m_{i},m_{ij},m_{j})\bigg)\frac{\bar{p}^{\nu}}{m_{ij}^{\prime}}k_{\perp}^{\mu}

\displaystyle{}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{(z-1)^{2}}\bigg(\frac{m_{i}}{m_{ij}}\rho(m_{i},m_{ij},m_{j})\bigg)\bigg(\frac{m_{i}}{m_{ij}^{\prime}}\rho(m_{i},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{j})\bigg)\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}}{m_{ij}}\frac{\bar{p}^{\nu}}{m_{ij}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle{}-\bigg(\frac{2m_{i}}{z}-m_{i}\rho(m_{i},m_{ij},m_{j})\bigg)\bigg(\frac{2m_{i}}{z}-m_{i}\rho(m_{i},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{j})\bigg)g^{\mu\nu}\bigg\}.

(122)

In particular, consider the limit
m_{i}\to 0
.
As discussed at the end of Section
2.7
, the longitudinal
polarization state of the vector boson
V_{i}
ceases to exist and is
replaced by the associated scalar
G_{i}
, which becomes physical.
Accordingly, we relabel
G_{i}\to H_{i}
.
With

\displaystyle\frac{m_{i}}{m_{ij}}\rho(m_{i},m_{ij},m_{j})I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}=-2\eta_{\bar{V}_{i}}\eta_{V_{ij}}I_{\bar{G}_{ij}\bar{G}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}}

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow[m_{i}\to 0]{G_{i}\to H_{i}}\;2\eta_{\bar{V}_{i}}\eta_{\bar{V}_{j}}\frac{m_{j}}{m_{ij}}I_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij}},

\displaystyle m_{i}\rho(m_{i},m_{ij},m_{j})I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}=m_{i}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}+\eta_{\bar{V}_{i}}C_{\bar{G}_{i}\bar{V}_{j}V_{ij}}

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow[m_{i}\to 0]{G_{i}\to H_{i}}\;2\eta_{\bar{V}_{i}}\eta_{\bar{V}_{j}}m_{j}I_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij}},

(123)

we see that the splitting function (
121
) does not
necessarily vanish for
m_{i}\to 0
, but that it instead reproduces the
splitting
function (
164
) for
V^{\ast}\to HV
,

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}\xrightarrow[m_{i}\to 0]{G_{i}\to H_{i}}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{i}=0},

(124)

which is given in Section
4.2.5
below.

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{T}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{L}
.

To obtain the TL result, we proceed in the same way.
Alternatively, it can be obtained by application of the
symmetry (
88
),

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{j}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{i}}\big(1-z,-k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(1-z,-k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j}},

(125)

where the last line is due to

I_{V_{j}\bar{V}_{i}}^{V_{ij\smash{{}^{(\prime)}}}}=-I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij\smash{{}^{(\prime)}}}}
.

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{L}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{L}
.

Once more, the gauge-dependent contributions
drop out in the combination of
all contributions from (
103
),

\displaystyle 0={}

\displaystyle\eta_{V_{i}}^{\ast}\eta_{V_{j}}^{\ast}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\xi)}\Big]_{G_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{G}}+\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\xi)}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{R}}

\displaystyle{}+\eta_{V_{j}}^{\ast}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\xi)}\Big]_{V_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{G}}+\eta_{V_{i}}^{\ast}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\xi)}\Big]_{G_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{R}}+(V_{ij}\rightarrow G_{ij}).

(126)

For the gauge-parameter-independent tHF parts, we get

\displaystyle\eta_{V_{i}}^{\ast}\eta_{V_{j}}^{\ast}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{G_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{G}}+\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{R}}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}+\eta_{V_{j}}^{\ast}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{V_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{G}}+\eta_{V_{i}}^{\ast}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{G_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{R}}+(V_{ij}\rightarrow G_{ij})

\displaystyle=\frac{eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\Big(m_{i}\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})-2\big(p_{j}r_{i}\big)\Big)\Big(r_{j}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle{}-\Big(m_{j}\rho(m_{j},m_{i},m_{ij})-2\big(p_{i}r_{j}\big)\Big)\Big(r_{i}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle{}+\bigg[

\displaystyle\rho(m_{ij},m_{j},m_{i})\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{m_{i}}{m_{j}}\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})-\frac{p_{j}r_{i}}{m_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle{}+\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\left(r_{i}r_{j}+\frac{p_{i}r_{j}}{m_{i}}\right)\bigg]\Big(p_{j}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle{}-\bigg[

\displaystyle\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{m_{j}}{m_{i}}\rho(m_{j},m_{i},m_{ij})-\frac{p_{i}r_{j}}{m_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle{}+\rho(m_{ij},m_{j},m_{i})\left(r_{i}r_{j}+\frac{p_{j}r_{i}}{m_{j}}\right)\bigg]\Big(p_{i}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)\bigg\},

(127)

which does not depend on
\eta_{V}
.
Proceeding as before, with the exception that no transverse polarization
sums have to be employed, we obtain the LL splitting function

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}={}

\displaystyle I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\bigg\{\frac{m_{i}\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})}{m_{j}}k_{\perp}^{\mu}+\frac{\chi(z,m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})}{2(z-1)zm_{i}m_{j}m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\cdot\bigg\{\frac{m_{i}\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime})}{m_{j}}k_{\perp}^{\nu}+\frac{\chi(z,m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime})}{2(z-1)zm_{i}m_{j}m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}\bar{p}^{\nu}\bigg\},

(128)

with

\displaystyle\chi(z,m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=m_{3}^{2}\big[

\displaystyle(z-1)zm_{1}^{2}\rho(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\big(2z-\rho(m_{3},m_{1},m_{2})\big)

\displaystyle-2(z-1)m_{1}^{2}\rho(m_{3},m_{2},m_{1})-2zm_{2}^{2}\rho(m_{3},m_{1},m_{2})\big],

(129)

fulfilling the symmetry relations

\displaystyle\chi(z,m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})

\displaystyle=-\chi(1-z,m_{2},m_{1},m_{3}),

\displaystyle\chi(z,m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})

\displaystyle=z^{3}\chi(1/z,m_{3},m_{2},m_{1}).

(130)

Similar to the previous results, (
128
) is well
behaved in all massless limits.
For the second terms in curly brackets, this can easily be seen by writing

\displaystyle\frac{\chi(z,m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})}{m_{i}m_{j}m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}=\bigg[

\displaystyle(z-1)z\frac{m_{i}}{m_{j}}\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})\big(2zm_{ij}-m_{ij}\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\big)

\displaystyle-2(z-1)m_{i}\frac{m_{ij}}{m_{j}}\rho(m_{ij},m_{j},m_{i})-2zm_{j}\frac{m_{ij}}{m_{i}}\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\bigg]\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}}{m_{ij}},

(131)

recalling (
105
), and
identifying the factors from (
115
).
Focusing on the limits
m_{i}\to 0
and
m_{j}\to 0
,
in analogy to (
124
),
we find

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[m_{i}\to 0]{G_{i}\to H_{i}}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{i}=0},

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[m_{j}\to 0]{G_{j}\to H_{j}}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{H_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{j}=0},

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[m_{i/j}\to 0]{G_{i/j}\to H_{i/j}}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{H_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{i}=m_{j}=0},

(132)

where the splitting functions on the r.h.s. can be found in
Sections
4.2.5
and
4.2.8
below.

Unpolarized case.

Combining the above results, we obtain the unpolarized
V^{\ast}\to VV

splitting function.
Denoting the summation over all polarization configurations
with
\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{U}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{U},\mathrm{U}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\rho(m_{j},m_{i},m_{ij}^{\prime})\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})+8\big)\big(2z-\rho(m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i},m_{j})\big)\bar{p}^{\nu}k_{\perp}^{\mu}

\displaystyle{}+\frac{1}{2}\big(\rho(m_{j},m_{i},m_{ij})\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime})+8\big)\big(2z-\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\big)\bar{p}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}

\displaystyle{}+\bigg[

\displaystyle-k_{\perp}^{2}\left(\frac{\rho(m_{ij},m_{j},m_{i})\rho(m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{j},m_{i})}{z^{2}m_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\rho(m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i},m_{j})}{(z-1)^{2}m_{i}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle{}+\frac{\chi(z,m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})\chi(z,m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime})}{4(z-1)^{2}z^{2}m_{i}^{2}m_{ij}^{2}m_{j}^{2}m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}

\displaystyle{}+2\big(2z-\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\big)\big(2z-\rho(m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i},m_{j})\big)\bigg]\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}

\displaystyle{}+\big(\rho(m_{j},m_{i},m_{ij})\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime})+8\big)k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}

\displaystyle{}+\bigg[

\displaystyle{}-\bigg(\frac{2}{z}-\rho(m_{i},m_{ij},m_{j})\bigg)\bigg(\frac{2}{z}-\rho(m_{i},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{j})\bigg)m_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle{}-\bigg(\frac{2}{1-z}-\rho(m_{j},m_{ij},m_{i})\bigg)\bigg(\frac{2}{1-z}-\rho(m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i})\bigg)m_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle{}+4\bigg(\frac{1}{z^{2}}+\frac{1}{(z-1)^{2}}\bigg)k_{\perp}^{2}\bigg]g^{\mu\nu}\bigg\}.

(133)

4.2.2
\boldsymbol{H^{\ast}\to VV}

splitting functions

For the computation of
H^{\ast}\to VV
splitting functions
we require the diagrams

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(H_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle{}={}

\lxSVG@closescope {

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-11.83986pt}{-2.16722pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

;{

\lxSVG@closescope {
{}{}{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{48.36969pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
;
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

(134)

which translate, using the coupling relations (
55
)
and (
49
), into

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(H_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}={}

\displaystyle-2\mathrm{i}eI_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}^{\ast}\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}T_{H_{ij}X}^{(n)}\left(p_{ij}\right)\varepsilon_{\lambda_{i}}^{\alpha\ast}(p_{i})\varepsilon_{\lambda_{j}\alpha}^{\ast}(p_{j}),

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(H_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\lambda_{j}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eI_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\frac{m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}^{\ast}}{m_{i}\eta_{V_{i}}^{\ast}}\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}T_{H_{ij}X}^{(n)}\left(p_{ij}\right)\big(2p_{i}+p_{j}\big)_{\alpha}\varepsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\alpha\ast}(p_{j}),

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(H_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}G_{j}X}^{\lambda_{i}\mathrm{G}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eI_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}T_{H_{ij}X}^{(n)}\left(p_{ij}\right)\big(p_{i}+2p_{j}\big)_{\alpha}\varepsilon_{\lambda_{i}}^{\alpha\ast}(p_{i}),

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(H_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{G}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eI_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\frac{m_{ij}^{2}}{m_{i}}\eta_{V_{i}}\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}T_{H_{ij}X}^{(n)}\left(p_{ij}\right).

(135)

None of these functions involves
gauge parameters
\xi_{V}
.

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{T}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
.

The TT splitting function

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}\big)=8m_{j}^{2}I_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}=8m_{i}^{2}I_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}\ast}

(136)

is symmetric under the substitution of
i\leftrightarrow j
, see (
87
).
This is due to the coupling relation (
50
), which was used in the
second equality.
In the
EWSM
, we have
H_{ij}=H_{ij^{\prime}}=H

and
m_{ij}=m_{ij}^{\prime}
, as well as
m_{i}=m_{j}
.
Inserting (
23
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H}_{V_{i}}{}^{H}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg|_{\mathrm{EWSM}}=\frac{8m_{i}^{4}}{(ev)^{2}}\delta_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}.

(137)

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{L}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
.

The LT splitting function

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{T}\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{4k_{\perp}^{2}m_{j}^{2}}{m_{i}^{2}(z-1)^{2}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}=-\frac{4k_{\perp}^{2}}{(z-1)^{2}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}\ast}.

(138)

is well behaved in the limit of a massless vector
boson
V_{i}
, and its relation to the
H^{\ast}\to HV

splitting function (
171
) is obvious.
The
EWSM
result reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H}_{V_{i}}{}^{H}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg|_{\mathrm{EWSM}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4k_{\perp}^{2}m_{i}^{2}}{(ev)^{2}(z-1)^{2}}\delta_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}.

(139)

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{T}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{L}
.

As for the symmetry of the TT splitting function, the relation between the TL result

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{L}\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{4k_{\perp}^{2}}{z^{2}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}=-\frac{4k_{\perp}^{2}m_{i}^{2}}{m_{j}^{2}z^{2}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}\ast}.

(140)

and the LT counterpart (
138
)
via application of (
88
),
which expresses the substitution
i\leftrightarrow j
,
requires the coupling relation (
50
).
The
EWSM
result reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H}_{V_{i}}{}^{H}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg|_{\mathrm{EWSM}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4k_{\perp}^{2}m_{i}^{2}}{(ev)^{2}z^{2}}\delta_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}.

(141)

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{L}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{L}
.

In the LL result

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{L}\big)={}

\displaystyle m_{j}^{2}I_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}\bigg(\frac{m_{i}\bar{\rho}(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})}{m_{j}}-\frac{2m_{i}}{zm_{j}}+\frac{2m_{j}}{(z-1)m_{i}}\bigg)

\displaystyle{}\cdot\bigg(\frac{m_{i}\bar{\rho}(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime})}{m_{j}}-\frac{2m_{i}}{zm_{j}}+\frac{2m_{j}}{(z-1)m_{i}}\bigg),

(142)

we introduce the auxiliary function

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\equiv\frac{m_{1}^{2}+m_{2}^{2}+m_{3}^{2}}{m_{1}^{2}}.

(143)

Again, the symmetry of the result under
substitution (
87
) follows
from the coupling relation (
50
).
The latter also implies that (
142
) is well
behaved in the individual limits
m_{i}\to 0
and
m_{j}\to 0
,
which follows from arguments similar to the ones given
for the factors (
115
).
Replacing
G_{i}\to H_{i}
and
G_{j}\to H_{j}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{L}\big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[m_{i}\to 0]{G_{i}\to H_{i}}\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg|_{m_{i}=0},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{L}\big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[m_{j}\to 0]{G_{j}\to H_{j}}\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{H_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg|_{m_{j}=0}.

(144)

In the simultaneous limit
m_{i},m_{j}\to 0
, (
142
)
remains finite due to coupling
relation (
55
) and reproduces the
splitting function for
H^{\ast}\to HH
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{L}\big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[m_{i/j}\to 0]{G_{i/j}\to H_{i/j}}\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{H_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu}\big)\bigg|_{m_{i}=m_{j}=0}.

(145)

The
EWSM
result reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H}_{V_{i}}{}^{H}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg|_{\mathrm{EWSM}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}}{(ev)^{2}}\bigg(2\bigg(1+\frac{1}{(z-1)z}\bigg)m_{i}^{2}+m_{ij}^{2}\bigg)^{2}.

(146)

Unpolarized case.

The above results combine into the unpolarized splitting function

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{U},\mathrm{U}\big)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle m_{j}^{2}I_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}\bigg\{\frac{4(m_{i}^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2})}{z^{2}m_{j}^{2}}+\frac{4(m_{j}^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2})}{(z-1)^{2}m_{i}^{2}}+\bar{\rho}(m_{j},m_{i},m_{ij})\bar{\rho}(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime})+8

\displaystyle{}-\frac{2m_{i}^{2}\big(\rho(m_{i},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{j})+\rho(m_{i},m_{ij},m_{j})\big)}{zm_{j}^{2}}+\frac{2m_{j}^{2}\big(\rho(m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i})+\rho(m_{j},m_{ij},m_{i})\big)}{(z-1)m_{i}^{2}}\bigg\},

(147)

which in the
EWSM
reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H}_{V_{i}}{}^{H}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{U},\mathrm{U}\big)\bigg|_{\mathrm{EWSM}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}}{(ev)^{2}}\bigg\{4m_{i}^{2}(m_{i}^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2})\left(\frac{1}{z^{2}}+\frac{1}{(z-1)^{2}}\right)+\frac{4m_{i}^{2}m_{ij}^{2}}{(z-1)z}+12m_{i}^{4}+4m_{i}^{2}m_{ij}^{2}+m_{ij}^{4}\bigg\}.

(148)

4.2.3
\boldsymbol{V^{\ast}/H^{\ast}\to VV}

splitting functions

The functions for
V^{\ast}/H^{\ast}\to VV
splittings resulting from
interferences
are obtained using
the ingredients from the previous sections.
Regarding the massless limits of the following result, compare to
Sections
4.2.1
and
4.2.2
.

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{T}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
.

The symmetry of the TT splitting function

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}

\displaystyle=-4m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}\bigg\{\bigg(2+\frac{1}{(z-1)z}\bigg)k_{\perp}^{\mu}-\big(\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})-2z\big)\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg\}

(149)

under (
87
) is somewhat hidden and
can be made explicit as described in
Section
4.2.2
.
In the
EWSM
, where
m_{i}=m_{j}
and
m_{ij}=M_{\mathrm{H}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{\vphantom{ij}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}_{\mathrm{EWSM}}=4m_{i}^{2}\frac{I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{ev}\delta_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}\bigg\{\bigg(2+\frac{1}{(z-1)z}\bigg)k_{\perp}^{\mu}-(1-2z)\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg\}.

(150)

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{L}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
.

The LT result reads

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}

\displaystyle=2m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})}{z-1}-\frac{2}{z}\bigg)k_{\perp}^{\mu}-\bigg(\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})}{(z-1)^{2}m_{i}^{2}}\bigg)\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg\}

(151)

and takes the following form in the
EWSM
,

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{\phantom{ij}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}_{\mathrm{EWSM}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{z-1}\frac{I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{ev}\delta_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{2m_{i}^{2}}{z}-m_{ij}^{2}\bigg)k_{\perp}^{\mu}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{z-1}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg\}.

(152)

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{T}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{L}
.

The connection between the TL result

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}

\displaystyle=-2m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{\rho(m_{j},m_{i},m_{ij})}{z}-\frac{2}{z-1}\bigg)k_{\perp}^{\mu}-\bigg(\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}\rho(m_{ij},m_{j},m_{i})}{z^{2}m_{j}^{2}}\bigg)\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg\}.

(153)

and the LT result (
151
)
via (
88
) is elucidated as described in
Section
4.2.2
.
The
EWSM
result reads

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{\vphantom{ij}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}_{\mathrm{EWSM}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{z}\frac{I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{ev}\delta_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{2m_{i}^{2}}{z-1}+m_{ij}^{2}\bigg)k_{\perp}^{\mu}+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{z}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg\}.

(154)

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{L}
,
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{L}
.

We get the LL splitting function

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}{}={}

\displaystyle-m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}\bigg(\frac{m_{i}\bar{\rho}(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime})}{m_{j}}-\frac{2m_{i}}{zm_{j}}+\frac{2m_{j}}{(z-1)m_{i}}\bigg)

\displaystyle{}\cdot\bigg(\frac{m_{i}\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})}{m_{j}}k_{\perp}^{\mu}+\frac{\chi(z,m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})}{2(z-1)zm_{i}m_{j}m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg),

(155)

with the
EWSM
result

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{\vphantom{ij}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}_{\mathrm{EWSM}}={}

\displaystyle\frac{I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{ev}\delta_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}\bigg(2\bigg(1+\frac{1}{(z-1)z}\bigg)m_{i}^{2}+m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}\bigg)

\displaystyle{}\cdot\bigg\{\rho(m_{i},m_{i},m_{ij})k_{\perp}^{\mu}+\frac{\chi(z,m_{i},m_{i},m_{ij})}{2(z-1)zm_{i}^{2}m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg\}.

(156)

Unpolarized case.

The combination
of (
151
)–(
155
) leads to
the unpolarized splitting function

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{U},\mathrm{U}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{G}_{j}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}\bigg\{\bigg[\frac{\chi(z,m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})}{2(1-z)zm_{i}m_{ij}^{2}m_{j}}\bigg(\frac{m_{i}\bar{\rho}(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime})}{m_{j}}-\frac{2m_{i}}{zm_{j}}+\frac{2m_{j}}{(z-1)m_{i}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad{}+\frac{2k_{\perp}^{2}\rho(m_{ij},m_{j},m_{i})}{z^{2}m_{j}^{2}}-\frac{2k_{\perp}^{2}\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})}{(z-1)^{2}m_{i}^{2}}+4\big(\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})-2z\big)\bigg]\bar{p}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\quad{}-\big(\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})\bar{\rho}(m_{j},m_{i},m_{ij}^{\prime})+8\big)k_{\perp}^{\mu}\bigg\},

(157)

with
EWSM
form

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{\vphantom{ij}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{U},\mathrm{U}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}_{\mathrm{EWSM}}

\displaystyle=\frac{I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{ev}\delta_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\bigg(2\frac{m_{i}^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2}}{(z-1)^{2}z^{2}}+\frac{m_{ij}^{2}+m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}{(z-1)z}\bigg)(1-2z)\bar{p}^{\mu}

\displaystyle+m_{i}^{2}\big(\rho(m_{i},m_{i},m_{ij})\bar{\rho}(m_{i},m_{i},m_{ij}^{\prime})+8\big)\bigg(k_{\perp}^{\mu}-\frac{(1-2z)}{2}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg)\bigg\}.

(158)

4.2.4
\boldsymbol{H^{\ast}/V^{\ast}\to VV}

splitting functions

The splitting functions for
H^{\ast}/V^{\ast}\to VV
are related to those
for
V^{\ast}/H^{\ast}\to VV
via application
of (
92
),
where
\varphi_{ij}\leftrightarrow\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}

corresponds to

V_{ij}\leftrightarrow H_{ij^{\prime}}
.
We get

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\lambda_{i},\lambda_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}=\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\lambda_{i},\lambda_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\ast}_{m_{ij}\leftrightarrow m_{ij}^{\prime}}.

(159)

4.2.5
\boldsymbol{V^{\ast}\to HV}

splitting functions

For
V^{\ast}\to HV
splittings, the issue regarding longitudinal
polarization of the
FS
vector boson is treated as discussed above.
In analogy to (
100
), we write

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\mathrm{L}}_{H_{i}V_{j}X}=\left[T^{(n+1)}_{H_{i}V_{j}X}\right]_{\mu_{j}}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}^{\mu_{j}\ast}(p_{j})=\eta_{V_{j}}\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\mathrm{G}}_{H_{i}G_{j}X}+\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)\,\mathrm{R}}_{H_{i}V_{j}X}

(160)

and select the diagrams of type I separately for the two
terms on the r.h.s., amounting to

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}V_{j}X}^{\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle{}={}

(161)

After insertion of the coupling
relations (
49
)
and (
55
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}V_{j}X}^{\lambda_{j}}={}

\displaystyle-2\mathrm{i}eI_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}^{\ast}T^{(n)\,\sigma}_{V_{ij}X}\left(p_{ij}\right)G^{\bar{V}_{ij}V_{ij}}_{\sigma\gamma}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\varepsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\gamma\ast}(p_{j}),

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}V_{j}X}^{\lambda_{j}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eI_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\frac{m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}^{\ast}}{m_{ij}^{2}}G^{\bar{G}_{ij}G_{ij}}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})T_{V_{ij}X}^{(n)\,\alpha}\left(p_{ij}\right)p_{ij,\alpha}\varepsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\gamma\ast}(p_{j})\left(2p_{i}+p_{j}\right)_{\gamma},

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eI_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}T^{(n)\,\sigma}_{V_{ij}X}\left(p_{ij}\right)G^{\bar{V}_{ij}V_{ij}}_{\sigma\alpha}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\left(p_{j}-p_{i}\right)_{\alpha},

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}}={}

\displaystyle+\mathrm{i}eI_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\frac{m_{i}^{2}}{m_{ij}^{2}}G^{\bar{G}_{ij}G_{ij}}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})T_{V_{ij}X}^{(n)\,\alpha}\left(p_{ij}\right)p_{ij,\alpha}

(162)

and arrive at the splitting functions quoted below.
The results for
V^{\ast}\to VH
splittings can be computed similarly,
or alternatively obtained via (
88
), i.e.

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{H_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\lambda\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}=\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{j}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{i}}\big(1-z,-k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\lambda\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j}}.

(163)

Polarization
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
.

The transverse-polarization result

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-4m_{j}^{2}I_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\bigg\{g^{\mu\nu}+\frac{\bar{p}^{\nu}k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(z-1)m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}+\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}}{(z-1)m_{ij}^{2}}+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{(z-1)^{2}m_{ij}^{2}m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}\bigg\}

(164)

has well-defined limits
m_{ij}\to 0
and
m_{ij}\to 0
due
to
WI
(
105
) and coupling
relation (
50
).
In the
EWSM
, we have
m_{ij}=m_{ij}^{\prime}=m_{j}
and get the result

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{\mathrm{EWSM}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4}{(ev)^{2}}\delta_{V_{ij}V_{j}}\delta_{V_{ij^{\prime}}V_{j}}\bigg\{m_{j}^{4}g^{\mu\nu}+\frac{m_{j}^{2}}{z-1}\big(\bar{p}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}+\bar{p}^{\nu}k_{\perp}^{\mu}\big)+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{(z-1)^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}\bigg\}.

(165)

Polarization
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{L}
.

The result for longitudinal polarization reads

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle I_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\bigg(\rho(m_{ij},m_{j},m_{i})+\frac{2m_{j}^{2}}{(z-1)m_{ij}^{2}}+2(z-1)\bigg)\bar{p}^{\mu}+2k_{\perp}^{\mu}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\bigg\{\bigg(\rho(m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{j},m_{i})+\frac{2m_{j}^{2}}{(z-1)m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}+2(z-1)\bigg)\bar{p}^{\nu}+2k_{\perp}^{\nu}\bigg\}.

(166)

The limits
m_{ij}\to 0
and
m_{ij}\to 0
behave as described as
for
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
.
In the limit
m_{j}\to 0
, this result reproduces the
V^{\ast}\to HH

splitting function when taking
G_{j}\to H_{j}
,

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}\xrightarrow[m_{j}\to 0]{G_{j}\to H_{j}}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{H_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{j}=0}.

(167)

The
EWSM
result reads

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{\mathrm{EWSM}}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(ev)^{2}}\delta_{V_{ij}V_{j}}\delta_{V_{ij^{\prime}}V_{j}}\bigg\{2m_{j}k_{\perp}^{\mu}+\bigg(2\bigg(z+\frac{1}{z-1}\bigg)m_{j}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}\bigg)\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}}{m_{j}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\cdot\bigg\{2m_{j}k_{\perp}^{\mu}+\bigg(2\bigg(z+\frac{1}{z-1}\bigg)m_{j}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}\bigg)\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}}{m_{j}}\bigg\}.

(168)

4.2.6
\boldsymbol{H^{\ast}\to HV}

splitting functions

To compute the splitting functions
H^{\ast}\to HV
, we need the diagrams

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(H_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}V_{j}X}^{\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle{}={}
\displaystyle\hbox to104.39pt{\vbox to56.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 17.27165pt\lower-28.57526pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
