\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

(169)

translating into the expressions

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(H_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}V_{j}X}^{\lambda_{j}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eI_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{H}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}}\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}T_{H_{ij}X}^{(n)}\left(p_{ij}\right)\varepsilon_{\lambda_{j}}^{\gamma\ast}(p_{j})\left(2p_{i}+p_{j}\right)_{\gamma},

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(H_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}G_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eI_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{H}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}}\frac{m_{ij}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}}{m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}^{\ast}}\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}T_{H_{ij}X}^{(n)}\left(p_{ij}\right),

(170)

where we used coupling relation (
58
).
In the
EWSM
, the couplings between two Higgs bosons and one vector boson vanish.
Therefore, the following splitting functions are absent in the
EWSM
.

Polarization
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
.

For a transversely polarized vector boson, we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T}\big)=-\frac{4k_{\perp}^{2}}{(z-1)^{2}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{H}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{H}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}\ast}.

(171)

Polarization
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{L}
.

In the longitudinal case, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L}\big)=m_{j}^{2}I_{\bar{H}_{ij}\bar{H}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{H}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}\ast}\bigg\{\rho(m_{j},m_{ij},m_{i})+\frac{2}{z-1}\bigg\}\bigg\{\rho(m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i})+\frac{2}{z-1}\bigg\}.

(172)

Taking
m_{j}\to 0
and
G_{j}\to H_{j}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L}\big)\xrightarrow[m_{j}\to 0]{G_{j}\to H_{j}}\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{H_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu}\big).

(173)

4.2.7
\boldsymbol{V^{\ast}/H^{\ast}\to HV}

splitting functions

The functions for the interference of the splittings

V^{\ast}/H^{\ast}\to HV
are computed with
the ingredients from Sections
4.2.5
and
4.2.6
.
They vanish in the
EWSM
, and
the various massless limits behave as discussed above.
The case
V^{\ast}/H^{\ast}\to VH
is obtained via application
of the
i\leftrightarrow j
symmetry relation (
88
)

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{H_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}=\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{j}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{i}}\big(1-z,-k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}_{m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j},\eta_{i}\leftrightarrow\eta_{j}}.

(174)

Note that we do not only swap masses, but also phases
\eta
.

Polarization
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{T}
.

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}=-4m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}^{\ast}I_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{H}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}\ast}\bigg\{\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{(z-1)^{2}m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{z-1}k_{\perp}^{\mu}\bigg\}.

(175)

Polarization
\lambda_{j}=\mathrm{L}
.

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}={}

\displaystyle m_{j}\eta_{V_{j}}^{\ast}I_{H_{i}\bar{G}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{\bar{H}_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{H}_{i}}^{\bar{V}_{j}\ast}\,\bigg\{\rho(m_{j},m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i})+\frac{2}{z-1}\bigg\}

\displaystyle{}\cdot\bigg\{\bigg(\rho(m_{ij},m_{j},m_{i})+\frac{2m_{j}^{2}}{(z-1)m_{ij}^{2}}+2(z-1)\bigg)\bar{p}^{\mu}+2k_{\perp}^{\mu}\bigg\}.

(176)

splitting function

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}H_{j}X}

\displaystyle{}={}
p_{ij}
G_{ij}
p_{i}
H_{i}
p_{j}
H_{j}
T^{(n)}_{G_{ij}X}

(177)

translate, after inserting the coupling
relation (
58
), into the expressions

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}H_{j}X}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eI_{H_{i}\bar{H}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}T^{(n)\,\sigma}_{V_{ij}X}\left(p_{ij}\right)G^{\bar{V}_{ij}V_{ij}}_{\sigma\alpha}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\left(p_{j}-p_{i}\right)^{\alpha},

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}H_{j}X}={}

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}eI_{H_{i}\bar{H}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\frac{m_{i}^{2}-m_{j}^{2}}{m_{ij}^{2}}T^{(n)\,\alpha}_{V_{ij}X}\left(p_{ij}\right)G^{\bar{G}_{ij}G_{ij}}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})p_{ij,\alpha}.

(178)

We obtain the
V\to HH
splitting function

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{H_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=I_{H_{i}\bar{H}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{H_{i}\bar{H}_{j}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\big\{\big(2z-\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\big)\bar{p}^{\mu}+2k_{\perp}^{\mu}\big\}\big\{\big(2z-\rho(m_{ij}^{\prime},m_{i},m_{j})\big)\bar{p}^{\nu}+2k_{\perp}^{\nu}\big\}.

(179)

The limits
m_{ij}\to 0
and
m_{ij}^{\prime}\to 0
are well-defined due
to
WI
(
105
)
and coupling relation (
58
).
Again, this splitting function does not exist in the
EWSM
.

4.2.9
\boldsymbol{H^{\ast}\to HH}

splitting function

The splitting function
H^{\ast}\to HH
is trivially obtained as

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{H_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu}\big)=C_{H_{ij}H_{i}H_{j}}C^{\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}H_{i}H_{j}}.

(180)

Note that dimensional analysis of the Lagrangian yields for the
pure-scalar coupling

\displaystyle C_{H_{ij}H_{i}H_{j}}=\mathcal{O}\left(m\right).

(181)

This implies that the above expression is of
\mathcal{O}\left(m^{2}\right)
, just like
all other splitting functions.
Inserting the
EWSM
triple-Higgs coupling (
365
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H}_{H}{}^{H}_{H}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu}\big)\bigg|_{\mathrm{EWSM}}=\frac{9m_{i}^{4}}{(ev)^{2}}.

(182)

4.2.10
\boldsymbol{V^{\ast}/H^{\ast}\to HH}

splitting function

Combining the results of Sections
4.2.8
and
4.2.9
, we get

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{H_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}=-I_{H_{i}\bar{H}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}C^{\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}H_{i}H_{j}}\big\{\big(2z-\rho(m_{ij},m_{i},m_{j})\big)\bar{p}^{\mu}+2k_{\perp}^{\mu}\big\}.

(183)

This splitting function again vanishes in the
EWSM
.

4.3
Fermionic FS splitting functions

Next, we switch to the derivation of
FS
splitting functions that involve
fermions
.
On top of the symmetries from Section
4.1
, these
results are subject to relations under charge conjugation,
which is discussed in some detail below.
Again, splitting functions that are not considered explicitly may be
obtained via symmetry relations and charge conjugation.

4.3.1
\boldsymbol{V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}}

splitting functions

The relevant diagrams for the computation of the
V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}

splitting functions read

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{f_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle{}={}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-11.50192pt}{-2.16722pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {

{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{48.36969pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
;
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

(184)

and correspond, upon insertion of (
60
), to
the expressions

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{f_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eT^{(n)\,\alpha}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})G^{\bar{V}_{ij}V_{ij}}_{\alpha\beta}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\bar{u}_{\kappa_{i}}(p_{i})\Big(\gamma^{\beta}\omega_{-}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}+\gamma^{\beta}\omega_{+}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\Big)v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j}),

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij})}\Big]_{f_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}={}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}eT^{(n)\,\alpha}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})p_{ij,\alpha}G^{\bar{G}_{ij}G_{ij}}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\,\frac{1}{m_{ij}^{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\bar{u}_{\kappa_{i}}(p_{i})\left\{\omega_{+}\left(m_{i}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}-m_{j}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}\right)+\omega_{-}\left(m_{i}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}-m_{j}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\right)\right\}v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j}).

(185)

The projectors
\omega_{\pm}
onto right- and left-handed chirality,
respectively, are defined in (
40
).
Combining the above equations leads us to

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{f_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}+\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{f_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{eT^{(n)\,\mu}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\bigg\{-\frac{m_{i}+m_{j}}{m_{ij}^{2}}p_{ij,\mu}\bar{u}_{\kappa_{i}}(p_{i})\gamma^{5}v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j})\Big(I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}-I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad{}+\frac{m_{i}-m_{j}}{m_{ij}^{2}}p_{ij,\mu}\bar{u}_{\kappa_{i}}(p_{i})v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j})\Big(I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}+I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad{}-\bar{u}_{\kappa_{i}}(p_{i})\gamma_{\mu}v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j})\Big(I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}+I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\Big)+\bar{u}_{\kappa_{i}}(p_{i})\gamma_{\mu}\gamma^{5}v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j})\Big(I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}-I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\Big)\bigg\}.

(186)

After squaring this expression, we plug in the spin-projected
completeness relations (
78
), keeping the
polarization-defining gauge vectors
r^{\mu}_{i}
and
r^{\mu}_{j}
separate.
We again stress that we are not restricted to helicity in some
specific frame, as one might intrinsically assume when
employing explicit parametrizations of spinors.
14
14

14

Recall that helicity is not a Lorentz-invariant property.

Specifically, given some reference frame, helicity is recovered with

r^{\mu}
that fulfills
\boldsymbol{r}\parallel\boldsymbol{p}
.
Despite the similarity of the vector
s^{\mu}

in (
79
) with the longitudinal polarization
vector (
61
), its mass-singular factor does not
require special care.
Indeed, it is already fully cancelled
in (
78
) due to
\not{p}^{2}=p^{2}=m_{f}^{2}
.

We are left with evaluating Dirac traces.
Apart from traces with even and odd numbers of slashed four-vectors,
which can be straightforwardly evaluated as chains of scalar products,
this also involves traces with
\gamma^{5}
.
As we work in
D=4
dimensions, there are no problems regarding the
definition of
\gamma^{5}
.
However, the evaluation of the latter type of traces requires the
contraction of four-vectors with the antisymmetric
tensor
\epsilon^{\mu\nu\sigma\rho}
.
For example, with the four-momenta
p_{1}^{\mu},p_{2}^{\mu},p_{3}^{\mu}
, and

p_{4}^{\mu}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\{\not{p}_{1}\not{p}_{2}\not{p}_{3}\not{p}_{4}\gamma^{5}\}=-4\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\sigma\rho}p_{1}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}p_{3}^{\sigma}p_{4}^{\rho}.

(187)

To facilitate the evaluation of the mass order of these expressions, we
decompose such contractions into individual scalar products, as
described in the following.
A similar procedure has been employed in
(
dittmaier2008polarized
)

for the evaluation of
IS

f\to f\gamma^{\ast}
splitting
functions.
We consistently use the convention
\epsilon^{0123}=1
.
First, we write

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\mu\nu\sigma\rho}p_{1}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}p_{3}^{\sigma}p_{4}^{\rho}=\epsilon^{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\rho^{\prime}}g_{\mu^{\prime}\mu}g_{\nu^{\prime}\nu}g_{\sigma^{\prime}\sigma}g_{\rho^{\prime}\rho}p_{1}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}p_{3}^{\sigma}p_{4}^{\rho}

(188)

and express the metric tensor as an outer product of four-vectors

\displaystyle g^{\mu^{\prime}\mu}=-\frac{\tilde{m}_{ij}^{2}n^{\mu^{\prime}}n^{\mu}}{(\bar{p}n)^{2}}+\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu^{\prime}}n^{\mu}+n^{\mu^{\prime}}\bar{p}^{\mu}}{\bar{p}n}-s_{\varepsilon}\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu^{\prime}}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu^{\prime}}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\big),

(189)

where
\bar{p}^{2}=\tilde{m}_{ij}^{2}
(see comment after (
84
)).
Here, we have taken
\bar{p}^{\mu}
and
n^{\mu}

from (
69
) and defined
polarization vectors

\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}^{\mu}=\varepsilon_{\lambda}^{\mu}(\bar{p})

with
\lambda=\pm

for the momentum
\bar{p}^{\mu}
,
fulfilling both

\displaystyle\bar{p}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}=0,\qquad\bar{\varepsilon}_{-\lambda}=s_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}^{\ast},\qquad\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-\lambda^{\prime}}=s_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda^{\prime}}^{\ast}=-s_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}\delta_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}},

(190)

and the gauge condition

\displaystyle n\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}=0.

(191)

We support arbitrary phases
s_{\varepsilon}
to account for different
conventions used in the literature.
15
15

15

For example,
Cuomo:2019siu
works with the
convention
s_{\varepsilon}=-1
, while
dittmaier2008polarized

implements
s_{\varepsilon}=+1
.

The polarization vectors
\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}^{\mu}

define transverse
helicity states
in
frames where
\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}\parallel\boldsymbol{n}
.
In any frame,
k_{\perp}^{\mu}
can be expressed as linear combination
of
\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}^{\mu}
,

\displaystyle k_{\perp}^{\mu}=-s_{\varepsilon}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}-s_{\varepsilon}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-})\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu},\qquad k_{\perp}^{2}=-2s_{\varepsilon}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}).

(192)

Inserting the decomposition (
189
)
into (
188
), only one non-zero contraction
of
\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
with four-vectors remains, which can
be expressed explicitly as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\bar{p}^{\mu}n^{\nu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\rho}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\sigma}=\mathrm{i}s_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}\bar{p}n.

(193)

This Lorentz-invariant expression can be derived from

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\bar{p}_{\mu}n_{\nu}=\mathrm{i}s_{\varepsilon}(\bar{p}n)\left(\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\rho}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\sigma}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\rho}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\sigma}\right),

(194)

which follows from an analogous manipulation as described in Footnote 6
of
dittmaier2008polarized

(although with massive momentum
\bar{p}^{\mu}
),
or more directly
in a frame where

\displaystyle n^{\mu}=\left(1,0,0,-1\right),\quad\bar{p}^{\mu}=\left(\sqrt{m_{ij}^{2}+|\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}|^{2}},0,0,|\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}|\right),\quad\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}^{\mu}=s_{\pm}\left(0,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}},\frac{\pm\mathrm{i}}{\sqrt{2}},0\right),

(195)

with
s_{+}s_{-}=s_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
.
Henceforth, the tensor
\epsilon^{\mu\nu\sigma\rho}
is completely
eliminated from the r.h.s. of (
188
).

Finally, we insert the Sudakov
parametrization (
69
) and neglect terms of higher
orders in the quasi-collinear rescaling parameter
\lambda
.
Once more, we only assume (
116
) in this procedure and
impose no further restrictions on the gauge vectors
r^{\mu}
.
We arrive at the splitting functions presented below.
As anticipated, these results are independent of the gauge vectors and
thereby of the precise definition of polarization.

It turns out that all types of
FS
splitting functions involving
two (anti-)fermions in the corresponding splitting
process from (
86
) are built from similar analytic structures.
Therefore, it is convenient to introduce appropriate abbreviations.
The third
bosonic field that participates in the splitting process besides the
two (anti-)fermions is denoted as
\varrho
.
Employing chirality labels
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{\mathrm{L},\mathrm{R}\}
and the notation (
41
)
for generic couplings
\mathcal{C}
, we define

\displaystyle a^{\varrho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=\zeta_{\mathcal{I}}m_{\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{1}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}\pm\zeta_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}m_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{2}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}},

\displaystyle b^{\varrho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=m_{\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{1}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}\pm m_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{2}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}},

\displaystyle c^{\varrho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}

\displaystyle=-\big(2\zeta_{\mathcal{X}}m_{\mathcal{X}}^{2}+\zeta_{\mathcal{I}}m_{\mathcal{I}}^{2}+\zeta_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}m_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}^{2}\big)\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{1}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}+m_{\mathcal{I}}m_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}\big(\zeta_{\mathcal{I}}+\zeta_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}\big)\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{2}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}},

\displaystyle d^{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime}}}\pm\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{R}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{R}\ast}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime}}},

\displaystyle e^{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{R}\ast}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime}}}\pm\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{R}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}^{\prime}}}.

(196)

These generic abbreviations are assigned to specific fermionic splitting
processes by choosing explicit indices
\mathcal{I},\bar{\mathcal{I}}\in\{i,j,ij^{(\prime)}\}
of the corresponding
outgoing
fermion and
antifermion, respectively, as well as the index
\mathcal{X}\in\{i,j,ij^{(\prime)}\}
of
\varrho
;
recall that the field labels on the symbols

\mathcal{C}^{\dots}_{\dots}
are considered
incoming
, whereas the
considered splitting process may involve
incoming
and
outgoing
particles.
In

V^{*}_{ij}\to f_{i}\bar{f}_{j}

splittings,
for example, the (anti-)fermions are both
outgoing
and correspond
to
\bar{\mathcal{I}}=j
and
\mathcal{I}=i
, while the vector boson is

incoming
,
\mathcal{X}=ij
.
Below, we explicitly discuss the abbreviations that arise for this situation.
In the case

f^{*}_{ij}\to V_{i}f_{j}
, on the other hand,
the fermion with the index
\bar{\mathcal{I}}=ij
is
incoming
and corresponds to an

outgoing
antifermion.
Similarly,
we write

\displaystyle\tilde{\varrho}_{ij^{(\prime)}}=\varrho_{ij^{(\prime)}},\qquad\tilde{\varrho}_{i}=\bar{\varrho}_{i},\qquad\tilde{\varrho}_{j}=\bar{\varrho}_{j},

(197)

because
\varrho_{ij^{(\prime)}}
is incoming w.r.t. the
considered splitting
process, while
\varrho_{i}
and
\varrho_{j}
are outgoing.
For the symbols
d^{\varrho}
and
e^{\varrho}
, we specify
i^{\prime}=i
,
j^{\prime}=j
.
The object
\zeta
is defined via

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}=1-z,\qquad\zeta_{j}=z,\qquad\zeta_{ij}=\zeta_{ij^{\prime}}=-(1-z)z.

(198)

For each possible assignment of
\mathcal{I}
,
\bar{\mathcal{I}}
,
and
\mathcal{X}
to the indices
i,j
, and
ij^{(\prime)}
, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathcal{X}}=-\frac{\zeta_{\mathcal{I}}\zeta_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}{\zeta_{\mathcal{I}}+\zeta_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}.

(199)

Similar generic abbreviations are defined for the
IS
case
in (
310
) below.

As described above, each specific splitting process corresponds to a
choice of
\varrho
and the indices
\mathcal{I}
,
\bar{\mathcal{I}}
,

\mathcal{X}
.
This turns the generic
abbreviations (
196
) into explicit expressions.
For
FS

V^{*}\to f\bar{f}
splittings, which are the subject of this
section, we get

\displaystyle a^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=(1-z)m_{i}I_{f_{i}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{V_{ij}}\pm zm_{j}I_{f_{i}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{V_{ij}},

\displaystyle b^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=m_{i}I_{f_{i}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{V_{ij}}\pm m_{j}I_{f_{i}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{V_{ij}},

\displaystyle c^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,i,j}

\displaystyle=\Big(2(1-z)zm_{ij}^{2}-(1-z)m_{i}^{2}-zm_{j}^{2}\Big)I_{f_{i}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{V_{ij}}+m_{i}m_{j}I_{f_{i}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{V_{ij}},

\displaystyle d^{V}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\pm I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast},

\displaystyle e^{V}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\pm I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}.

(200)

In order to reduce notational complexity
with these kind of symbols,
we often drop their indices
and use the shorthand notations
a_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}^{V}
,

b_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}^{V}
,
c_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}^{V}
,
d^{V}
,

e^{V}
, respectively.
The proper index assignments are always understood
w.r.t. the splitting process that is discussed in the given context.
Similarly, replacing
ij

by
ij^{\prime}
in the first three symbols
from (
200
) defines
a_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}^{V\prime}
,
b_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}^{V\prime}
, and
c_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}^{V\prime}
.

Wherever two splitting functions are related by symmetries, the
corresponding mappings also relate the respective sets of abbreviations,
as discussed in detail below.
Writing our results in terms of the expressions (
196
)
therefore highlights their symmetry properties.
This also applies to the crossing relations between
IS
and

FS
splitting functions from
Section
4.1.3
, with the definition of the
generic
IS
abbreviations from (
310
).

We conclude with a comment on the various massless limits of the
abbreviations.
First, we focus on
m_{\mathcal{X}}\to 0
.
To this end, we rewrite

\displaystyle c^{\varrho\sigma(-\sigma)}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}=-\zeta_{\mathcal{I}}m_{\mathcal{I}}b^{\varrho\sigma(-\sigma)}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(-1)+\zeta_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}m_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}b^{\varrho(-\sigma)\sigma}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(-1)-2\zeta_{\mathcal{X}}m_{\mathcal{X}}^{2}\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}.

(201)

Furthermore, the coupling relation (
60
) implies

\displaystyle b^{V\sigma(-\sigma)}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(-1)=\eta_{\tilde{V}_{\mathcal{X}}}m_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}_{\tilde{G}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}.

(202)

Similar to the discussion for the terms (
115
), this means
that
b^{V\sigma(-\sigma)}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}/m_{\mathcal{X}}

and
c^{V\sigma(-\sigma)}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}/m_{\mathcal{X}}

are well behaved for
m_{\mathcal{X}}\to 0
.
In fact, we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{\mathcal{X}}}c^{V\sigma(-\sigma)}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}\;\xrightarrow[m_{\mathcal{X}}\to 0]{G_{\mathcal{X}}\to H_{\mathcal{X}}}\;-\eta_{\tilde{V}_{\mathcal{X}}}a^{H\sigma(-\sigma)}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(-1),

(203)

upon relabeling the fields
G_{\mathcal{X}}\to H_{\mathcal{X}}
.
For massless fermions,
m_{\mathcal{I}},m_{\mathcal{\bar{I}}}\to 0
,
all symbols from (
196
) are obviously well behaved,
and we get

\displaystyle a^{\varrho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(\pm 1),b^{\varrho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(\pm 1)\;\xrightarrow{m_{\mathcal{I}},m_{\mathcal{\bar{I}}}\to 0}\;0,\qquad c^{\varrho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}\;\xrightarrow{m_{\mathcal{I}},m_{\mathcal{\bar{I}}}\to 0}\;-2\zeta_{\mathcal{X}}m_{\mathcal{X}}^{2}\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{1}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}.

(204)

Polarization
\kappa_{i}=+
,
\kappa_{j}=+
.

We obtain

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,+\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(z-1)z}\bigg\{a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(+1)a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(+1)\Big[g^{\mu\nu}+s_{\varepsilon}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}-s_{\varepsilon}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu}\Big]+k_{\perp}^{2}\frac{b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)}{m_{ij}^{2}m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\quad{}+2s_{\varepsilon}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-})\frac{a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(+1)b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)}{m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu}+2s_{\varepsilon}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})\frac{a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(+1)b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)}{m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}\bar{p}^{\nu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bigg\},

(205)

This result is well behaved in the limit of massless vector bosons since

b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)/m_{ij}
is well behaved, and because
of
WI
(
105
).
It is easily seen via (
204
) that the
splitting function (
205
) vanishes in the limit of
massless fermions.
This aligns with the expectation from the chirality structure of
the
V\bar{f}f
vertex (
38
).

Polarization
\kappa_{i}=+
,
\kappa_{j}=-
.

We find

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,-\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(z-1)z}\bigg\{

\displaystyle{}2I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\frac{c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}}{m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}\Big(s_{\varepsilon}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}+zk_{\perp}^{\nu}\Big)+2I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\frac{c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}}{m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}\bar{p}^{\nu}\Big(s_{\varepsilon}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-})\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}+zk_{\perp}^{\mu}\Big)

\displaystyle{}+I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\Big(-k_{\perp}^{2}g^{\mu\nu}-4(z-1)zk_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}+s_{\varepsilon}(2z-1)k_{\perp}^{2}\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle{}-\frac{c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}}{m_{ij}^{2}m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}\bigg\},

(206)

which remains finite in the limits
m_{ij}\to 0

and
m_{ij}^{\prime}\to 0
due to (
105
)
and (
203
).
For massless fermions, (
204
) implies
that all left-handed couplings
I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}
drop out
of (
206
).
This is, again, expected form the structure of the
V\bar{f}f

vertex (
38
).

Flipping the fermion polarizations.

Flipping the (anti-)fermion polarizations in the
V^{*}\to f\bar{f}
splitting
functions amounts to flipping the signs of the
\gamma^{5}
matrices inside
all spin projection matrices (
79
).
This, in turn, is equivalent to a global replacement

\gamma^{5}\to-\gamma^{5}
, followed by

I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}\leftrightarrow I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}

to reverse this
chirality flip in the couplings of the splitting vertices.
Because of (
187
),

\gamma^{5}\to-\gamma^{5}

goes along with a sign flip of the
\epsilon
-tensor and, thus, with
exchanging
\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}
and
\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}
,
according to (
193
) and (
194
).
With this at hand, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},-,\mp\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,\pm\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{\mathrm{L}\leftrightarrow\mathrm{R},\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}\leftrightarrow\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}}.

(207)

Unpolarized case.

Summing the above results, we get

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{U},\mathrm{U}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}\Big((m_{i}^{2}+m_{j}^{2})d^{V}(+1)-2m_{i}m_{j}e^{V}(+1)\Big)-c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}-c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}}{m_{ij}^{2}m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}(z-1)z}\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}

\displaystyle+\bigg(d^{V}(+1)\frac{-k_{\perp}^{2}+m_{i}^{2}(z-1)^{2}+m_{j}^{2}z^{2}}{(z-1)z}-2m_{i}m_{j}e^{V}(+1)\bigg)g^{\mu\nu}-4d^{V}(+1)k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}

\displaystyle+2\bigg(d^{V}(+1)\frac{m_{i}^{2}+m_{ij}^{2}(1-2z)-m_{j}^{2}}{m_{ij}^{2}}+d^{V}(-1)\bigg)\bar{p}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}

\displaystyle+2\bigg(d^{V}(+1)\frac{m_{i}^{2}+m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}(1-2z)-m_{j}^{2}}{m_{ij}^{\prime\,2}}+d^{V}(-1)\bigg)\bar{p}^{\nu}k_{\perp}^{\mu}

\displaystyle+d^{V}(-1)\frac{(2z-1)k_{\perp}^{2}+m_{i}^{2}(z-1)^{2}-m_{j}^{2}z^{2}}{(z-1)z}s_{\varepsilon}\Big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu}\Big)

\displaystyle+4s_{\varepsilon}d^{V}(-1)\Big((k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-})\bar{p}^{\nu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}+(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}\Big).

(208)

4.3.2
\boldsymbol{V^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f}

splitting functions

The splitting functions for
V^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
may be obtained
via (
88
) from those for
V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
,

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\bar{f}_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}=\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{j}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{i}}\big(1-z,-k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{j},\kappa_{i}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j}}.

(209)

More precisely, the couplings on the r.h.s. are related to the ones of

V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
of Section
4.3.1
by

\displaystyle I_{f_{i}^{\sigma}f_{j}^{\sigma}}^{V_{ij}^{(\prime)}}\to I_{f_{j}^{\sigma}f_{i}^{\sigma}}^{V_{ij}^{(\prime)}}.

(210)

Of course, this is reflected by the fact that the diagrams for
V^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
splittings,

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle{}={}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{48.36969pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
;
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

(211)

and their corresponding analytical expressions,

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij})}\Big]_{\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}={}

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}eT^{(n)\,\alpha}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})G^{\bar{V}_{ij}V_{ij}}_{\alpha\beta}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\bar{u}_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j})\Big(\gamma^{\beta}\omega_{-}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}+\gamma^{\beta}\omega_{+}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\Big)v_{\kappa_{i}}(p_{i}),

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij})}\Big]_{\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}={}

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}eT^{(n)\,\alpha}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})p_{ij,\alpha}G^{\bar{G}_{ij}G_{ij}}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\frac{1}{m_{ij}^{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\bar{u}_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j})\left\{\omega_{+}\left(m_{j}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}-m_{i}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}\right)+\omega_{-}\left(m_{j}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}-m_{i}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\right)\right\}v_{\kappa_{i}}(p_{i}),

(212)

are obtained from the
V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
counterparts by swapping
i\leftrightarrow j
and multiplying with a conventional
sign
-1
.
16
16

16

The relative sign factor between (
212
)
and (
185
) accounts for the interchange of
fermionic fields that is involved in the symmetry relation between the two
splitting processes.

This relation can, alternatively, be expressed in terms of charge
conjugation.
The charge conjugation matrix
C
is defined via its action on Dirac matrices,

\displaystyle C\gamma^{\mu}C^{-1}=-(\gamma^{\mu})^{\mathrm{T}},\qquad C\gamma^{5}C^{-1}=+(\gamma^{5})^{\mathrm{T}}.

(213)

In the Dirac and the chiral representations of the
\gamma^{\mu}
matrices, we get

\displaystyle C=\mathrm{i}\gamma^{2}\gamma^{0},\qquad C^{-1}=C^{\dagger}=C^{\mathrm{T}}=-C,

(214)

relating spinor fields
\psi
,
\bar{\psi}

to their
respective charge-conjugated counterparts
\psi^{\mathrm{c}}
,

\bar{\psi}^{\mathrm{c}}
via

\displaystyle\psi^{\mathrm{c}}=C\bar{\psi}^{\mathrm{T}},\qquad\bar{\psi}^{\mathrm{c}}=\psi^{\mathrm{T}}C,

(215)

where trivial charge parity phases are assumed.
In particular, we can choose the phases of the Dirac spinors
u,v
so that

\displaystyle v_{\kappa}(p)=C\bar{u}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{T}}(p),\quad u_{\kappa}(p)=C\bar{v}_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{T}}(p),\quad\bar{v}_{\kappa}(p)=u_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{T}}(p)C,\quad\bar{u}_{\kappa}(p)=v_{\kappa}^{\mathrm{T}}(p)C.

(216)

By transposition, insertions of
1=C^{\dagger}C
, and identification
of (
216
), it can be seen
that (
212
) is obtained from (
185
)
by multiplication with the conventional sign
-1
and swapping the
couplings

\displaystyle I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}^{(\prime)}}\to I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}^{(\prime)}},\qquad I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}^{(\prime)}}\to I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}^{(\prime)}}.

(217)

In contrast to (
210
), this flips
the assignment of chiralities to the couplings
(without changing
m_{i}
,
m_{j}
,
z
).
Altogether,

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\bar{f}_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{\eqref{eq:VFF-swap-cpl-w-chir}},

(218)

where the polarizations
\kappa_{i}
and
\kappa_{j}
remain fixed.
Note that this derivation does not assume CP invariance of the model,
i.e. this relation remains valid in the presence of CKM-like mixing.

For
V^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
splittings, we obtain the following
abbreviations from (
196
),

\displaystyle a^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=zm_{j}I_{f_{j}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{V_{ij}}\pm(1-z)m_{i}I_{f_{j}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{V_{ij}},

\displaystyle b^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=m_{j}I_{f_{j}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{V_{ij}}\pm m_{i}I_{f_{j}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{V_{ij}},

\displaystyle c^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,j,i}

\displaystyle=\big[2(1-z)zm_{ij}^{2}-(1-z)m_{i}^{2}-zm_{j}^{2})\big]I_{f_{j}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{V_{ij}}+m_{i}m_{j}I_{f_{j}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{V_{ij}},

\displaystyle d^{V}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\pm I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast},

\displaystyle e^{V}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}\pm I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}.

(219)

As anticipated in Section
4.3.1
, the combination of
the substitutions

m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j}
,
z\to 1-z
, and (
210
)
from the
i\leftrightarrow j
symmetry rule (
209
) maps
the abbreviations (
200
) for
V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}

splittings into these expressions.
Thus, when written in terms of the respective abbreviations, the

i\leftrightarrow j
symmetry relation between
V^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
and

V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
splitting functions becomes particularly
transparent.
17
17

17

The rule (
217
) from charge conjugation
(without changing
m_{i}
,
m_{j}
, and
z
)
also
induces mappings between the two sets of abbreviations,

\displaystyle a^{V\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle\to\pm a^{V\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1),

\displaystyle a^{V\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle\to\pm a^{V\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1),

\displaystyle b^{V\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle\to\pm b^{V\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1),

\displaystyle b^{V\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle\to\pm b^{V\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1),

\displaystyle c^{V\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}_{ij,i,j}

\displaystyle\to c^{V\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}_{ij,j,i},

\displaystyle c^{V\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}_{ij,i,j}

\displaystyle\to c^{V\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}_{ij,j,i},

\displaystyle d^{V}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle\to\pm d^{V}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1),

\displaystyle e^{V}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle\to\pm e^{V}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1).

(220)

Although slightly more complicated, these relations highlight the
symmetry between the associated splitting functions as well.

As an explicit example, we consider the special case of
V^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
splittings with massless vector bosons
V_{ij}
,

V_{ij^{\prime}}
.
The corresponding
V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
splitting
functions are obtained from the results of Section
4.3.1
by
taking the limit

\displaystyle m_{ij},m_{ij}^{\prime}\to 0,\qquad\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}}{m_{ij}}\to 0,\qquad\frac{\bar{p}^{\nu}}{m_{ij}^{\prime}}\to 0,

(221)

leading to

\displaystyle\bigg[{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\pm,\mp\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{ij}^{(\prime)}=0}

\displaystyle=I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R/L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R/L}}}^{V_{ij}}\Big(I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R/L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R/L}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}\Big)^{\ast}\begin{aligned} \bigg\{&\frac{-k_{\perp}^{2}}{(z-1)z}g^{\mu\nu}-4k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}\\
&{}\pm s_{\varepsilon}\frac{2z-1}{(z-1)z}k_{\perp}^{2}\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}\big)\bigg\},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\bigg[{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\pm,\pm\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{ij}^{(\prime)}=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{V\,\mathrm{LR/RL}}_{ij,i,j}(+1)a^{V\,\mathrm{LR/RL}}_{ij^{\prime},i,j}(+1)}{(z-1)z}\bigg\{g^{\mu\nu}\mp s_{\varepsilon}\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}\big)\bigg\}.

(222)

First, applying (
209
) and (
210
) via

\displaystyle z\to 1-z,\quad k_{\perp}^{\mu}\to-k_{\perp}^{\mu},\quad\kappa_{i}\leftrightarrow\kappa_{j},\quad m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j},\quad I_{f_{i}^{\sigma}f_{j}^{\sigma}}^{V_{ij\smash{{}^{(\prime)}}}}\to I_{f_{j}^{\sigma}f_{i}^{\sigma}}^{V_{ij\smash{{}^{(\prime)}}}}

(223)

yields the
V^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
splitting functions

\displaystyle\bigg[{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\bar{f}_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mp,\pm\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{ij}^{(\prime)}=0}

\displaystyle=I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R/L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R/L}}}^{V_{ij}}\Big(I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R/L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R/L}}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}\Big)^{\ast}\begin{aligned} \bigg\{&\frac{-k_{\perp}^{2}}{(z-1)z}g^{\mu\nu}-4k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}\\
&{}\mp s_{\varepsilon}\frac{2z-1}{(z-1)z}k_{\perp}^{2}\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}\big)\bigg\},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\bigg[{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\bar{f}_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\pm,\pm\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{ij}^{(\prime)}=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{V\,\mathrm{LR/RL}}_{ij,j,i}(+1)a^{V\,\mathrm{LR/RL}}_{ij^{\prime},j,i}(+1)}{(z-1)z}\bigg\{g^{\mu\nu}\mp s_{\varepsilon}\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}\big)\bigg\}.

(224)

Second, these results may also be obtained from (
222
)
by simply applying (
217
).

(225)

translates into

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(H_{ij})}\Big]_{f_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}=\frac{eT^{(n)}_{H_{ij}X}(p_{ij})}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{u}_{\kappa_{i}}(p_{i})\left(\omega_{+}C^{\mathrm{R}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}+\omega_{-}C^{\mathrm{L}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}\right)v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j}).

(226)

In the derivation of the splitting functions given below, we use the abbreviations

\displaystyle a^{H\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=(1-z)m_{i}C^{\sigma_{1}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}\pm zm_{j}C^{\sigma_{2}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}},

\displaystyle b^{H\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=m_{i}C^{\sigma_{1}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}\pm m_{j}C^{\sigma_{2}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}},

\displaystyle d^{H}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=C^{\mathrm{L}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}\pm C^{\mathrm{R}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{R}\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}},

\displaystyle e^{H}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=C^{\mathrm{L}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{R}\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}\pm C^{\mathrm{R}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}},

(227)

as obtained from (
196
).
The symbols
c^{H}
are not relevant and hence omitted.

Polarization
\kappa_{i}=+
,
\kappa_{j}=+
.

The result

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,+\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{(z-1)z}C^{\mathrm{L}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}

(228)

only involves left-handed couplings.
In the massless limit, this reproduces the expectation from the
chirality structure of the

H\bar{f}f
vertex (
39
).
In the
EWSM
, (
228
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H}_{f_{i}}{}^{H}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,+\big)\bigg|_{\mathrm{EWSM}}=\frac{m_{i}^{2}}{(ev)^{2}}\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{(z-1)z}\delta_{f_{i}f_{j}}.

(229)

Polarization
\kappa_{i}=+
,
\kappa_{j}=-
.

Consistently with the structure of the
H\bar{f}f

vertex (
39
), the properties
(
204
) imply that the splitting
function

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,-\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H}(-1)a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)}{(z-1)z}

(230)

vanishes for massless fermions.
In the
EWSM
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H}_{f_{i}}{}^{H}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,-\big)\bigg|_{\mathrm{EWSM}}=-\frac{m_{i}^{4}}{(ev)^{2}}\frac{(1-2z)^{2}}{(z-1)z}\delta_{f_{i}f_{j}}.

(231)

Flipping the fermion polarizations.

This proceeds as described in (
207
), with
\mathrm{L}\leftrightarrow\mathrm{R}
meaning

\displaystyle C^{\mathrm{L}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}\leftrightarrow C^{\mathrm{R}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}.

(232)

Unpolarized case.

The above expressions, together with their polarization-flipped
equivalents, combine into

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{U},\mathrm{U}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}d^{H}(+1)-a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H}(-1)a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)-a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H}(-1)a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)}{(z-1)z}.

(233)

4.3.4
\boldsymbol{H^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f}

splitting functions

Again, the splitting functions for
H^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
and
H^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
are related via (
88
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\bar{f}_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}{}^{H_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{j}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{i}}\big(1-z,-k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{j},\kappa_{i}\big)\Big|_{m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j}},

(234)

where the ones on the r.h.s. are obtained from the results of
Section
4.3.3
through

\displaystyle C^{\sigma}_{H_{ij}^{(\prime)}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}\to C^{\sigma}_{H_{ij}^{(\prime)}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}.

(235)

Inspecting the r.h.s. of (
234
), we observe
that
m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j}
is equivalent to
z\to 1-z
,

k_{\perp}\to-k_{\perp}
, and
\kappa_{i}\leftrightarrow\kappa_{j}
.
Thus, the splitting functions for
H^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
are recovered
from those for
H^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
by
applying (
235
) alone.

This is confirmed when studying the relation of

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(H_{ij})}\Big]_{\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle{}={}
p_{ij}
H_{ij}
p_{i}
\bar{f}_{i},\kappa_{i}
p_{j}
f_{j},\kappa_{j}
T^{(n)}_{H_{ij}X}

(236)

and

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(H_{ij})}\Big]_{\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}=\frac{-eT^{(n)}_{H_{ij}X}(p_{ij})}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{u}_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j})\left(\omega_{+}C^{\mathrm{R}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}+\omega_{-}C^{\mathrm{L}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}\right)v_{\kappa_{i}}(p_{i}),

(237)

to (
225
) and (
226
) via charge
conjugation, along the lines of Section
4.3.1
.
Specifically, due to the absence of a
\gamma^{\mu}
-matrix in the
scalar–fermion coupling, it is found that (
226
)
differs from (
237
) only
by (
235
), in agreement with the results given above.
In contrast to (
217
) for the
V^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
case, the chirality labels in the couplings are
not
swapped
in (
226
), and charge conjugation compensates the
conventional sign between the diagrams (
225
)
and (
236
).

For
H^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
splittings, the
abbreviations (
196
) read

\displaystyle a^{H\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=zm_{j}C^{\sigma_{1}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}\pm(1-z)m_{i}C^{\sigma_{2}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}},

\displaystyle b^{H\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=m_{j}C^{\sigma_{1}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}\pm m_{i}C^{\sigma_{2}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}},

\displaystyle d^{H}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=C^{\mathrm{L}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}\pm C^{\mathrm{R}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}C^{\mathrm{R}\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}},

\displaystyle e^{H}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=C^{\mathrm{L}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}C^{\mathrm{R}\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}\pm C^{\mathrm{R}}_{H_{ij}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{j}f_{i}}.

(238)

Similar to the situation for
V^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
splittings, the
combination of
m_{i}\leftrightarrow m_{j}
and
z\to 1-z

with (
235
) maps the
abbreviations (
227
) for
H^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
splittings
into these expressions.
Again, this emphasizes the
i\leftrightarrow j
symmetry between the
associated splitting functions.
18
18

18

As in the
V\to\bar{f}f
case, the symmetry relation between
H^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
and
H^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
splitting functions that is
suggested by charge conjugation, i.e. the application
of (
235
)
alone
, yields abbreviation
mappings

\displaystyle a^{H\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle\to\pm a^{H\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1}}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1),

\displaystyle b^{H\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle\to\pm b^{H\sigma_{2}\sigma_{1}}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1),

\displaystyle d^{H}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle\to d^{H}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1),

\displaystyle e^{H}_{ij,i,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle\to e^{H}_{ij,j,i}(\pm 1),

which are slightly more complicated.
Nevertheless, these substitutions highlight the symmetries of the
splitting functions, too.
Compare also to Footnote
17
.

4.3.5
\boldsymbol{V^{\ast}/H^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}}

splitting functions

Utilizing the ingredients from above, we obtain splitting functions
for the interference
V^{\ast}/H^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
.
Because they are proportional to the product of “spin-preserving”

V\bar{f}f
and “spin-flipping”
H\bar{f}f
couplings, they vanish for massless
fermions.
Results for flipped polarizations and the case
V^{\ast}/H^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
may be obtained as before.
Specifically, charge conjugation corresponds
to (
217
), (
235
), and
multiplication by
-1
.
When recovering
H^{\ast}/V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
splitting functions
via (
92
), complex conjugation entails

\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{\pm}\to\bar{\varepsilon}^{\ast\mu}_{\pm}=s_{\varepsilon}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{\mp}
.

Polarization
\kappa_{i}=+
,
\kappa_{j}=+
.

We get

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,+\big)\bigg]^{\mu}=\frac{C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}\bar{f}_{i}f_{j}}}{(z-1)z}\bigg\{\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)}{m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}+2s_{\varepsilon}a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(+1)(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bigg\},

(239)

with the
EWSM
special case

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{f_{i}}{}^{H_{\vphantom{ij}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,+\big)\bigg]^{\mu}_{\mathrm{EWSM}}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ev}\frac{m_{i}^{2}}{(z-1)z}\bigg\{2s_{\varepsilon}\Big((z-1)I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}-zI_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\Big)(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\quad{}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{m_{ij}^{2}}\Big(I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}-I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\Big)\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg\}\delta_{f_{i}f_{j}}.

(240)

Polarization
\kappa_{i}=+
,
\kappa_{j}=-
.

For

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,-\big)\bigg]^{\mu}=2s_{\varepsilon}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)\bigg(\frac{(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-})}{z}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}+\frac{(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})}{z-1}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bigg)+\frac{a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}}{(z-1)zm_{ij}^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu},

(241)

the
EWSM
limit becomes

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{f_{i}}{}^{H_{\vphantom{ij}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,-\big)\bigg]^{\mu}_{\mathrm{EWSM}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(2z-1)m_{i}^{2}}{ev}\bigg\{

\displaystyle 2s_{\varepsilon}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\biggl(\frac{(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-})}{z}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}+\frac{(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})}{z-1}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\biggr)

\displaystyle{}-\frac{1}{(z-1)zm_{ij}^{2}}\Big(\big(m_{i}^{2}+2(z-1)zm_{ij}^{2}\big)I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}-m_{i}^{2}I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}\Big)\bar{p}^{\mu}\bigg\}\delta_{f_{i}f_{j}}.

(242)

Unpolarized case.

The combination of all polarization configurations yields

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{U},\mathrm{U}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\frac{a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}+a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}+k_{\perp}^{2}\Big(I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)+I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}b_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)\Big)}{(z-1)zm_{ij}^{2}}\bar{p}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\phantom{={}}+2\bigg(I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H\prime\ast}(+1)+I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}b_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H\prime\ast}(+1)\bigg)k_{\perp}^{\mu}.

(243)

4.3.6
\boldsymbol{\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}}

splitting functions

The computation of
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}
splitting functions
involves the diagrams

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\bar{f}_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\lambda_{i}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle{}={}
\displaystyle\hbox to115.86pt{\vbox to57.03pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 17.27165pt\lower-28.6808pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-10.45801pt}{-1.16167pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

;{

\lxSVG@closescope {
{}{}{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{48.36969pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
;
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

(244)

corresponding to the analytical expressions

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\bar{f}_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\lambda_{i}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}e\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})G^{f_{ij}\bar{f}_{ij}}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\not{\varepsilon}_{\lambda_{i}}^{\ast}(p_{i})\left(\omega_{-}I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}+\omega_{+}I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\right)v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j}),

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\bar{f}_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}e\eta_{V_{i}}\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})G^{f_{ij}\bar{f}_{ij}}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\frac{1}{m_{i}}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\cdot\left\{\omega_{+}\left(m_{ij}I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}-m_{j}I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\right)+\omega_{-}\left(m_{ij}I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}-m_{j}I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\right)\right\}v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j}),

(245)

where (
60
) was used for the
{\bar{G}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}
coupling.
Combining the techniques introduced in the previous sections,
we obtain the results quoted below.
In particular, we employ an identity similar
to the decomposition (
100
) for the treatment of mass-singular
parts in the splitting functions involving longitudinally polarized vector bosons.
On top of this, we insert the spin-projected completeness
relation (
78
), the Sudakov
parametrization (
69
), reduce the occurring Dirac
chains into a minimal set, and evaluate the quasi-collinear limit
using the power-counting (
116
).
The adaption of
our alternative strategy for the treatment of mass-singular factors,
developed in
Appendix
C.1
for
V^{\ast}\to VV
splittings,
to the
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}
case is
discussed in Appendix
C.2
.
We arrive at results featuring the following Dirac structures,
sandwiched between stripped underlying amplitudes

\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}X}
and

T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}X}
,

\displaystyle\not{\bar{p}},\qquad\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5},\qquad\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}},\qquad\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}.

(246)

As we detail in Section
E.1
,
these contractions
may be expressed in terms of matrix elements
of definite polarization,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\pm}_{\bar{f}X}

\displaystyle=\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}X}v_{\pm},\qquad\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\pm\,\ast}_{\bar{f}X}=\bar{v}_{\pm}T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}X}.

(247)

Recall that
\Sigma_{\pm}v_{\pm}=v_{\pm}
correspond to antifermions with spin

\pm 1/2
in the
reference direction defined by a vector
r^{\mu}
, as discussed
below (
79
).
In particular, for massless
antifermions
\bar{f}_{ij}
,
\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}

with on-shell momentum
p^{\mu}
,
we can identify matrix elements of definite helicity via

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p)\,\not{p}\frac{1\pm\gamma^{5}}{2}\,T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}(p)=\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p)\,\frac{1\mp\gamma^{5}}{2}\not{p}\,T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}(p)=\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\pm}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\pm\,\ast}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}.

(248)

Terms involving
\not{k}_{\perp}
typically receive an additional
suppression in the
quasi-collinear limit, as detailed in Section
E.2
.
According to (
92
), our results are
symmetric under

\displaystyle m_{ij}\leftrightarrow m_{ij}^{\prime},\quad f_{ij}\leftrightarrow f_{ij^{\prime}},

(249)

and complex conjugation.
Combining complex conjugation with the transposition of Dirac chains
and subsequent reordering of
\gamma_{0}
from

T^{\dagger}\dots\gamma_{0}T
to
T^{\dagger}\gamma_{0}\dots T

implies

\displaystyle\not{\bar{p}}\to\not{\bar{p}},\qquad\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\to\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5},\qquad\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\to-\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}},\qquad\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\to\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}

(250)

for the Dirac structures from (
246
).
In the
EWSM
with diagonal
Cabibbo–Kobayashi–Maskawa (CKM)
matrix, we have
m_{ij}=m_{ij}^{\prime}
,
and our splitting functions simplify accordingly.

Finally, for
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}
splittings, the abbreviations
introduced in (
196
) read

\displaystyle a^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{i,ij,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=-(1-z)zm_{ij}I_{f_{ij}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\pm zm_{j}I_{f_{ij}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}},

\displaystyle b^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{i,ij,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=m_{ij}I_{f_{ij}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\pm m_{j}I_{f_{ij}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}},

\displaystyle c^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{i,ij,j}

\displaystyle=\big((1-z)zm_{ij}^{2}-2(1-z)m_{i}^{2}-zm_{j}^{2})\big)I_{f_{ij}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}+m_{ij}m_{j}z^{2}I_{f_{ij}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{j}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}},

\displaystyle d^{V}_{i,ij,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{f_{ij^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}\pm I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{f_{ij^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}

\displaystyle e^{V}_{i,ij,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{f_{ij^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}\pm I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{f_{ij^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}.

(251)

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{T}
,
\kappa_{j}=+
.

We find

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},+\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{z^{2}}\bigg(I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(+1)+I_{f_{ij^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(+1)\bigg)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}

\displaystyle\phantom{={}}-\frac{1}{z^{2}}\bigg(I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(+1)-I_{f_{ij^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(+1)\bigg)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}

\displaystyle\phantom{={}}+\frac{1}{(z-1)z^{2}}\bigg(I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{f_{ij^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}k_{\perp}^{2}(z^{2}-2z+2)-a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(+1)a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(+1)\bigg)\not{\bar{p}}

\displaystyle\phantom{={}}+\frac{1}{(z-1)z^{2}}\bigg(I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{f_{ij^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}k_{\perp}^{2}(z^{2}-2z+2)+a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(+1)a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(+1)\bigg)\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}.

(252)

The limit of vanishing fermion masses can be taken
via (
204
) and yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},+\big)\bigg|_{m_{ij}=m_{ij}^{\prime}=m_{j}=0}=2k_{\perp}^{2}\frac{z^{2}-2z+2}{(z-1)z^{2}}\,I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{f_{ij^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}\,\not{\bar{p}}\frac{1+\gamma^{5}}{2}.

(253)

Using (
248
), we can associate this
result to the hard matrix element for a massless antifermion of
helicity
+\frac{1}{2}
.
Once more, the spin structure matches the anticipation from the
“spin-preserving” nature of the
V\bar{f}f

vertex (
38
).
Similarly, only left-handed couplings remain.

Polarization
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{L}
,
\kappa_{j}=+
.

We get

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},+\big)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m_{i}^{2}(z-1)z}\bigg\{\Big(b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}-b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V})\Big)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}

\displaystyle\quad{}+\Big(b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}+b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V})\Big)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}

\displaystyle\quad{}-\bigg(zk_{\perp}^{2}b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)+\frac{c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}}{z}\bigg)\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}

\displaystyle\quad{}+\bigg(zk_{\perp}^{2}b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)-\frac{c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}}{z}\bigg)\not{\bar{p}}\bigg\}.

(254)

This result is well behaved for
m_{i}\to 0
due to the fact that

c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V(\prime)}/m_{i}
,

b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)/m_{i}
, and

b_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}(-1)/m_{i}

all have proper small-mass limits.
More specifically, (
202
)
and (
203
) yield

\displaystyle\frac{b_{\sigma(-\sigma)}^{V}(-1)}{m_{i}}\,\xrightarrow[m_{i}\to 0]{G_{i}\to H_{i}}\,-\eta_{V_{i}}^{\ast}C^{\sigma}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}},\qquad\frac{c_{\sigma(-\sigma)}^{V}}{m_{i}}\,\xrightarrow[m_{i}\to 0]{G_{i}\to H_{i}}\,\eta_{V_{i}}^{\ast}a_{\sigma(-\sigma)}^{H}(-1),

(255)

which implies that we recover the corresponding splitting
function (
275
) for
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to H\bar{f}
given below,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\kappa\big)\,\xrightarrow[m_{i}\to 0]{G_{i}\to H_{i}}\,\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa\big)\bigg|_{m_{i}=0}.

(256)

For vanishing fermion masses, (
204
) implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},+\big)\bigg|_{m_{ij}=m_{ij}^{\prime}=m_{j}=0}=-\frac{2m_{i}^{2}(z-1)}{z^{2}}\,I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}I_{f_{ij^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}\ast}\,\not{\bar{p}}\frac{1+\gamma^{5}}{2},

(257)

again consistent with the “spin-preserving”
V\bar{f}f

vertex (
38
).

Flipping the antifermion polarization.

This proceeds as described in Section
4.3.1
, with the
exception that the
\gamma^{5}
matrices appear explicitly in our results.
We get

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T}/\mathrm{L},\mp\big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T}/\mathrm{L},\pm\big)\bigg|_{\mathrm{L}\leftrightarrow\mathrm{R},\gamma^{5}\to-\gamma^{5}}.

(258)

Unpolarized cases.

Summing over the polarizations of the external
antifermion while keeping the vector boson transverse,
we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{U}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(z-1)z^{2}}\begin{aligned} \Big\{&\Big(k_{\perp}^{2}(z^{2}-2z+2)d^{V}(-1)+a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(+1)a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(+1)-a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}(+1)a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}(+1)\Big)\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\\
&{}+\Big(k_{\perp}^{2}(z^{2}-2z+2)d^{V}(+1)-a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(+1)a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(+1)-a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}(+1)a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}(+1)\Big)\not{\bar{p}}\Big\}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\phantom{={}}+\frac{z-1}{z}\Big\{(m_{ij}+m_{ij}^{\prime})d^{V}(-1)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}+(m_{ij}-m_{ij}^{\prime})d^{V}(+1)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\Big\}.

(259)

For a longitudinal vector boson, this procedure yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{L},\mathrm{U}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(z-1)m_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\frac{1}{z}\Big(b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}-b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}+b_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}(-1)c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}-b_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}\Big)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}

\displaystyle\phantom{={}}+\frac{1}{z}\Big(b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}+b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}-b_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}(-1)c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}-b_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}\Big)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}

\displaystyle\phantom{={}}+\bigg(-\frac{c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}}{z^{2}}-\frac{c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}}{z^{2}}+k_{\perp}^{2}\Big(b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)+b_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}(-1)b_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)\Big)\bigg)\not{\bar{p}}

\displaystyle\phantom{={}}+\bigg(\frac{c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}c_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}}{z^{2}}-\frac{c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}c_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}}{z^{2}}-k_{\perp}^{2}\Big(b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V}(-1)b_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)-b_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V}(-1)b_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{V\prime\ast}(-1)\Big)\bigg)\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\Big\}.

(260)

4.3.7

splitting functions

f^{\ast}\to Vf

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-10.76846pt}{-2.13611pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

;{
{}{}{}}{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 66.93 0 M 70.87 0 C 70.87 2.17 69.1 3.94 66.93 3.94 C 64.75 3.94 62.99 2.17 62.99 0 C 62.99 -2.17 64.75 -3.94 66.93 -3.94 C 69.1 -3.94 70.87 -2.17 70.87 0 Z M 66.93 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{48.36969pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

(261)

with the analytical expressions

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(f_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}f_{j}X}^{\lambda_{i}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}e\bar{u}_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j})\not{\varepsilon}_{\lambda_{i}}^{\ast}(p_{j})\left(\omega_{-}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}+\omega_{+}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\right)G^{f_{ij}\bar{f}_{ij}}(-p_{ij},p_{ij})T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}(p_{ij}),

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(f_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}f_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}e\eta_{V_{i}}\bar{u}_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j})\frac{1}{m_{i}}\left\{\omega_{+}\left(m_{j}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}-m_{ij}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\right)+\omega_{-}\left(m_{j}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}-m_{ij}I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\cdot G^{f_{ij}\bar{f}_{ij}}(-p_{ij},p_{ij})T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}(p_{ij}).

(262)

Alternatively, we may recover these results from the
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}

case by studying the effect of charge conjugation.
Specifically, we find that (
262
) is obtained
from (
245
) by transposition, multiplication
with
-1
, replacing

\displaystyle I_{f_{ij^{(\prime)}}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\to I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{ij^{(\prime)}}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}},\qquad I_{f_{ij^{(\prime)}}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\to I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{ij^{(\prime)}}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}},

(263)

and inserting the spinor relations (
216
) as well as

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\to T^{(n)\,T}_{f_{ij}X}(p_{ij})C,

(264)

with
C
denoting the charge-conjugation matrix defined in (
213
).
The object
T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}

occurs in the
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}
splitting functions
in Dirac chains of the form

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Gamma T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Gamma\gamma^{5}T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij}),

(265)

where
\Gamma=\not{v}_{1}\dots\not{v}_{m}
for some
real four-vectors
v_{i}^{\mu}
.
To be precise, we encounter

\displaystyle\Gamma=\not{\bar{p}}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\Gamma=\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}.

(266)

The substitution (
264
) transforms
the Dirac chains from (
265
)
in the following way:

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Gamma T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})

\displaystyle\to(-1)^{m+1}\bar{T}^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\tilde{\Gamma}T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}(p_{ij}),

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Gamma\gamma^{5}T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})

\displaystyle\to-\bar{T}^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\tilde{\Gamma}\gamma^{5}T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}(p_{ij}),

(267)

where
\tilde{\Gamma}=\not{v}_{m}\dots\not{v}_{1}
.
Utilization of commutation relations and
\bar{p}k_{\perp}=0
shows that
the application of (
267
)
and therefore of (
264
) to the
Dirac chains involving (
266
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\not{\bar{p}}\to\not{\bar{p}},\qquad\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\to-\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5},\qquad\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\to\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}},\qquad\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\to\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}.

(268)

Combining the above, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{f_{ij^{\prime}}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\lambda_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)=\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{V_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\lambda_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg|_{\eqref{eq:FVF-swap-cpl-w-chir},\,\eqref{eq:ch-conj-transpos-effect-on-dirac-chains-equiv}}.

(269)

Finally, similar to the
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}
case, the Dirac
structures (
246
) are related to
matrix elements of definite polarization,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\pm}_{fX}

\displaystyle=\bar{u}_{\pm}T^{(n)}_{fX},\qquad\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\pm\,\ast}_{fX}=\bar{T}^{(n)}_{fX}u_{\pm},

(270)

as we discuss in Appendix
E.1
.
Again, the spinors
\Sigma_{\pm}u_{\pm}=u_{\pm}
correspond to fermions with
spin
\pm 1/2
in the reference direction defined by some vector
r^{\mu}
,
as detailed below (
79
).
For massless
fermions
f_{ij}
,
f_{ij^{\prime}}

with on-shell momentum
p^{\mu}
,
we get

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}(p)\,\not{p}\frac{1\mp\gamma^{5}}{2}\,T^{(n)}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}(p)=\bar{T}^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}(p)\,\frac{1\pm\gamma^{5}}{2}\not{p}\,T^{(n)}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}(p)=\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\pm}_{f_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\pm\,\ast}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X},

(271)

with matrix elements of definite helicity.
