is evaluated to

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\bar{f}_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}e\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})G^{f_{ij}\bar{f}_{ij}}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\Big(\omega_{+}C^{\mathrm{R}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}+\omega_{-}C^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}\Big)v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j}).

(273)

Our results on the splitting functions,
quoted below, are symmetric
under (
249
), combined
with (
250
).
They are expressed in terms of the abbreviations

\displaystyle a^{H\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{i,ij,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=-(1-z)zm_{ij}C^{\sigma_{1}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}\pm zm_{j}C^{\sigma_{2}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}},

\displaystyle b^{H\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{i,ij,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=m_{ij}C^{\sigma_{1}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}\pm m_{j}C^{\sigma_{2}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}

\displaystyle d^{H}_{i,ij,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=C^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}f_{j}}\pm C^{\mathrm{R}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{R}\ast}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}f_{j}}

\displaystyle e^{H}_{i,ij,j}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=C^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{R}\ast}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}f_{j}}\pm C^{\mathrm{R}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}f_{j}},

(274)

which are implied by (
196
).

Polarization
\kappa_{j}=+
.

We get

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+\big)=\frac{1}{2(z-1)z}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\bigg(zC^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}f_{j}}k_{\perp}^{2}-\frac{a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H}(-1)a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)}{z}\bigg)\not{\bar{p}}

\displaystyle-\bigg(zC^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}f_{j}}k_{\perp}^{2}+\frac{a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H}(-1)a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)}{z}\bigg)\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}

\displaystyle-\bigg(C^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)-C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}f_{j}}a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H}(-1)\bigg)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}

\displaystyle-\bigg(C^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)+C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}f_{j}}a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H}(-1)\bigg)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\bigg\},

(275)

with the
EWSM
form

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+\big)\bigg|_{\mathrm{EWSM}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(ev)^{2}}\frac{m_{j}^{2}}{2(z-1)}\bigg\{\big(k_{\perp}^{2}-(z-2)^{2}m_{j}^{2}\big)\not{\bar{p}}-\big(k_{\perp}^{2}+(z-2)^{2}m_{j}^{2}\big)\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}-2(z-2)m_{j}\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\bigg\}\delta_{f_{ij}f_{j}}\delta_{f_{ij}^{\prime}f_{j}}.

(276)

For massless fermions, (
204
) implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+\big)\bigg|_{m_{ij}=m_{ij}^{\prime}=m_{j}=0}=\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{z-1}\,C^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij}f_{j}}C^{\mathrm{L}\ast}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}f_{j}}\,\not{\bar{p}}\frac{1-\gamma^{5}}{2},

(277)

with a Dirac structure that is associated to a hard matrix element with
a massless antifermion of
helicity
-\frac{1}{2}
, reflecting the
“spin-flipping” nature of the

H\bar{f}f
vertex (
39
).
Consistently, only left-handed couplings remain
in (
277
).

Unpolarized case.

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+\big)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\frac{1}{z-1}\bigg(k_{\perp}^{2}d^{H}(+1)-\frac{a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H}(-1)a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)+a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H}(-1)a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)}{z^{2}}\bigg)\not{\bar{p}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{z-1}\bigg(k_{\perp}^{2}d^{H}(-1)+\frac{a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H}(-1)a_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{R}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)-a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H}(-1)a_{\mathrm{R}\mathrm{L}}^{H\prime\ast}(-1)}{z^{2}}\bigg)\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}

\displaystyle+\big(m_{ij}-m_{ij}^{\prime}\big)d^{H}(+1)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}-\big(m_{ij}+m_{ij}^{\prime}\big)d^{H}(-1)\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\bigg\}.

(278)

4.3.9
\boldsymbol{f^{\ast}\to Hf}

splitting functions

The diagram corresponding to the
f^{\ast}\to Hf
splitting functions reads

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(f_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}f_{j}X}^{\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle{}={}
p_{ij}
f_{ij}
p_{i}
H_{i}
p_{j}
f_{j},\kappa_{j}
T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}

(279)

and translates into

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(f_{ij})}\Big]_{H_{i}f_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}e\bar{u}_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j})\Big(\omega_{+}C^{\mathrm{R}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{j}f_{ij}}+\omega_{-}C^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{j}f_{ij}}\Big)G^{f_{ij}\bar{f}_{ij}}(-p_{ij},p_{ij})T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}(p_{ij}),

(280)

which is recovered from (
273
) by transposition
and
insertion of (
216
), (
264
),
and

\displaystyle C^{\sigma}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{ij^{(\prime)}}f_{j}}\to C^{\sigma}_{\bar{H}_{i}\bar{f}_{j}f_{ij^{(\prime)}}}.

(281)

In contrast to (
263
), the chiralities of the couplings
are not swapped in (
281
).
Altogether, the
f^{\ast}\to Hf
splitting functions are obtained from the

\bar{f}^{\ast}\to H\bar{f}
results via

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{f_{ij^{\prime}}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa\big)=\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{H_{i}}{}^{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa\big)\bigg|_{\eqref{eq:FHF-swap-cpl-wo-chir},\,\eqref{eq:ch-conj-transpos-effect-on-dirac-chains-equiv}}.

(282)

4.4
Computing the azimuthal average of FS splitting functions

4.4.1
General procedure

The tensor-valued structure of the splitting functions from
Sections
4.2
and
4.3

encodes spin correlations between
the matrix elements for the

(n+1)
- and
n
-particle process
in (
80
).
Via
k_{\perp}^{\mu}
, these correlations depend on the plane of branching,
and the azimuthal average around the collinear axis
corresponds to taking the spin average
of the branching particle
\varphi_{ij}

over the polarizations transverse
to the collinear axis.
This average is of interest
for experiments that are not sensitive to the
orientation of the splitting plane and significantly simplifies the
structure of the splitting functions.
Moreover, when
splitting functions are
used for the construction of subtraction algorithms,
the azimuthal average constitutes an essential step in the computation
of integrated counterterms.

Examples
for the computation of the azimuthal average
are provided
in
catani1996general
;
catani2002massivedipole
for the unpolarized
branching processes
\mathrm{g}^{\ast}\to\mathrm{g}\mathrm{g}
,

\mathrm{g}^{\ast}\to q\bar{q}
, and in
dittmaier2008polarized
for
unpolarized
\gamma^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
.
In these cases, the azimuthal average turns the respective Lorentz structures into
the transverse polarization sums
of the gluon or photon.

The precise definition of the azimuthal angle requires a choice of
frame, which we consider to be distinguished by
\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}\parallel\boldsymbol{n}
, with

\bar{p}^{\mu}
and
n^{\mu}
from the
Sudakov parametrization (
69
).
Aligning
\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}
with the 3-axis, we get the explicit
component-wise expressions for
\bar{p}^{\mu}
,
n^{\mu}
,
and
\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{\pm}
from (
195
).
Additionally, the conditions
\bar{p}k_{\perp}=nk_{\perp}=0
imply

\displaystyle k_{\perp}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=(0,|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|\cos\phi,|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|\sin\phi,0),

(283)

where
k_{\perp}^{2}=-|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|^{2}
and
\phi
defines the
azimuthal angle of the splitting plane.
Taking the average with respect to
\phi
, denoted by

\displaystyle\langle\dots\rangle_{\phi}=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\phi}{2\pi}\dots,

(284)

yields

\displaystyle\langle k_{\perp}^{\mu}\rangle_{\phi}=0.

(285)

All remaining four-vectors
\bar{p}^{\mu}
,
n^{\mu}
,
and
\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}^{\mu}
are independent of
\phi
and do not
change under
\langle\dots\rangle_{\phi}
.
The scalar product of
k_{\perp}^{\mu}
with any of these
four-vectors vanishes
upon integrating over
\phi
.
In particular,

\displaystyle\langle k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}\rangle_{\phi}=0.

(286)

Explicitly, we find, in the chosen frame of reference,

\displaystyle k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}=-s_{\pm}\frac{|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|}{\sqrt{2}}\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\phi},

(287)

so that (
192
) implies

\displaystyle k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}=-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\left(\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu\ast}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu\ast}+s_{+}s_{-}^{\ast}\mathrm{e}^{+2\mathrm{i}\phi}\,\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu\ast}+s_{-}s_{+}^{\ast}\mathrm{e}^{-2\mathrm{i}\phi}\,\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu\ast}\right)

(288)

and

\displaystyle\langle k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}\rangle_{\phi}=-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\left(\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu\ast}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu\ast}\right),

(289)

which agrees with
k_{\perp}^{2}g^{\mu\nu}/2
in leading mass order
according to (
189
).
All other outer products
k_{\perp}^{\mu}v^{\nu}
between
k_{\perp}^{\mu}
and
four-vectors
v^{\nu}=\bar{p}^{\nu},n^{\nu},\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}^{\nu}

vanish under the azimuthal integration, while the outer
products
v_{1}^{\mu}v_{2}^{\nu}
that do not involve
k_{\perp}^{\mu}

and
k_{\perp}^{\nu}
are unaffected.
This, of course, also applies to
g^{\mu\nu}
.

Combining these results, the computation of the azimuthal average of our

FS
splitting functions becomes straightforward.
For the individual splitting types, this is discussed in more detail
below.
Throughout, we perform our discussion in view of the construction of
subtraction functions for subtraction algorithms.
Specifically, this means that we are interested in the
exact

azimuthal average of our
splitting functions, including potential
mass-suppressed terms.

4.4.2
Splittings of vector bosons

We start with the splittings
V_{ij}^{\ast}\to\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}
, discussing separately the case of massless and massive
vector bosons.
In the basic formulas, we keep the general case of fields

V_{ij}
,
V_{ij^{\prime}}
. Note, however, that
in the non-diagonal case
V_{ij}\neq V_{ij^{\prime}}

the evaluation of products

\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\lambda}_{V_{ij}X}\big(\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\lambda}_{V_{ij^{\prime}}X}\big)^{*}

of matrix elements,
where
\lambda
is the polarization of
V_{ij}
and
V_{ij^{\prime}}
,
deserve particular care if
m_{ij}\neq m_{ij}^{\prime}
,
i.e. in one of the two factors some distortion of momenta
is required to obtain the relevant on-shell kinematics
(which is admitted in leading mass order).
In the diagonal case
V_{ij}=V_{ij^{\prime}}
, some of the
emerging tensor structures can be identified
with spin-summed squared matrix elements of the underlying hard
n
-particle process.

Examples: Unpolarized

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}^{\ast}\to\mathrm{g}\mathrm{g}}
,

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}^{\ast}\to q\bar{q}}
,

\boldsymbol{\gamma^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}}

.

As described in Appendices
D.1

and
D.2
, the splitting functions
from
catani1996general
;
catani2002massivedipole
;
dittmaier2008polarized

with
massless
vector bosons exhibit structures
proportional to
k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}
or
g^{\mu\nu}
.
Because of (
289
) and (
189
),
combined with (
402
) and (
407
),
both structures become proportional to the transverse polarization sum
(up to irrelevant terms
\propto\bar{p}^{\mu}
or
\propto\bar{p}^{\nu}
),
as claimed.

Massless intermediate vector bosons.

The same procedure applies to all other splittings of a massless vector
boson, resulting in

\displaystyle\bigg\langle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}\bigg\rangle_{\phi}

\displaystyle=C\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu\ast}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu\ast}\big),

\displaystyle\bigg\langle T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X,\mu}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}T^{(n)\ast}_{V_{ij^{\prime}}X,\nu}\bigg\rangle_{\phi}

\displaystyle=C\sum_{\lambda=\pm}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\lambda}_{V_{ij}X}\left(\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\lambda}_{V_{ij^{\prime}}X}\right)^{*}.

(290)

The coefficient
C
depends on the specific splitting and
polarizations.
We have

\displaystyle C_{V^{\ast}\to VV}

\displaystyle=-4k_{\perp}^{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{z^{2}}+\frac{1}{(z-1)^{2}}+1\bigg)I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast},

\displaystyle C_{V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(-\frac{2\big(m_{i}^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2}\big)}{z(z-1)}+4k_{\perp}^{2}\bigg)I_{f_{i}f_{j}}^{V_{ij}}I_{f_{i}f_{j}}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}\ast}

(291)

for entirely massless, unpolarized
V^{\ast}\to VV
splittings and
unpolarized
V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
splittings with
non-chiral fermions of mass

m_{i}
, respectively, corresponding to the above examples.

An exception arises for
V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
with

polarized fermions
or
chiral couplings
.
The corresponding splitting functions contain additional terms

\propto(\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu\ast}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu\ast})
, which remain after azimuthal averaging.
We obtain

\displaystyle\bigg\langle T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X,\mu}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}T^{(n)\ast}_{V_{ij^{\prime}}X,\nu}\bigg\rangle_{\phi}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda=\pm}C_{\lambda}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\lambda}_{V_{ij}X}\left(\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\lambda}_{V_{ij^{\prime}}X}\right)^{*},

(292)

with two coefficients
C_{\lambda}
, encoding correlations with hard matrix
elements of different polarization.
A similar situation is detailed for the azimuthal average of polarized
IS
f\to f\gamma^{\ast}
splittings in
dittmaier2008polarized
.

Massive intermediate vector bosons.

The case of
massive
vector bosons is more involved, as terms

\propto\bar{p}^{\mu}
remain and
-g^{\mu\nu}
cannot
simply be replaced by
\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu\ast}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu\ast}
in the azimuth-averaged splitting functions.
Their tensor structure
remains intricate, and the identification of spin-summed products of matrix elements
for the hard process similar to (
290
) is not directly possible.
Keeping mass-suppressed terms emerging from the averaging process,
much simpler results, however, are obtained for splitting functions emerging from
the following

modification
of the
V_{ij}^{\ast}\to\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}
results from
Sections
4.2

and
4.3
,

\displaystyle k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}\to k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}n^{\nu}+n^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}}{\bar{p}n}-\frac{\tilde{m}_{ij}^{2}n^{\mu}n^{\nu}}{(\bar{p}n)^{2}}\right),\qquad\bar{p}^{2}=\tilde{m}_{ij}^{2},

(293)

which only affects terms beyond the leading mass order.
Because the corresponding adaption of the azimuth-averaged
results amounts to

\displaystyle\langle k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}\rangle_{\phi}\to\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}g^{\mu\nu},

(294)

only
\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}
and
g^{\mu\nu}

are left after taking
\langle\dots\rangle_{\phi}
.
This allows us to write

\displaystyle\bigg\langle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}\bigg\rangle_{\phi}

\displaystyle=C_{V}\left(-g^{\mu\nu}+\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}}{m_{ij}m_{ij}^{\prime}}\right)+C_{G}\,\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{p}^{\nu}}{m_{ij}m_{ij}^{\prime}},

(295)

with suitable coefficients
C_{V}
and
C_{G}
.
For
V_{ij}=V_{ij^{\prime}}

this facilitates the identification of spin-summed squared matrix elements.
Specifically, employing (
105
) we find

\displaystyle\bigg\langle T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X,\mu}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{V_{ij}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}T^{(n)\ast}_{V_{ij}X,\nu}\bigg\rangle_{\phi}

\displaystyle=C_{V}\sum_{\lambda=\pm,\mathrm{L}}\Big|\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\lambda}_{V_{ij}X}\Big|^{2}+C_{G}\,\Big|\mathcal{M}^{(n)}_{G_{ij}X}\Big|^{2}.

(296)

In contrast to the massless case, we are left
with a sum that does not only involve
a squared,
unpolarized matrix element
for the vector boson,
but also a squared would-be Goldstone-boson matrix
element.
We note that
the specific frame in which the transverse and longitudinal
polarization vectors
are defined does not matter for the evaluation of the r.h.s. of (
296
).
This is a key property, for example, when applying splitting
functions in subtraction algorithms.
Because the latter require the exact correspondence between splitting functions
and their azimuth-averaged counterparts,
we stress that this conclusion is only reached
after
performing
the redefinition (
293
), which introduces terms beyond the leading mass order.

Once more, this does not apply to
V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
splittings with
polarized fermions or chiral
couplings, where additional structures

\propto\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu\ast}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu\ast}
remain after the integration over
\phi
.
Even with the modification (
293
), the integrated
splitting functions cannot be expressed in the
form (
296
).
Rather, separate coefficients
C_{V,\lambda}
for the longitudinal and
the two transverse polarizations are found.
A simpler result is obtained by performing, instead
of (
293
), the substitution

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\to-\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu\ast}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu\ast}\big),

(297)

which is valid in leading mass order, according to (
189
).
After averaging over
\phi
,
we find

\displaystyle\bigg\langle T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X,\mu}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}T^{(n)\ast}_{V_{ij^{\prime}}X,\nu}\bigg\rangle_{\phi}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda=\pm}C_{V,\lambda}\,\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\lambda}_{V_{ij}X}\left(\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\lambda}_{V_{ij^{\prime}}X}\right)^{*}

\displaystyle{}+C_{G}\,\mathcal{M}^{(n)}_{G_{ij}X}\left(\mathcal{M}^{(n)}_{G_{ij^{\prime}}X}\right)^{*}.

(298)

Unlike (
296
),
the result from the evaluation of this
expression
does

retain dependence on the frame used for the definition of the
polarization vectors of the
massive
vector
bosons
V_{ij}
and
V_{ij^{\prime}}
.

4.4.3
Splittings of vector-boson–scalar interferences

All terms of the splitting functions
for
V_{ij}^{\ast}/H_{ij^{\prime}}^{\ast}\to\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}
and
H_{ij}^{\ast}/V_{ij^{\prime}}^{\ast}\to\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}
are proportional to the four-vectors
\bar{p}^{\mu}
,
k_{\perp}^{\mu}
,
or
(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\mp})\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}^{\mu}
.
Only the terms
\propto\bar{p}^{\mu}

survive upon integrating over
\phi
.
With suitable coefficients
C
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\bigg\langle T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X,\mu}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{H_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu}T^{(n)\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}X}\bigg\rangle_{\phi}

\displaystyle=C\,\mathcal{M}^{(n)}_{G_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\ast}_{H_{ij^{\prime}}X}.

(299)

This result is free from gauge-boson matrix elements and therefore not
subject to issues regarding the frame-dependent definition of
polarization vectors.

4.4.4
Splittings of fermions and antifermions

All coefficients of the
structures (
246
) in the

f_{ij}^{\ast}\to\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}
and

\bar{f}_{ij}^{\ast}\to\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}
splitting functions are
azimuthal-angle independent.
Thus, the terms
\propto\not{k}_{\perp}
vanish after azimuthal
integration.
The remaining contributions can be collected into
terms
\propto\Sigma_{\kappa}(\not{\bar{p}}\pm\tilde{m}_{ij})

after performing the redefinitions

\displaystyle\not{\bar{p}}\to\not{\bar{p}}\pm\tilde{m}_{ij},\qquad\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\to\mp\gamma^{5}\not{\bar{s}}(\not{\bar{p}}\pm\tilde{m}_{ij}),

(300)

where the signs apply to fermion and antifermion splittings,
respectively.
We write
\bar{s}^{\mu}=\bar{p}^{\mu}/\tilde{m}_{ij}+r^{\mu}

with some unspecified
gauge vector
r^{\mu}
, according to (
79
).
Again, these substitutions are valid at leading mass order.
Assuming
f_{ij}=f_{ij^{\prime}}
and

m_{ij}=m_{ij}^{\prime}=\tilde{m}_{ij}
,
we get,
via (
78
),

\displaystyle\bigg\langle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{f_{ij}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j})\bigg]T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}\bigg\rangle_{\phi}

\displaystyle=C_{+}\,\Big|\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+}_{f_{ij}X}\Big|^{2}+C_{-}\,\Big|\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-}_{f_{ij}X}\Big|^{2},

(301)

with coefficients
C_{\pm}
, where
\pm
indicates the two
possible polarizations of
f_{ij}
.
A similar relation is found for
\bar{f}_{ij}^{\ast}\to\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}
.

4.5
IS splitting functions

The computation of
IS
splitting functions directly carries over
from the
FS
case, including the treatment of mass-singular terms
from longitudinal polarization states for vector bosons.
However, care w.r.t. signs and phases
has to be taken when
employing
WIs
and coupling relations.
For example, the
WI
for the underlying amplitude, with incoming
momentum
p_{ai}^{\mu}
and incoming
V_{ai}
, reads

\displaystyle T^{(n)}_{G_{ij}X}(p_{ai})=\eta_{V_{ai}}^{\ast}\frac{p_{ai}^{\mu}}{m_{ai}}T^{(n)}_{V_{ai}X,\mu}(p_{ai}).

(302)

Similar to (
116
), the quasi-collinear limit is evaluated
entirely via

\displaystyle k_{\perp}^{\mu}=\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right),\quad p_{a}p_{i}=\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right),\quad\bar{p}^{\mu}\,T^{(n)}_{V_{ai}X,\mu}(p_{ai})=\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right),\quad r^{\mu}_{a/i}=\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda\right).

(303)

Potentially remaining scalar products
\bar{p}r_{a}
and
\bar{p}r_{i}
are
eliminated via

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=p_{a}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}(p_{a})=m_{a}+\bigg(\frac{1}{x}\bar{p}-B_{a}n\bigg)r_{a}=m_{a}+\frac{1}{x}\,\big(\bar{p}r_{a}\big)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right),

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=p_{i}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}(p_{i})=m_{i}+\bigg(\frac{1-x}{x}\bar{p}-B_{i}n+k_{\perp}\bigg)r_{i}=m_{i}+\frac{1-x}{x}\,\big(\bar{p}r_{j}\big)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\right),

(304)

with
B_{a}
and
B_{i}
defined in (
70
).
As in the
FS
case, we observe that our results obey the symmetries from
Sections
4.1.1
,

4.1.2
, and charge conjugation.
Moreover, as discussed in Section
4.1.3
, all

IS
splitting functions can be recovered from
FS
counterparts
via crossing symmetry.
For this reason, we do not explicitly report our results for the

IS
case and refer to (
95
) instead.
For example, instead of deriving the
\bar{f}\to\bar{f}V^{\ast}

splitting functions from the diagrams
