(305)

the crossing formula (
95
) implies the relation

\displaystyle\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\bar{f}_{a}}{}^{V_{ai^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{i}}\bigg(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{a},\kappa_{i}\bigg)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}=-\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{\bar{V}_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{f_{a}}{}^{\bar{V}_{ai^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{i}}\bigg(\frac{1}{x},-\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{x},-\hat{p}^{\mu},-\kappa_{a},\kappa_{i}\bigg)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{\mathrm{FS}}\to m_{\mathrm{IS}}}

(306)

to the
FS
case
V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
from
Section
4.3.1
,
with the identification

\displaystyle I_{f_{i}^{\sigma}f_{j}^{\sigma}}^{V_{ij\smash{{}^{(\prime)}}}}\to I_{f_{a}^{\sigma}f_{i}^{\sigma}}^{\bar{V}_{ai\smash{{}^{(\prime)}}}}.

(307)

Here, we introduced

\displaystyle\hat{p}^{\mu}\equiv\bar{p}^{\mu}-k_{\perp}^{\mu},

(308)

which considerably simplifies
the results and makes both the
symmetry rule (
90
) and the crossing
relation (
95
) more obvious.
19
19

19

This is also beneficial in view of the leading-mass-order expression
of the
WI
(
302
),

\displaystyle p_{ai}^{\mu}\,T^{(n)}_{V_{ai}X,\mu}(p_{ai})=\left(\bar{p}-k_{\perp}\right)^{\mu}T^{(n)}_{V_{ai}X,\mu}(p_{ai})+\mathcal{O}\left(m^{2}\right).

(309)

The crossing symmetry (
95
) suggests the
following definition of generic abbreviations for
IS
splittings
with two (anti-)fermions and a third field
\varrho
,

\displaystyle\bar{a}^{\varrho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=\bar{\zeta}_{\mathcal{I}}m_{\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{1}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}\pm\bar{\zeta}_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}m_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{2}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}},

\displaystyle\bar{b}^{\varrho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=m_{\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{1}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}\pm m_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{2}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}},

\displaystyle\bar{c}^{\varrho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{\mathcal{X}\mathcal{I}\bar{\mathcal{I}}}

\displaystyle=-\big(2\bar{\zeta}_{\mathcal{X}}m_{\mathcal{X}}^{2}+\bar{\zeta}_{\mathcal{I}}m_{\mathcal{I}}^{2}+\bar{\zeta}_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}m_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}^{2}\big)\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{1}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}+m_{\mathcal{I}}m_{\bar{\zeta}_{\mathcal{I}}}\big(\bar{\zeta}_{\mathcal{I}}+\bar{\zeta}_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}\big)\mathcal{C}^{\sigma_{2}}_{\tilde{\varrho}_{\mathcal{X}}\bar{f}_{\mathcal{I}}f_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}},

(310)

where
\mathcal{I},\bar{\mathcal{I}},\mathcal{X}\in\{a,i,ai^{(\prime)}\}
denote the indices of the outgoing fermion,
outgoing antifermion, and
\varrho
, respectively, in the
processes
\varphi_{a}\to\varphi_{i}[\varphi_{ai}^{\ast}/\varphi_{ai^{\prime}}^{\ast}]
.
We write
\tilde{\varrho}_{a}=\varrho_{a}
,

\tilde{\varrho}_{ai^{(\prime)}}=\bar{\varrho}_{ai^{(\prime)}}
,
and
\tilde{\varrho}_{i}=\bar{\varrho}_{i}
.
These abbreviations are obtained from the
FS
counterparts (
196
) simply by replacing the
symbols
\zeta
with
\bar{\zeta}
, defined as

\displaystyle\bar{\zeta}_{a}=\frac{x-1}{x},\qquad\bar{\zeta}_{i}=\frac{1}{x},\qquad\bar{\zeta}_{ai}=\bar{\zeta}_{ai^{\prime}}=-\frac{x-1}{x^{2}}.

(311)

Similar to the identity (
199
) for
\zeta
, this variable obeys

\displaystyle\bar{\zeta}_{\mathcal{X}}=-\frac{\bar{\zeta}_{\mathcal{I}}\bar{\zeta}_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}{\bar{\zeta}_{\mathcal{I}}+\bar{\zeta}_{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}}.

(312)

Specifically, the
IS
abbreviations are constructed such that they
are related to the
FS
counterparts by crossing.
For example, the
IS
abbreviations for
\bar{f}\to\bar{f}V^{\ast}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\bar{a}^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ai,a,i}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=\frac{x-1}{x}m_{a}I_{f_{a}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}}\pm\frac{1}{x}m_{i}I_{f_{a}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}},

\displaystyle\bar{b}^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ai,a,i}(\pm 1)

\displaystyle=m_{a}I_{f_{a}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}}\pm m_{i}I_{f_{a}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}},

\displaystyle\bar{c}^{V\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}_{ai,a,i}

\displaystyle=\bigg(2\frac{x-1}{x^{2}}m_{ai}^{2}-\frac{x-1}{x}m_{a}^{2}-\frac{1}{x}m_{i}^{2}\bigg)I_{f_{a}^{\sigma_{1}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{1}}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}}+m_{a}m_{i}I_{f_{a}^{\sigma_{2}}f_{i}^{\sigma_{2}}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}},

(313)

are obtained from the corresponding
FS

V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}

symbols (
200
) via the combination of the mappings

m_{a}\leftrightarrow m_{i}
,
x\to 1/x
,
and (
307
) from the crossing
rule (
306
).
Thus, when recovering
IS

\bar{f}\to\bar{f}V^{\ast}
splitting
functions from
FS

V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
results, the associated
abbreviation symbols (
200
) simply need to be
exchanged with those from (
313
).

To make the above example even more explicit, we consider the special case of

IS

\bar{f}\to\bar{f}V^{\ast}
splittings with
massless vector bosons
V_{ai}
,
V_{ai^{\prime}}
.
The corresponding
FS

V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
splitting functions can
be found in (
222
).
Multiplication of these results with
-1
and application of

\displaystyle z\to\frac{1}{x},\quad k_{\perp}^{\mu}\to\frac{-k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{x},\quad\bar{p}^{\mu}\to-\bar{p}^{\mu}+k_{\perp}^{\mu},\quad\kappa_{i}\to-\kappa_{a},\quad\kappa_{j}\to\kappa_{i},

\displaystyle m_{i}\to m_{a},\quad m_{j}\to m_{i},\quad I_{f_{i}^{\sigma}f_{j}^{\sigma}}^{V_{ij\smash{{}^{(\prime)}}}}\to I_{f_{a}^{\sigma}f_{i}^{\sigma}}^{\bar{V}_{ai\smash{{}^{(\prime)}}}}

(314)

leads us to the
IS

\bar{f}\to\bar{f}V^{\ast}
splitting functions

\displaystyle\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\bar{f}_{a}}{}^{V_{ai^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mp,\mp\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{ai}^{(\prime)}=0}

\displaystyle=I_{f_{a}^{\mathrm{R/L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R/L}}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}}\Big(I_{f_{a}^{\mathrm{R/L}}f_{i}^{\mathrm{R/L}}}^{\bar{V}_{ai^{\prime}}}\Big)^{\ast}\begin{aligned} \bigg\{&\frac{-k_{\perp}^{2}}{x-1}g^{\mu\nu}+\frac{4}{x^{2}}k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}\\
&{}\pm s_{\varepsilon}\frac{2-x}{(x-1)x}k_{\perp}^{2}\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}\big)\bigg\},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ai^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\bar{f}_{a}}{}^{V_{ai^{\prime}}}_{\bar{f}_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mp,\pm\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}_{m_{ai}^{(\prime)}=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{2}\bar{a}^{V\,\mathrm{LR/RL}}_{ai,a,i}(+1)\bar{a}^{V\,\mathrm{LR/RL}}_{ai^{\prime},a,i}(+1)}{x-1}\bigg\{g^{\mu\nu}\mp s_{\varepsilon}\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}\big)\bigg\}.

(315)

4.6
Computing the azimuthal average of IS splitting functions

The azimuthal average of
IS
splitting functions
(w.r.t. the kinematics defined by the
IS
version (
69
) of the Sudakov parametrization)
may be evaluated
similarly to the
FS
case discussed in
Section
4.4
.
Once more defining the azimuthal angle
\phi
of the transverse momentum

k_{\perp}^{\mu}
in the frame distinguished by
\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}\parallel\boldsymbol{n}
(where
k_{\perp}^{\mu}
,
\bar{p}^{\mu}
, and

n^{\mu}
are now taken from (
69
)), we get
the form (
283
) of
k_{\perp}^{\mu}
.
Again, this allows for the straightforward computation of
the average
\langle\dots\rangle_{\phi}

of the
IS
splitting functions by application of the
rules (
285
) and (
289
).
With this at hand, the functions for the various splittings of vector
bosons, vector-boson–scalar interferences, and (anti-)fermions may be
treated similarly to the discussion from
Sections
4.4.2
–
4.4.4
.

5
Comparison with existing results

In this section, we briefly review the existing
literature
Chen:2016wkt
;
Cuomo:2019siu
;
Kleiss:2020rcg
;
Brooks:2021kji
;
Masouminia:2021kne

on electroweak quasi-collinear factorization
and comment on the compatibility with our results.
As already mentioned, these references derive factorization formulas at
the level of individual amplitudes, in contrast to our approach.
This considerably complicates the comparison with our findings.
Specifically, the partial matrix
elements (
75
) are written as
products of matrix elements for the underlying hard process and

splitting amplitudes
for
1\to 2
splitting processes.
Both
are evaluated with on-shell polarization vectors and spinors,
while the incoming momentum of the splitting amplitudes is off-shell.
This procedure relies on the non-trivial, well-defined decomposition of

off-shell
propagator numerators into

on-shell
polarization sums of outer products of wave functions.
These steps yield remainder terms that must be identified as
subleading in the quasi-collinear limit.
The splitting amplitudes are evaluated either in explicit,
component-wise, and frame-dependent parametrizations of momenta,
spinors, and polarization
vectors
Chen:2016wkt
;
Cuomo:2019siu
;
Masouminia:2021kne
, or using
spinor–helicity methods
Kleiss:2020rcg
;
Brooks:2021kji
.
The issues regarding mass-singular factors in longitudinal polarization
vectors are dealt with in different ways, as detailed in the following.
Throughout this section, we focus on
FS
splittings.
The
IS
case can be treated analogously.

5.1
Comparison to Cuomo, Vecchi, and Wulzer
Cuomo:2019siu

Details of the approach.

The approach of
Cuomo:2019siu
is based on the redefinition of
longitudinal polarization vectors with the help of
WIs
.
While this procedure is defined for a general spontaneously broken gauge
theory with arbitrarily many gauge bosons and scalars,
we review it in the simpler setting with only one vector-boson
field
V^{\mu}
and its associated would-be Goldstone boson field
G
.
As starting point, their fields are collected into a common
5-dimensional vector
\Theta^{M}=(V^{\mu},G)
, where the index runs over the
four Lorentz components
M=\mu
and a scalar component
M=\mathrm{G}
.
In this notation, the
WI
(
45
) can be written
as
20
20

20

Here and in the following, we adapt the formulas
from
Cuomo:2019siu
to our conventions and notation.
This concerns, for example, the phases of would-be Goldstone bosons.

\displaystyle\Big\langle T\prod_{i=0}^{m}\Big[\mathcal{K}_{M_{i}}(p_{i})\underline{\Theta^{M_{i}}}(p_{i})\Big]\prod_{j=1}^{n}\varphi_{j}^{\mathrm{phys}}\Big\rangle_{\mathrm{c}}=0.

(316)

At tree level, the vector and scalar components of
\mathcal{K}
read

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{0}_{\mu}(p)=\mathrm{i}\frac{p_{\mu}}{\sqrt{p^{2}}},\qquad\mathcal{K}^{0}_{\mathrm{G}}(p)=-\mathrm{i}\eta_{V}\frac{M_{V}}{\sqrt{p^{2}}}.

(317)

The standard polarization vectors are extended into 5-dimensional
notation via

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\mathrm{st}}_{\lambda,M}(p)=\big(\varepsilon^{\mathrm{st}}_{\lambda,\mu}(p),0\big).

(318)

Next, longitudinal polarization vectors for a general
off-shell

momentum
p^{\mu}
are redefined.
21
21

21

In the approach of
Cuomo:2019siu
, off-shell longitudinal
polarization vectors, obtained from (
61
) by
replacing
M\to\sqrt{p^{2}}
, are required for the decomposition of
propagators.
See below.

For incoming and outgoing vector bosons
V
, respectively,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L},M}(p)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{L},M}(p)+\mathrm{i}\mathcal{K}_{M}(+p)

\displaystyle=\bigg(r_{\mu}(p),+\eta_{V}\frac{M_{V}}{\sqrt{p^{2}}}\bigg),

\displaystyle\overline{\varepsilon}_{\mathrm{L},M}(p)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{\mathrm{st}\,\ast}_{\mathrm{L},M}(p)-\mathrm{i}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{M}(-p)

\displaystyle=\bigg(r_{\mu}(p),-\eta_{\bar{V}}\frac{M_{V}}{\sqrt{p^{2}}}\bigg),

(319)

where
\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{M}(-p)
refers to an incoming field
\bar{V}
(= outgoing
V
).
Here, the problematic terms
p^{\mu}/\sqrt{p^{2}}
are canceled
between
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{L}}
and
\mathcal{K}
,
such that
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}
and
\overline{\varepsilon}_{\mathrm{L}}
are well behaved.
The computation of amplitudes with redefined longitudinal polarization
vectors
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}
now involves would-be Goldstone
amplitudes, too.
Due to the
WI
(
316
),
the result agrees with the amplitude
for
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{L}}
.
For incoming vector bosons, this reads

\displaystyle\prod_{i=0}^{m}\varepsilon^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathrm{L},\mu_{i}}(p_{i})\Big\langle T\prod_{i=0}^{m}\underline{V^{\mu_{i}}}(p_{i})\prod_{j=1}^{n}\varphi_{j}^{\mathrm{phys}}\Big\rangle=\prod_{i=0}^{m}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L},M_{i}}(p_{i})\Big\langle T\prod_{i=0}^{m}\underline{\Theta^{M_{i}}_{\vphantom{a_{i}}}}(p_{i})\prod_{j=1}^{n}\varphi_{j}^{\mathrm{phys}}\Big\rangle.

(320)

The r.h.s. is now manifestly free of mass-singular factors.
Because this statement is equivalent to
the exact version (
63
) of the
GBET
, the
redefinition (
319
) is analogous to our procedure
from (
101
)
and (
103
).

The new
propagators for gauge bosons require special care.
First, they are written as
5\times 5
matrices
G_{MN}
.
Then, for the off-shell particle that undergoes the splitting
an
equivalent
propagator
G^{\mathrm{eq}}_{MN}
is
defined, which is manifestly free of mass-singular factors.
At tree level,

\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{eq},0}_{MN}(p,-p)=\frac{1}{p^{2}-M_{V}^{2}}\sum_{\lambda=\pm,\mathrm{L}}\varepsilon^{\lambda}_{M}(p)\overline{\varepsilon}^{\lambda}_{N}(p)+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S},M}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S},M},\qquad\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S},M}=(0,0,0,0,1).

(321)

Similar to the redefinition of polarization vectors, the

WI
(
316
) implies that computing the
diagrams (
67
) with equivalent and
standard propagators produces the same result.
Finally, the polarization vectors in (
321
) are replaced
by counterparts that are evaluated on the on-shell-projected momentum

p^{\mu}_{\mathrm{on}}
, which is obtained from
p^{\mu}
via modification
of the component
p^{0}
.
This absorbs the second, scalar part of (
321
)
into the
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{L}}
part of the polarization sum, up to
contributions that are irrelevant in the quasi-collinear limit.
This completes the decomposition of vector-boson propagators into outer
products of (redefined) polarization vectors.

Putting everything together, quasi-collinear factorization of
(
75
) is expressed as

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\varphi_{ij})}\Big]_{\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathop{\resizebox{1423121}{0}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\textstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1423121}{0}{\hbox{$\textstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1256560}{0}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1212868}{0}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}}\sum_{\kappa_{ij}}\frac{1}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\,\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\kappa_{ij}}_{\varphi_{ij}X}(p_{ij}^{\mathrm{on}})\,\mathcal{M}^{(\mathrm{split})\,\kappa_{ij}\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}_{\varphi_{ij}\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}(p_{ij},p_{i},p_{j}),

(322)

where the hard matrix elements

\mathcal{M}^{(n)}

are evaluated with on-shell-projected momentum and wave functions.
Here, standard polarization vectors may be used.
The splitting amplitudes
\mathcal{M}^{(\mathrm{split})}
for the
processes
\varphi_{ij}^{\ast}\to\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}
are
evaluated with the
off-shell
momentum
p_{ij}^{\mu}=p_{i}^{\mu}+p_{j}^{\mu}
, but with on-shell wave functions for the intermediate
particles.
Here, modified longitudinal polarization
vectors (
319
) need to be used.
Similar to our approach, contributions for intermediate would-be
Goldstone bosons are not explicitly considered, as they are
already fully taken into account in the definition of the equivalent
propagator.
However, the computation of
\mathcal{M}^{(\mathrm{split})}

does

require the evaluation of would-be Goldstone-boson amplitudes, due
to (
319
).
To this end, the employed Feynman rules are parametrized in such a way
that the coupling relations from Section
2.6

are manifest.

The splitting matrix elements are finally evaluated at leading order in
the quasi-collinear limit, employing explicit, component-wise
parametrizations of the splitting
3-momenta (cf. (5.16) of
Cuomo:2019siu
),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{p}_{ij}

\displaystyle=(0,0,|\boldsymbol{p}_{ij}|),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{p}_{i}

\displaystyle=(|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|\cos\phi,|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|\sin\phi,(1-x^{\prime})|\boldsymbol{p}_{ij}|),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{p}_{j}

\displaystyle=(-|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|\cos\phi,-|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|\sin\phi,x^{\prime}|\boldsymbol{p}_{ij}|),

(323)

polarization vectors (with
s_{\varepsilon}=-1
), and spinors.
The results are reported in Appendix B of
Cuomo:2019siu
.
These formulas, however, appear to contain some typographical errors.
First, the denominator in the expressions for
V_{0}^{a}\to V_{\pm 1}^{b}V_{\mp 1}^{c}
should be
m_{a}
instead of
m_{1}
.
In the numerator,
m_{c}^{3}
should be replaced by
m_{c}^{2}
.
Likewise, in the numerator for
V_{0}^{a}\to V_{\pm 1}^{b}V_{0}^{c}
, the
exponent of
m_{a}
should be replaced by
2
.

Compatibility of results.

Matching the Sudakov
decomposition (
69
) to the explicit
parametrization (
323
),
our quasi-collinear factorization formula (
80
)
agrees with the
counterpart of factorization (
322
) in
Cuomo:2019siu

(up to a trivial factor
e^{2}
).
For this, we set

\displaystyle n^{\mu}

\displaystyle=(1,0,0,-1)

(324)

and get,
via
\bar{p}n=p_{ij}n
, that

\displaystyle z=(p_{i}n)/(\bar{p}n)=(1-x^{\prime})+\mathcal{O}\left(m^{2}\right).

(325)

After identifying
k_{\perp}^{2}=-|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|^{2}

from (
72
), the coefficients
A_{i}
,

A_{j}
are expressed completely in terms of the parameters
from (
323
), giving us an explicit expression for

\displaystyle\bar{p}^{\mu}=p_{ij}^{\mu}+(A_{i}+A_{j})n^{\mu}.

(326)

Furthermore, with
k_{\perp}^{\mu}=p_{i}^{\mu}-z\bar{p}^{\mu}+A_{i}n^{\mu}
, we obtain

\displaystyle k_{\perp}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=(0,|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|\cos\phi,|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|\sin\phi,0).

(327)

Fixing
s_{\pm}=\mp 1
in (
195
),
the specific choice of
n^{\mu}
corresponds to

\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}=-(0,1,\mathrm{i},0)/\sqrt{2}

and

\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}=(0,1,-\mathrm{i},0)/\sqrt{2}
.
Thus,
the parameters
|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|
and phases
\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\phi}
can be computed via the scalar products between
k_{\perp}^{\mu}

and
\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}^{\mu}
,

\displaystyle k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}=\pm\frac{|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|}{\sqrt{2}}\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\phi},\qquad|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|^{2}=-k_{\perp}^{2}=-2\big(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}\big)\big(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}\big).

(328)

The relation between our splitting functions from
Sections
4.2
and
4.3

and the splitting matrix elements
from
Cuomo:2019siu
is now found by
plugging (
322
)
into (
74
) and comparing
with (
80
).
This yields

\displaystyle e^{2}\left[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{V_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\right]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda_{ij},\lambda_{ij}^{\prime}\in\{\pm,\mathrm{L}\}}\varepsilon^{\ast\mu}_{\lambda_{ij}}(p_{ij}^{\mathrm{on}})\varepsilon^{\nu}_{\lambda_{ij}^{\prime}}(p_{ij}^{\mathrm{on}\prime})\,\mathcal{M}^{(\mathrm{split})\,\lambda_{ij}\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}_{V_{ij}\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(\mathrm{split})\,\lambda_{ij}^{\prime}\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}_{V_{ij^{\prime}}\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}\Big]^{\ast}

(329)

for two intermediate vector bosons
and

\displaystyle e^{2}\,\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij^{\vphantom{\prime}}}}_{\varphi_{i}}{}^{f_{ij^{\prime}}}_{\varphi_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\kappa_{ij},\kappa_{ij}^{\prime}\in\{\pm\}}u_{\kappa_{ij}^{\prime}}(p_{ij}^{\mathrm{on}\prime})\bar{u}_{\kappa_{ij}}(p_{ij}^{\mathrm{on}})\,\mathcal{M}^{(\mathrm{split})\,\kappa_{ij}\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}_{f_{ij}\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(\mathrm{split})\,\kappa_{ij}^{\prime}\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}\Big]^{\ast}

(330)

for two intermediate fermions.
For antifermions, we need to exchange
u\to\bar{v}
and
\bar{u}\to v
.
The situations for intermediate scalars and vector–scalar interference
contributions are obvious.
Here,
p_{ij}^{\mathrm{on}(\prime)}
represents the on-shell projection
of
p_{ij}
w.r.t. the mass
m_{ij}^{(\prime)}
.
In (
329
), we use
standard

polarization vectors, because they belong to the hard process.

To align the parametrizations of the r.h.s. with the l.h.s., we
express
x^{\prime}
through
z
, and rewrite
|\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}|
and

\mathrm{e}^{\pm\mathrm{i}\phi}
in terms of the scalar products
k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}
, as discussed above.
Because the mass parameters
m_{a},m_{b},m_{c}
in the splitting amplitudes
of
Cuomo:2019siu
are not consistently assigned to the labels of
the momenta in (
323
), they must be
appropriately re-mapped to
m_{ij}^{(\prime)},m_{i},m_{j}
.
Moreover, the outer products of polarization vectors from the r.h.s. need to be expressed in the basis of the tensor structures used in
Sections
4.2
and
4.3
.
We start with the case of two intermediate vector bosons.
Vector–scalar interference contributions are treated similarly.
First, we employ
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}^{\mu}\big(p_{ij}^{\mathrm{on}(\prime)}\big)=\bar{p}^{\mu}/m_{ij}^{(\prime)}+\mathcal{O}\left(m\right)
.
Second, the choice of
n^{\mu}
implies that the arguments of the
transverse polarization vectors
\varepsilon_{\pm}^{\nu}\big(p_{ij}^{\mathrm{on}(\prime)}\big)
can be replaced by
\bar{p}
.
This allows us to use (
192
), with

s_{\varepsilon}=-1
, to reduce

\bar{p}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}
,

\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{+}
,

\bar{p}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{-}

into at most two terms.
Third, the four structures

\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{\pm}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{\pm}
,

\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{\pm}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{\mp}

are addressed by inserting

\displaystyle\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{-}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{-}=

\displaystyle{}-\left(\frac{k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}}{k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}}\right)^{2}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{+}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{+}-\frac{k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}}{k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}}\left(\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{+}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{-}+\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{+}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{-}\right)+\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}}{\big(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}\big)^{2}},

(331)

which simply follows from (
192
) as well.
We observe that this also eliminates

\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{+}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{+}
, leaving us with

\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{\pm}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{\mp}

and

k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}
.
Last, via

\displaystyle a\,\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{+}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{-}+b\,\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{-}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{+}=\frac{a+b}{2}\big(g^{\mu\nu}+\mathcal{O}\left(m\right)\big)+\frac{a-b}{2}\big(\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{+}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{-}-\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{-}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\nu}_{+}\big),

(332)

we identify the metric tensor
g^{\mu\nu}
.

For intermediate fermions or antifermions, on the other hand, we
use (
438
) to write

\displaystyle u_{\pm}(\bar{p})\bar{u}_{\pm}(\bar{p})

\displaystyle=\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{\mp}+\mathcal{O}\left(m\right),

\displaystyle v_{\pm}(\bar{p})\bar{v}_{\pm}(\bar{p})

\displaystyle=\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{\pm}+\mathcal{O}\left(m\right),

(333)

and identify, similar to (
443
)
and (
444
), the corresponding
structures
\propto\not{k}_{\perp}
,

\displaystyle(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\mp})\,u_{\pm}(\bar{p})\bar{u}_{\mp}(\bar{p})

\displaystyle=\mp\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{\pm}+\mathcal{O}\left(m\right),

\displaystyle(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm})\,v_{\pm}(\bar{p})\bar{v}_{\mp}(\bar{p})

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{\mp}+\mathcal{O}\left(m\right).

(334)

5.2
Comparison to Chen, Han, and Tweedie
Chen:2016wkt

Details of the approach.

In
Chen:2016wkt
, mass-singular factors from longitudinal
polarization vectors
are circumvented by the construction of an
appropriate axial gauge, dubbed
Goldstone Equivalence Gauge (GEG)
.
It is defined via the momentum-space gauge-fixing Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{fix}}^{\mathrm{GEG}}=-\frac{1}{2\xi}\sum_{a}\left[n_{\mu}(p)V^{\mu}_{a}(p)\right]\left[n_{\nu}(p)V^{\nu}_{a}(-p)\right],

(335)

in the limit
\xi\to 0
.
Here, the sum is understood to run over the vector-boson fields
V_{a}
in
the real, symmetric basis.
With the choices
n^{\mu}(p)=(1,-\mathrm{sign}(p^{0})\hat{\boldsymbol{p}})
,
\bar{n}^{\mu}(p)=\left(1,\mathrm{sign}(p^{0})\hat{\boldsymbol{p}}\right)

and the definitions

\varepsilon_{n}^{\mu}\propto n^{\mu}
,
\varepsilon_{\bar{n}}^{\mu}\propto\bar{n}^{\mu}
,
the vector-boson fields are parametrized as

\displaystyle V^{\mu}(p)=V_{+}(p)\varepsilon_{+}^{\mu}(p)+V_{-}(p)\varepsilon_{-}^{\mu}(p)+V_{n}(p)\varepsilon_{n}^{\mu}(p)+V_{\bar{n}}(p)\varepsilon_{\bar{n}}^{\mu}(p).

(336)

The mass-singular components of the longitudinal polarizations are tied
to the
\bar{n}
polarizations.
Sending
\xi\to 0
endows
V_{\bar{n}}
with an infinite mass, so that it
ceases to propagate.
Because the would-be Goldstone-boson fields
G
are not involved in the
gauge-fixing Lagrangian (
335
), the propagators
retain tree-level mixing,

\displaystyle G^{V_{\pm}V_{\pm}}_{0}(p,-p)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{p^{2}-M_{V}^{2}},

\displaystyle\qquad G^{V_{n}V_{n}}_{0}(p,-p)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{p^{2}-M_{V}^{2}}\mathrm{sign}(p^{2}),

\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{G}}_{0}(p,-p)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{p^{2}-M_{V}^{2}},

\displaystyle G^{V_{n}G}_{0}(p,-p)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{p^{2}-M_{V}^{2}}\frac{M_{V}}{\sqrt{|p^{2}|}}.

(337)

These contributions share a common pole at
p^{2}=M_{V}^{2}
.
To compute a matrix element with an external longitudinal vector boson
V_{\mathrm{L}}
,
the corresponding matrix elements for
V_{n}
and
G
have to
be combined.
With the phase choices from
Chen:2016wkt
, this reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{V_{\mathrm{L}}X}=\mathcal{M}_{V_{n}X}+\mathcal{M}_{GX},

(338)

where the r.h.s. is manifestly free of mass-singular contributions.

In diagrams like (
67
), all propagator
terms from (
337
) need to be taken into account.
Adapting the arguments from
Chen:2016wkt
to our notation, this
means

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}})}\Big]_{\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathop{\resizebox{1423121}{0}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\textstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1423121}{0}{\hbox{$\textstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1256560}{0}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1212868}{0}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}}\,\sum_{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}\in\{V_{ij}^{n},G_{ij}\}}\,-\mathrm{i}G^{\varphi\varphi^{\prime}}_{0}(p_{ij},-p_{ij})\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{\varphi X}(p_{ij})\,\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(\mathrm{split})\,\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}_{\varphi^{\prime}\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}(p_{ij},p_{i},p_{j}),

(339)

where the subscript of
\mathcal{M}
indicates that we omit the
contributions from transverse
V
.
The momentum
p_{ij}^{\mu}
is off shell, and the hats denote that

\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}
and
\hat{\mathcal{M}}^{(\mathrm{split})}
are
evaluated with the corresponding off-shell polarization vectors.
We also absorbed the polarization label for
ij
into
\varphi_{ij}
.
Finally, at leading mass order, replacing the off-shell polarization
vectors with on-shell counterparts cancels the factors

M_{V}/\sqrt{|p^{2}|}
from the mixed propagators
in (
337
), leading to

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}})}\Big]_{\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}X}^{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathop{\resizebox{1423121}{0}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\textstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1423121}{0}{\hbox{$\textstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1256560}{0}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\resizebox{1212868}{0}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}\limits_{\lambda\to 0}}}\frac{1}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{M}^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij}^{\mathrm{on}})+\mathcal{M}^{(n)}_{G_{ij}X}(p_{ij}^{\mathrm{on}})\right]

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{M}^{(\mathrm{split})\,\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}_{V_{ij}\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}(p_{ij},p_{i},p_{j})+\mathcal{M}^{(\mathrm{split})\,\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}_{G_{ij}\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}(p_{ij},p_{i},p_{j})\right].

(340)

Together with the transverse contributions, this provides the
factorization of (
75
) in terms of hard matrix elements
and splitting amplitudes, similar to (
322
).
The latter are evaluated at leading order in the quasi-collinear limit,
using explicit parametrizations of momenta and wave functions.
The Feynman rules for 3-point vertices in
GEG
essentially
agree with those from
R_{\xi}
gauges, with the exception
that (
338
) is used whenever external
longitudinal polarization vectors occur.
Thereby, the practical computation of splitting matrix elements
from
Cuomo:2019siu
and
Chen:2016wkt
essentially agrees.
However, the results of
Chen:2016wkt
are reported in the form
of splitting functions, which correspond to the product of two splitting
matrix elements,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(\mathrm{split})\,\kappa_{ij}\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}_{\varphi_{ij}\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(\mathrm{split})\,\kappa_{ij}^{\prime}\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}_{\varphi_{ij^{\prime}}\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}\Big]^{\ast}.

(341)

Flavor and spin interference effects regarding the mother particle are
not always considered, with the exception of the pairs

\gamma/\mathrm{Z}_{\mathrm{T}}
and
\mathrm{H}/\mathrm{Z}_{\mathrm{L}}
.

In
Chen:2016wkt

the splitting processes are organized into two distinct classes,
labeled
conventional
and
ultra-collinear
.
Conventional splitting functions are non-zero in the unbroken phase of
the
EWSM
, with
v=0
and massless particles.
They are provided in Tables 1–3 of
Chen:2016wkt
and do not
receive corrections in the broken phase of the
EWSM
.
Conventional splittings that involve scalars directly correspond to
counterparts with longitudinal vector bosons.
For non-conventional processes, ultra-collinear splitting
functions emerge in the broken phase of the
EWSM
.
They are proportional to
v^{2}
and quoted in Tables 4–6
of
Chen:2016wkt
.
22
22

22

Ultra-collinear splittings are identified systematically by
considering all effects of
v\neq 0
.
First, fermion spinors of helicity
\pm\frac{1}{2}
receive
left/right-chiral components proportional to the fermion mass.
Second, the on-shell polarization vector
\varepsilon_{n}^{\mu}
is
proportional to the vector-boson mass.
Third, replacing a Higgs field in quartic Lagrangian terms by
v

yields trilinear couplings that have no correspondence in the unbroken
phase.
For dimensional reasons, the factor
v^{2}
is accompanied by an
uncanceled propagator factor
1/\big(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}\big)
.
This
provides the ultra-collinear splitting functions with a more
pronounced scaling in the collinear regime, which is the reason for their name.

The combination of conventional and ultra-collinear results
provides the complete set of broken-phase splitting functions.

Compatibility of results.

Because not all interference contributions (
341
)
are quoted, a direct comparison to our results, e.g. via (
329
), is not feasible.
The splitting functions from
Chen:2016wkt
can, however, be
matched to products of the splitting matrix elements from
Cuomo:2019siu
.
We performed this check explicitly for all polarization configurations
in
V^{\ast}\to VV
and
V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
,
finding agreement.

5.3
Comparison to Kleiss, Verheyen, Brooks, and Skands
Kleiss:2020rcg
;
Brooks:2021kji

Details of the approach.

The computation of splitting amplitudes is performed using the
spinor–helicity method of
Kleiss:1985yh
.
For this, massive spinors
u_{\kappa}(p)
,
v_{\kappa}(p)
and transverse
polarization vectors
\varepsilon_{\pm}^{\mu}(p)
are expressed in terms of
massless spinors
u_{\kappa}(n)

with light-like reference vectors
n^{\mu}(p)=(1,-\hat{\boldsymbol{p}})
.
This expresses the splitting matrix elements in terms of ordinary scalar
products between four-vectors and structures like

\displaystyle S_{\kappa}(n_{a},p_{i},p_{j},\dots,n_{b})=\bar{u}_{\kappa}(n_{a})\not{p}_{i}\not{p}_{j}\dots u_{\pm\kappa}(n_{b}),

(342)

where
n_{a}
and
n_{b}
are light-like.
The mass-singular part
p^{\mu}/M_{V}
of the longitudinal polarization
vector
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}^{\mu}(p)
is treated similarly to our
procedure described in
Appendix
C
.
Specifically, this term generates contributions proportional to the
propagator denominator of the diagrams in (
75
).
These components are manually removed from the splitting amplitudes.
In practice, this is realized via the replacements

\displaystyle 2p_{i}p_{j}

\displaystyle\to m_{ij}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}-m_{j}^{2},

\displaystyle 2p_{ij}p_{i}

\displaystyle\to m_{ij}^{2}+m_{i}^{2}-m_{j}^{2},

\displaystyle 2p_{ij}p_{j}

\displaystyle\to m_{ij}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}+m_{j}^{2}.

(343)

For example, in
FS

f^{\ast}\to fV
splittings, this proceeds via

\displaystyle\frac{\not{p}_{i}\not{p}_{j}\not{p}_{ij}}{m_{j}}=\frac{1}{m_{j}}\left(p_{ij}^{2}\not{p}_{i}-m_{i}^{2}\not{p}_{ij}\right)\to\frac{1}{m_{j}}\left(m_{ij}^{2}\not{p}_{i}-m_{i}^{2}\not{p}_{ij}\right).

(344)

Taking all reference vectors collinear, only one non-trivial spinor
product remains.
At leading order in the quasi-collinear limit, it becomes, up to a phase,

\displaystyle S_{-\kappa}(n,p_{i},p_{j},n)\propto 2(p_{ij}n)\sqrt{z(1-z)}\sqrt{\tilde{Q}^{2}},\qquad\tilde{Q}^{2}=(p_{i}+p_{j})^{2}-\frac{m_{j}^{2}}{1-z}-\frac{m_{i}^{2}}{z},

(345)

where
z
is defined as the energy fraction of
p_{ij}^{\mu}
carried by

p_{i}^{\mu}
.
The results for the splitting amplitudes are quoted in Table 1–7
of
Kleiss:2020rcg
, where the phase factors
from (
345
) are neglected.
In contrast to
Chen:2016wkt
;
Cuomo:2019siu
;
Masouminia:2021kne
, no
component-wise parametrizations of momenta, spinors, and polarization
vectors are used.
On the other hand, no arguments for the decomposition of internal
propagators into products of on-shell wave functions are
presented.
The results from
Kleiss:2020rcg
have been restated
in
Brooks:2021kji
in squared form, using antenna
collinear momentum fractions
x_{i}
and
x_{j}
.

Compatibility of results.

The splitting matrix elements can directly be compared to the
corresponding expressions from
Cuomo:2019siu
.
We performed this check explicitly for all polarization configurations
in
V^{\ast}\to VV
and
V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}
.
Up to the neglected phase factors, the results agree, apart from an
apparent typographical mistake in the fifth row of Table 2.
Here, the sign in front of
m_{j}/m_{ij}
should be
-
instead of
+
.
In
Kleiss:2020rcg
, agreement with the results
from
Chen:2016wkt
is reported.
Due to the missing phase factors, however, the results
from
Kleiss:2020rcg
cannot be used directly to reconstruct our
factorization formulas from this paper.

5.4
Comparison to Masouminia and Richardson (Herwig 7)
Masouminia:2021kne

Details of the approach.

The splitting amplitudes from Tables 1–4 of
Masouminia:2021kne

are evaluated in explicit parametrizations
of momenta, spinors, and polarization vectors, at leading order in the
quasi-collinear limit.
The results are also combined into splitting functions, but
spin interference contributions are neglected.
The factorization of (
75
) into hard amplitudes and
splitting matrix elements is not explicitly proven.

The mass-singular term
p^{\mu}/m
of the longitudinal polarization
vector
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}^{\mu}(p)
is treated by simply subtracting
it.
This produces redefined polarization
vectors
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}^{\ast}}^{\mu}(p)
.
Longitudinal splitting matrix elements are then computed
with
\varepsilon^{\mu}_{\mathrm{L}^{\ast}}(p)
instead
of
\varepsilon^{\mu}_{\mathrm{L}}(p)
.
Since a similar technique has been used in
Dawson:1984gx
for the
derivation of the
EWA
, this procedure is coined as “Dawson’s
approach”.
For the specific situation of the
EWA
, i.e. for
IS

f\to fV^{\ast}
splittings with massless fermions, the subtracted terms actually
vanish in the splitting amplitude, due to the
WI
and zero
Yukawa coupling.
For general splittings, however, this is
not
the case.
23
23

23

Already for
IS

f_{a}\to f_{i}V^{\ast}_{ai}
splittings with massive fermions
and
FS

f^{\ast}_{ij}\to f_{i}V_{j}
splittings with massless fermions, this
argument breaks down.
In the first case, replacing the gauge boson with the associated
would-be Goldstone boson yields a splitting amplitude that does not
vanish.
In the second case, the incoming fermion is off-shell, so that the

WI
for the gauge boson with index
j

cannot be applied.

In contrast,
Cuomo:2019siu
replaces these contributions with
splitting amplitudes for would-be Goldstone bosons.
24
24

24

The arguments leading to the procedure of
Cuomo:2019siu
involve

WIs
for the full
(n+1)
particle matrix element, not just the
splitting amplitude, as suggested by “Dawson’s approach”.

Thus, generally, the results from
Masouminia:2021kne
for
longitudinal vector bosons cannot be expected to consistently capture
all terms that are enhanced in the quasi-collinear limit.

Compatibility of results.

As detailed above, the splitting amplitudes
from
Cuomo:2019siu
and
Masouminia:2021kne
can only be
compared with each other for splittings without longitudinal vector
bosons.
We observe full agreement for transversely polarized
V^{\ast}\to VV

splittings.
However, for splittings with at least one longitudinal vector boson, the
results differ.
In particular, all splitting functions from
Masouminia:2021kne

with two or more longitudinal vector bosons vanish, in conflict with
both our results and those from
Cuomo:2019siu
.

6
Summary

In this paper, we
have derived
LO

FS
and
IS
splitting
functions for generic
spontaneously broken gauge theories with scalars, fermions,
and vector bosons.
These results constitute important ingredients for the construction of

EW
parton-shower simulations and subtraction algorithms
for soft singularities and collinear enhancements.
As such, they are of phenomenological interest
for LHC physics in the TeV energy range and of great importance
for physics at
future colliders with center-of-mass energies
\sqrt{s}\approx 100\,\text{TeV}
.
The main complication in the derivation of our results was
the presence of mass-singular terms in longitudinal polarization vectors,
severely complicating the process of quasi-collinear power counting.
To address this delicate issue, we have performed our computations with
two different strategies, employing gauge invariance and
WIs
as
guiding principle.
We obtained full agreement between the results obtained with these methods.

We have discussed various special cases such as the specialization to the
EWSM
and massless limits,
including the reproduction of literature results in massless and massive

QED
and
QCD
.
Moreover, we have thoroughly compared our strategy and results with existing literature
on
EW
splitting functions.
While we find agreement with corresponding results from other approaches,
we also point out differences to some earlier publications.
We stress, in particular, that our derivation is unique in that it uses a
different general methodology and
significantly reduces the number of underlying
assumptions.
In detail, we do not use any explicit, component-wise parametrization of
momenta or wave functions and also do not distinguish a particular frame
of reference.
Furthermore, we make no assumptions on the individual spin reference
axes that define the polarizations of the external particles.
Finally, our factorization formulas are expressed directly in terms of
squared matrix elements, which facilitates the application to
both parton-shower algorithms and
subtraction formalisms for soft singularities and collinear enhancements.
Our splitting functions fulfill the symmetry relations implied by those
of the corresponding real-emission matrix elements.
In particular, we confirmed that the
FS
and
IS
splitting
functions are related by crossing relations.

As an additional step, we discussed the procedure of taking the
azimuthal average of our splitting functions.
Where possible, we took special care that the final formulas can be
evaluated using squared matrix elements that are independent of the
considered frame of reference.
Apart from phenomenological motivations, this constitutes the first step
towards the computation of the full integral over the emission phase space.
This is relevant not only for the construction of subtraction
algorithms, but also for the comparison to and combination with the
virtual factorization formulas
from
denner2001oneloop1
;
denner2001oneloop2
.
In particular, this may be used for the assessment of cancellations
between the leading real and virtual corrections for specific
observables.
Both of these aspects are, however, left for future work.

Appendix A
The Electroweak Standard Model

In this appendix we describe the embedding of the EWSM into the general
framework of our description of generic spontaneously broken gauge theories
given in Section
2
, which builds on the formulation
of
denner2001oneloop1
;
denner2001oneloop2
.
In particular, we specialize all generic couplings to their specific
choices in the EWSM, based on the conventions of

Bohm:1986rj
;
Denner:1991kt
;
bohm2001gauge
;
Denner:2019vbn
.

A.1
Gauge structure

The gauge symmetry of the
EWSM
is provided by the
group
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}\times\mathrm{U}(1)_{Y}
.
Left-handed neutrino fields
\nu^{\mathrm{L}}_{\ell}
and lepton
fields
\ell^{\mathrm{L}}
are organized into left-handed lepton
doublets
L^{\mathrm{L}}=(\nu^{\mathrm{L}}_{\ell},\ell^{\mathrm{L}})^{\mathrm{T}}
,
whereas left-handed up-type quark fields
u^{\mathrm{L}}
and down-type
quark fields
d^{\mathrm{L}}
are organized into left-handed quark
doublets
Q^{\mathrm{L}}=(u^{\mathrm{L}},d^{\mathrm{L}})^{\mathrm{T}}
.
Generically, they are denoted as left-handed fermion
doublets
\varphi=f^{\mathrm{L}}
that transform in the fundamental
representation of
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
.
All fermion fields occur in three generations, which are not
mixed by gauge transformations.
The scalar Higgs doublet
\varphi=\phi
of the
EWSM
,

\displaystyle\phi(x)=\left(\begin{matrix}\phi^{+}(x)\\
\phi^{0}_{\phantom{+}}(x)\end{matrix}\right),

(346)

behaves in the same way under
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
transformations.
We also write
\phi^{-}=\phi^{+\ast}
.
In the convention of

(
denner2001oneloop1
;
denner2001oneloop2
;
bohm2001gauge
;
Denner:2019vbn
)
,
the generators of the fundamental representation
of
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
are defined, in the symmetric basis, as

\displaystyle I^{W_{a}}_{\varphi_{k}\varphi_{l}}=\frac{g_{2}}{e}T^{a}_{kl},\qquad T^{a}_{kl}=\frac{\tau^{a}_{kl}}{2}.

(347)

Here,
W_{a}
denote the
(real) gauge fields of
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
,

g_{2}
is the coupling strength of
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
gauge
interactions, and
\tau^{a}
represent the Pauli
matrices

\displaystyle\tau^{1}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{matrix}\right),\qquad\tau^{2}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&-\mathrm{i}\\
\mathrm{i}&0\end{matrix}\right),\qquad\tau^{3}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{matrix}\right).

(348)

Right-handed leptons
\ell^{\mathrm{R}}
, up-type quarks
u^{\mathrm{R}}
,
and down-type quarks
d^{\mathrm{R}}
are summarized as right-handed
fermion singlets
\varphi=f^{\mathrm{R}}
and transform in the trivial
representation of
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
.
The
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
doublets and singlet fields transform in
one-dimensional representations of
\mathrm{U}(1)_{Y}
with

\displaystyle I^{B}_{\varphi_{k}\varphi_{l}}=-\frac{g_{1}}{e}\frac{Y_{\varphi}}{2}\delta_{kl},

(349)

which defines couplings to the gauge field
B
with strength
g_{1}
.
Here,
Y_{\varphi}
is the weak hypercharge of
\varphi
.
As detailed below, it fixes the electric charges of the individual
components
\varphi_{l}
.
To normalize
g_{1}
, we set
Y_{\phi}=1
, which gives the Gell-Mann–Nishijima
relation the form quoted in (
358
).

Since the adjoint representation of an abelian Lie group is trivial, the
gauge field
B
is invariant under
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}\times\mathrm{U}(1)_{Y}
transformations.
In the symmetric field basis, the adjoint
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}

generators are obtained from the commutation relation of fundamental
generators

\displaystyle\left[I^{W_{a}},I^{W_{b}}\right]_{\varphi_{k}\varphi_{l}}=\mathrm{i}\frac{g_{2}}{e}\epsilon^{abc}I^{W_{c}}_{\varphi_{k}\varphi_{l}}.

(350)

The fields
W_{a}
thus transform with

\displaystyle I^{W_{a}}_{W_{b}W_{c}}=-\mathrm{i}\frac{g_{2}}{e}\epsilon^{abc}.

(351)

A.2
Electroweak symmetry breaking

The EWSM Lagrangian contains the Higgs potential

\displaystyle V(\phi)=\frac{\lambda}{4}\big(\phi^{\dagger}\phi\big)^{2}-\mu^{2}\phi^{\dagger}\phi,\qquad\mu^{2},\lambda>0,

(352)

with a degenerate minimum for

\phi^{\dagger}\phi=v^{2}/2=2\mu^{2}/\lambda
.
The latter is responsible for the non-zero
vev
of
\phi
which can, after a suitable gauge transformation, be written as

\langle\phi\rangle=(0,v/\sqrt{2})^{\mathrm{T}}
with

v>0
.
Applying the generators (
347
)
and (
349
) to
\langle\phi\rangle
, we
obtain

\displaystyle I^{W_{1}}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\langle\phi_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{g_{2}}{e}\frac{v}{2\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}1\\
0\end{matrix}\right)_{i},

\displaystyle I^{W_{2}}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\langle\phi_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\frac{g_{2}}{e}\frac{v}{2\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}1\\
0\end{matrix}\right)_{i},

\displaystyle I^{W_{3}}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\langle\phi_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{g_{2}}{e}\frac{v}{2\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
1\end{matrix}\right)_{i},

\displaystyle I^{W_{1}}_{\phi^{\ast}_{i}\phi^{\ast}_{j}}\langle\phi^{\ast}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{g_{2}}{e}\frac{v}{2\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}1\\
0\end{matrix}\right)_{i},

\displaystyle I^{W_{2}}_{\phi^{\ast}_{i}\phi^{\ast}_{j}}\langle\phi^{\ast}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}\frac{g_{2}}{e}\frac{v}{2\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}1\\
0\end{matrix}\right)_{i},

\displaystyle I^{W_{3}}_{\phi^{\ast}_{i}\phi^{\ast}_{j}}\langle\phi^{\ast}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{g_{2}}{e}\frac{v}{2\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
1\end{matrix}\right)_{i},

\displaystyle I^{B}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\langle\phi_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{g_{1}}{e}\frac{v}{2\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
1\end{matrix}\right)_{i},

\displaystyle I^{B}_{\phi^{\ast}_{i}\phi^{\ast}_{j}}\langle\phi^{\ast}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{g_{1}}{e}\frac{v}{2\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
1\end{matrix}\right)_{i}.

(353)

Via a rotation of the generator basis

\displaystyle\left(\begin{matrix}I^{Z}\\
I^{A}\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}c_{\mathrm{w}}&s_{\mathrm{w}}\\
-s_{\mathrm{w}}&c_{\mathrm{w}}\end{matrix}\right)\left(\begin{matrix}I^{W_{3}}\\
I^{B}\end{matrix}\right),\qquad\left(\begin{matrix}Z^{\mu}\\
A^{\mu}\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}c_{\mathrm{w}}&s_{\mathrm{w}}\\
-s_{\mathrm{w}}&c_{\mathrm{w}}\end{matrix}\right)\left(\begin{matrix}W_{3}^{\mu}\\
B^{\mu}\end{matrix}\right),

(354)

by the weak mixing angle
\theta_{\mathrm{w}}
, with

\displaystyle s_{\mathrm{w}}\equiv\sin(\theta_{\mathrm{w}})=\frac{g_{1}}{\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}}},\qquad c_{\mathrm{w}}\equiv\cos(\theta_{\mathrm{w}})=\frac{g_{2}}{\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}}},

(355)

we obtain the unbroken generator

\displaystyle I^{A}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}=-I^{A}_{\phi^{\ast}_{i}\phi^{\ast}_{j}}=-\frac{1}{e}\frac{g_{1}g_{2}}{\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}}}\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&0\\
\end{matrix}\right)_{ij},\qquad I^{A}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\langle\phi_{j}\rangle=I^{A}_{\phi^{\ast}_{i}\phi^{\ast}_{j}}\langle\phi^{\ast}_{j}\rangle=0.

(356)

The matrix
Q\equiv-I^{A}
generates the unbroken symmetry subgroup

\mathrm{U}(1)_{Q}\subset\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}\times\mathrm{U}(1)_{Y}
, which is thence identified as the group of
electromagnetic gauge transformations.
Thus,
Q
is interpreted as the electric charge operator.
The corresponding gauge field
A_{\mu}
is massless and identified as the
photon field.
Since
Q
is unbroken, the
vev
of the scalar doublet remains
electrically neutral.
Comparing the photon–fermion couplings to
QED
, we identify

\displaystyle e=\frac{g_{1}g_{2}}{\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}}},

(357)

resulting in the Gell-Mann–Nishijima relation

\displaystyle Q=\frac{e}{g_{2}}I^{W_{3}}-\frac{e}{g_{1}}I^{B}=T^{3}+\frac{Y}{2}.

(358)

The weak hypercharges
Y_{\varphi}
of all
EWSM
multiplets
\varphi

are chosen such that (
358
) reproduces their
electric charges.
Diagonalizing the adjoint generator
Q
with respect to
W_{1}^{\mu}

and
W_{2}^{\mu}
, we obtain

\displaystyle W_{\pm}^{\mu}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(W_{1}^{\mu}\mp\mathrm{i}W_{2}^{\mu}),\qquad I^{W_{\pm}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(I^{W_{1}}\pm\mathrm{i}I^{W_{2}}),

(359)

while
Z^{\mu}
remains electrically neutral.
The generators
I^{W_{\pm}}
and
I^{Z}
are broken,

\displaystyle I^{W_{+}}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\langle\phi_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=-I^{W_{-}}_{\phi^{\ast}_{i}\phi^{\ast}_{j}}\langle\phi^{\ast}_{j}\rangle=\frac{g_{2}}{e}\frac{v}{2}\left(\begin{matrix}1\\
0\end{matrix}\right)_{i},\qquad I^{W_{-}}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\langle\phi_{j}\rangle=I^{W_{+}}_{\phi^{\ast}_{i}\phi^{\ast}_{j}}\langle\phi^{\ast}_{j}\rangle=0,

\displaystyle I^{Z}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\langle\phi_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=-I^{Z}_{\phi^{\ast}_{i}\phi^{\ast}_{j}}\langle\phi^{\ast}_{j}\rangle=-\frac{\sqrt{g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}}}{e}\frac{v}{2\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
1\end{matrix}\right)_{i}.

(360)

Via (
19
), we obtain the gauge-boson masses

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{Z}}^{2}=\frac{v^{2}}{4}\big(g_{1}^{2}+g_{2}^{2}\big),\qquad M_{\mathrm{W}}^{2}=\frac{v^{2}}{4}g_{2}^{2},

(361)

and identify

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{w}}=M_{\mathrm{W}}/M_{\mathrm{Z}}.

(362)

The would-be Goldstone-boson fields corresponding to
W_{\pm}
and
Z
read

\displaystyle G_{\pm}=\pm\eta_{W_{\pm}}\phi^{\pm},\qquad G_{Z}=-\frac{\eta_{Z}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\phi^{0}-\phi^{0\ast}\right).

(363)

With
\eta_{W_{\pm}}=\pm 1
and
\eta_{Z}=\mathrm{i}
, we obtain the
standard conventions from
e.g.
(
denner2001oneloop1
;
denner2001oneloop2
;
bohm2001gauge
;
Denner:2019vbn
)
,
where
\phi^{\pm}
correspond to
G_{\pm}
and

\chi=-\mathrm{i}/\sqrt{2}(\phi^{0}-\phi^{0\ast})
corresponds to
G_{Z}
.
The remaining scalar field can be parametrized as

\displaystyle H(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\big(\phi^{0}(x)+\phi^{0\ast}(x)\big),\qquad\langle H\rangle=v,

(364)

and is identified with the Higgs field.
The associated bilinear term of the potential (
352
)
yields the mass
M_{\mathrm{H}}=\sqrt{2\mu^{2}}
,
while the cubic term
implies the triple-Higgs coupling (
37
),

\displaystyle C_{HHH}=-\frac{3\lambda v}{2e}=-\frac{3M_{\mathrm{H}}^{2}}{ev}.

(365)

The relation (
23
) explicitly reads

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{W}}=-ev\eta_{W_{\mp}}I^{W_{\pm}}_{G_{\pm}H},\qquad M_{\mathrm{Z}}=-ev\eta_{Z}I^{Z}_{G_{Z}H},

(366)

agreeing with (
361
) after plugging in the generators from
Appendix
A.3
.

Finally, we discuss the fermion mass terms of the
EWSM
,
writing
f^{\mathrm{L}}_{i\pm}

for the left-handed fermion field of generation
i
and
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}

doublet component
\pm
, and
f^{\mathrm{R}}_{i\pm}
for the right-handed

\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
singlet counterpart.
25
25

25

This means we divide the set of fermion indices
i

from (
27
) into gauge group (singlet and
doublet) and generation indices.
This is convenient because gauge transformations do not mix
generation indices.

Moreover, we define the charge conjugate scalar doublet

\displaystyle\phi^{\mathrm{c}}_{\zeta}=\mathrm{i}\tau^{2}_{\zeta\rho}\phi_{\rho}^{\ast}=\left(\begin{matrix}\phi^{0\ast}\\
-\phi^{-}\end{matrix}\right)_{\zeta},

(367)

which transforms like
\phi

under
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
.
Note that
\phi_{\pm}
represent the upper and lower components
of the Higgs doublet, respectively, and
differ

from
\phi^{\pm}
as defined in (
346
).
The condition (
28
) is fulfilled both
for
V_{a}=W_{1},W_{2},W_{3}
and for
V_{a}=B
if

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{\phi_{\zeta}}_{f^{\mathrm{L}}_{i\rho}f^{\mathrm{R}}_{j\gamma}}=-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{v}M_{f^{\mathrm{L}}_{i-}f^{\mathrm{R}}_{j-}}\delta_{\rho\zeta}\delta_{\gamma-},\qquad\Upsilon^{\phi_{\zeta}^{\ast}}_{f^{\mathrm{L}}_{i\rho}f^{\mathrm{R}}_{j\gamma}}=-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{v}M_{f^{\mathrm{L}}_{i+}f^{\mathrm{R}}_{j+}}\mathrm{i}\tau^{2}_{\rho\zeta}\delta_{\gamma+}.

(368)

In the conventions of
Denner:1991kt
;
bohm2001gauge
;
Denner:2019vbn
, where the
Yukawa coupling matrices are denoted
G^{f}
(
f=\ell,d,u
), this
corresponds to

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{\phi_{-}}_{\ell^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}\ell^{\mathrm{R}}_{j}}=\Upsilon^{\phi_{+}}_{\nu^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}\ell^{\mathrm{R}}_{j}}=-G^{\ell}_{ij},\qquad\Upsilon^{\phi_{-}}_{d^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}d^{\mathrm{R}}_{j}}=\Upsilon^{\phi_{+}}_{u^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}d^{\mathrm{R}}_{j}}=-G^{d}_{ij},\qquad\Upsilon^{\phi_{-}^{\ast}}_{u^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}u^{\mathrm{R}}_{j}}=-\Upsilon^{\phi_{+}^{\ast}}_{d^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}u^{\mathrm{R}}_{j}}=-G^{u}_{ij},

(369)

with
\Upsilon^{\dots}_{\dots}=0
otherwise.
Diagonalizing the mass matrix, we arrive at the Yukawa interaction terms

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{Yuk}}={}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}\frac{m_{\ell_{i}}}{v}\bar{\ell}^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}\ell^{\mathrm{R}}_{i}\phi_{-}-\sqrt{2}\frac{m_{d_{i}}}{v}\bar{d}^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}d^{\mathrm{R}}_{i}\phi_{-}-\sqrt{2}\frac{m_{u_{i}}}{v}\bar{u}^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}u^{\mathrm{R}}_{i}\phi^{\mathrm{c}}_{+}

\displaystyle{}-\sqrt{2}\frac{m_{\ell_{i}}}{v}\bar{\nu}^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}\ell^{\mathrm{R}}_{i}\phi_{+}-\sqrt{2}\frac{m_{d_{i}}}{v}\bar{u}^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}d^{\mathrm{R}}_{j}\phi_{+}V_{ij}-\sqrt{2}\frac{m_{u_{i}}}{v}\bar{d}^{\mathrm{L}}_{i}u^{\mathrm{R}}_{j}\phi^{\mathrm{c}}_{-}V^{\dagger}_{ij}+\mathrm{h.c.},

(370)

where
(V_{ij})
is the

CKM
matrix
describing quark mixing.
Reducing the Higgs fields to its

vevs
,
\langle\phi_{\zeta}\rangle=\delta_{\zeta-}v/\sqrt{2}

and
\langle\phi^{\mathrm{c}}_{\zeta}\rangle=\delta_{\zeta+}v/\sqrt{2}
,
produces the fermion mass terms

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{m_{f}}=

\displaystyle-\Big(m_{f_{i-}}\bar{f}^{\mathrm{L}}_{i-}f^{\mathrm{R}}_{i-}+m_{f_{i+}}\bar{f}^{\mathrm{L}}_{i+}f^{\mathrm{R}}_{i+}\Big)+\mathrm{h.c.}=-\Big(m_{f_{i-}}\bar{f}_{i-}f_{i-}+m_{f_{i+}}\bar{f}_{i+}f_{i+}\Big).

(371)

Correspondingly, the fermion–Higgs coupling (
39
) reads

\displaystyle C^{\sigma}_{H\bar{f}_{k}f_{l}}=C_{H\bar{f}_{k}f_{l}}=-\frac{m_{f_{l}}}{ev}\delta_{f_{k}f_{l}}.

(372)

A.3
Explicit coupling factors

Here, we provide explicit values for the coupling
factors
I^{V_{a}}_{\varphi_{j}\varphi_{k}}
for the various
fields
\varphi_{j}
,
\varphi_{k}
, and
V_{a}
of the
EWSM
.
These are largely taken from Appendix B of
denner2001oneloop1
.
Using definition (
1
), they can be easily read off from (23) of

Denner:2019vbn
as well.
Coupling factors that are not given below vanish.
The couplings for conjugated fields are obtained
via (
7
)
and (
8
).

Charged-current
couplings
\boldsymbol{I^{W_{\pm}}}
.

For the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}

doublets
\varphi=L^{\mathrm{L}},Q^{\mathrm{L}},\phi,\phi^{\mathrm{c}}
,
the representations of
I^{W_{a}}
with
a=\pm
have the
non-vanishing components

\displaystyle I^{W_{a}}_{\varphi_{\zeta}\varphi_{-\zeta}}=\frac{g_{2}}{e}\frac{\delta_{a\zeta}}{\sqrt{2}}=\frac{\delta_{a\zeta}}{\sqrt{2}s_{\mathrm{w}}}.

(373)

Here,
\zeta=\pm
indicate the upper and lower components of
the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
doublet
\varphi
,
respectively, and represent the eigenvalues of
\tau^{3}
.
The scalar fields
S=H,\chi
in the physical basis do not correspond
to eigenstates of
\tau^{3}
, because the neutral Higgs
fields
\phi^{0}
and
\phi^{0\ast}
in the symmetric
basis possess distinct eigenvalues of
\tau^{3}
.
Thus, the transformation of (
373
) into the physical basis
is non-trivial and results in

\displaystyle I^{W_{a}}_{S\phi^{-b}}=-I^{W_{a}}_{\phi^{b}S}=\delta_{ab}I^{W_{a}}_{S},\qquad a,b=\pm,

(374)

with

\displaystyle I^{W_{a}}_{H}=-\frac{a}{2s_{\mathrm{w}}},\qquad I^{W_{a}}_{\chi}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2s_{\mathrm{w}}}.

(375)

Again, we stress that
\phi_{\pm}
differs
from
\phi^{\pm}
.
For the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
singlets

\varphi=\ell^{\mathrm{R}},q^{\mathrm{R}},d^{\mathrm{R}}
, the couplings

I^{W_{\pm}}
vanish.
After transforming the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
structure constants
into the physical bases of the charged sector (
W_{\pm}
) and the neutral
sector (
A,Z
), the
W_{\pm}
couplings to gauge fields read

\displaystyle I^{W_{a}}_{NW_{-b}}=-I^{W_{a}}_{W_{b}N}=\delta_{ab}I^{W_{a}}_{N},

(376)

with
a,b=\pm
,
N=A,Z
, and

\displaystyle I^{W_{a}}_{A}=-a,\qquad I^{W_{a}}_{Z}=a\frac{g_{2}}{g_{1}}=a\frac{c_{\mathrm{w}}}{s_{\mathrm{w}}}.

(377)

Neutral-current couplings
\boldsymbol{I^{A}}
,

\boldsymbol{I^{Z}}
.

The photon couplings
I^{A}=-Q
are determined via the Gell-Mann–Nishijima
relation (
358
) by the weak isospin
T^{3}
and
weak hypercharge
Y
.
The couplings to the
Z
field are obtained via
T^{3}
and
Y
according to

\displaystyle I^{Z}=\frac{1}{g_{1}g_{2}}\left(g_{2}^{2}T^{3}-g_{1}^{2}\frac{Y}{2}\right)=\frac{1}{s_{\mathrm{w}}c_{\mathrm{w}}}\left(c_{\mathrm{w}}^{2}T^{3}-s_{\mathrm{w}}^{2}\frac{Y}{2}\right)=\frac{T^{3}-s_{\mathrm{w}}^{2}Q}{s_{\mathrm{w}}c_{\mathrm{w}}}.

(378)

For
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
doublet components, we have

\displaystyle T^{3}_{\varphi_{\zeta}\varphi_{\rho}}=\delta_{\zeta\rho}\frac{\zeta}{2},

(379)

with
\zeta,\rho=\pm
denoting the doublet
components as before.
For right-handed
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
singlets,
T^{3}
vanishes.
In the basis of charge eigenstates,
the only non-zero adjoint components of
T^{3}
are

\displaystyle T^{3}_{W_{a}W_{b}}=a\delta_{ab},\qquad a,b=\pm.

(380)

The weak hypercharges
Y_{V_{a}}
of all gauge fields
vanish,
while we have
Y_{\phi}=-Y_{\phi^{\mathrm{c}}}=1
for
the Higgs doublet.
The isospin and hypercharge matrices
I^{3}
and
Y
are diagonal with respect to all fields of the
EWSM
in the
physical basis, except for
H
and
\chi
.
In the physical basis of the neutral Higgs sector, we get

\displaystyle T^{3}_{HH}=T^{3}_{\chi\chi}=0,\qquad T^{3}_{H\chi}=-T^{3}_{\chi H}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}

(381)

and

\displaystyle T^{3}_{SS^{\prime}}=-\frac{Y_{SS^{\prime}}}{2},\qquad S,S^{\prime}\in\{H,\chi\},

(382)

such that the electric charges corresponding to
H
and
\chi
indeed
vanish.
The couplings to the Z boson are given by

\displaystyle I^{Z}_{H\chi}=-I^{Z}_{\chi H}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2s_{\mathrm{w}}c_{\mathrm{w}}}.

(383)

For the individual (left- and right-handed) lepton and quark fields, the
hypercharge eigenvalues are chosen such that the desired electric
charges
Q
are obtained.
The eigenvalues for all fields of the
EWSM
are tabulated in

Appendix B
Derivation of generic would-be Goldstone couplings

In this appendix, we discuss the derivation of the coupling relations
given in Section
2.6
.

VGH
couplings.

The vertex structure for couplings between one scalar and two vector
bosons is defined in (
35
), while that for one
vector boson and two scalars is is provided
by (
36
).
With this, we have

\displaystyle p_{1}^{\mu_{1}}G^{\underline{V_{a}V_{b}H_{m}}}_{0,\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\varepsilon_{b}^{\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}eC_{H_{m}V_{a}V_{b}}(p_{1}\varepsilon_{b}),

\displaystyle\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}G^{\underline{G_{a}V_{b}H_{m}}}_{0,\mu_{2}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\varepsilon_{b}^{\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle=-2\mathrm{i}e\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}I^{V_{b}}_{\bar{G}_{a}H_{m}}(p_{1}\varepsilon_{b}),

(384)

as well as

\displaystyle p_{2}^{\mu_{2}}G^{\underline{V_{a}V_{b}H_{m}}}_{0,\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\varepsilon_{a}^{\mu_{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}eC_{H_{m}V_{a}V_{b}}(p_{2}\varepsilon_{a}),

\displaystyle\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}G^{\underline{V_{a}G_{b}H_{m}}}_{0,\mu_{1}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\varepsilon_{a}^{\mu_{1}}

\displaystyle=-2\mathrm{i}e\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}I^{V_{a}}_{\bar{G}_{b}H_{m}}(p_{2}\varepsilon_{a}),

(385)

where
\varepsilon_{a}(p_{1})
and
\varepsilon_{b}(p_{2})
are polarization
vectors for the momenta
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
, respectively.
Employing the
WI
(
46
), we get the
identities (
49
) between
HVV
and
VGH

couplings.

GVV
couplings.

With (
35
) and the pure-gauge vertex
from (
34
), we have

\displaystyle p_{1}^{\mu_{1}}G^{\underline{V_{a}V_{b}V_{c}}}_{0,\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\varepsilon_{b}^{\mu_{2}}\varepsilon_{c}^{\mu_{3}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}[M_{V_{c}}^{2}-M_{V_{b}}^{2}](\varepsilon_{b}\varepsilon_{c}),

\displaystyle\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}G^{\underline{G_{a}V_{b}V_{c}}}_{0,\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\varepsilon_{b}^{\mu_{2}}\varepsilon_{c}^{\mu_{3}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}eC_{G_{a}V_{b}V_{c}}\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}(\varepsilon_{b}\varepsilon_{c}),

(386)

where the momenta
p_{2}
and

are assumed on-shell.
Through the

(

), this yields the

) between

and
GVV

VGG

We obtain (
54
), which expresses the
connection between

and
VVV

\displaystyle\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}G^{\underline{G_{a}G_{b}V_{c}}}_{0,\mu_{3}}\varepsilon_{c}^{\mu_{3}}

\displaystyle=-2\mathrm{i}e\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}I^{V_{c}}_{\bar{G}_{a}G_{b}}p_{1}\varepsilon_{c},

\displaystyle p_{1}^{\mu_{1}}p_{2}^{\mu_{2}}G^{\underline{V_{a}V_{b}V_{c}}}_{0,\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\varepsilon_{c}^{\mu_{3}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}M_{V_{c}}^{2}p_{1}\varepsilon_{c},

\displaystyle p_{2}^{\mu_{2}}\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}G^{\underline{G_{a}V_{b}V_{c}}}_{0,\mu_{2},\mu_{3}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\varepsilon_{c}^{\mu_{3}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}e\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}C_{G_{a}V_{b}V_{c}}p_{2}\varepsilon_{c}=\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}\big(M_{V_{b}}^{2}-M_{V_{c}}^{2}\big)p_{1}\varepsilon_{c},

\displaystyle p_{1}^{\mu_{1}}\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}G^{\underline{V_{a}G_{b}V_{c}}}_{0,\mu_{1},\mu_{3}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})\varepsilon_{c}^{\mu_{3}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}e\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}C_{G_{b}V_{a}V_{c}}p_{1}\varepsilon_{c}=\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}\big(M_{V_{a}}^{2}-M_{V_{c}}^{2}\big)p_{1}\varepsilon_{c},

(387)

using the
WI
(

).
Here,
p_{3}
is assumed on-shell and we have
employed (

) to replace
GVV

With the purely scalar vertex structure from (

),

\displaystyle\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}G^{\underline{G_{a}G_{b}H_{m}}}_{0}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})

\displaystyle p_{1}^{\mu_{1}}p_{2}^{\mu_{2}}G^{\underline{V_{a}V_{b}H_{m}}}_{0,\mu_{1}\mu_{1}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})=\mathrm{i}eC_{H_{m}V_{a}V_{b}}p_{1}p_{2}=-2\mathrm{i}e\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}I^{V_{b}}_{\bar{G}_{a}H_{m}}p_{1}p_{2},

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}e\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}I^{V_{b}}_{\bar{G}_{a}H_{m}}(p_{1}p_{2}-p_{2}p_{3}).

GGG

To show that all
GGG

(

and

,

26
26

26

Note, however, that the
WI
(

) for
three external vector bosons actually does not require any of the

We obtain

\displaystyle p_{1}^{\mu_{1}}p_{2}^{\mu_{2}}p_{3}^{\mu_{3}}G_{0,\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}^{\underline{V^{a}}\underline{V^{b}}\underline{V^{c}}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle-p_{1}^{\mu_{1}}p_{2}^{\mu_{2}}\eta_{V_{c}}M_{V_{c}}G_{0,\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{\underline{V^{a}}\underline{V^{b}}\underline{G^{c}}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}\big(M_{V_{b}}^{2}-M_{V_{a}}^{2}\big)p_{1}p_{2},

\displaystyle-p_{2}^{\mu_{2}}p_{3}^{\mu_{3}}\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}G_{0,\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}^{\underline{G^{a}}\underline{V^{b}}\underline{V^{c}}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}\big(M_{V_{c}}^{2}-M_{V_{b}}^{2}\big)\big(-p_{1}p_{2}-M_{V_{b}}^{2}\big),

\displaystyle-p_{1}^{\mu_{1}}p_{3}^{\mu_{3}}\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}G_{0,\mu_{1}\mu_{3}}^{\underline{V^{a}}\underline{G^{b}}\underline{V^{c}}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}\big(M_{V_{a}}^{2}-M_{V_{c}}^{2}\big)\big(-p_{1}p_{2}-M_{V_{a}}^{2}\big),

\displaystyle p_{3}^{\mu_{3}}\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}G_{0,\mu_{3}}^{\underline{G^{a}}\underline{G^{b}}\underline{V^{c}}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})

\displaystyle=+\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}\frac{M_{V_{c}}^{2}\big(M_{V_{a}}^{2}-M_{V_{b}}^{2}\big)+M_{V_{b}}^{4}-M_{V_{a}}^{4}}{2},

\displaystyle p_{1}^{\mu_{1}}\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}\eta_{V_{c}}M_{V_{c}}G_{0,\mu_{1}}^{\underline{V^{a}}\underline{G^{b}}\underline{G^{c}}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}\frac{M_{V_{a}}^{2}-M_{V_{b}}^{2}-M_{V_{c}}^{2}}{2}\big(2p_{1}p_{2}+M_{V_{a}}^{2}\big),

\displaystyle p_{2}^{\mu_{2}}\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}\eta_{V_{c}}M_{V_{c}}G_{0,\mu_{2}}^{\underline{G^{a}}\underline{V^{b}}\underline{G^{c}}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{\bar{V}_{b}V_{c}}\frac{M_{V_{b}}^{2}-M_{V_{c}}^{2}-M_{V_{a}}^{2}}{2}\big(-2p_{1}p_{2}-M_{V_{b}}^{2}\big),

\displaystyle-\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}\eta_{V_{c}}M_{V_{c}}G_{0,\mu_{3}}^{\underline{G^{a}}\underline{G^{b}}\underline{G^{c}}}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{i}eC_{G_{a}G_{b}G_{c}}\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}\eta_{V_{b}}M_{V_{b}}\eta_{V_{c}}M_{V_{c}}.

(392)

Summing the r.h.s. of the first seven lines yields zero,
implying (
59
).

G\bar{f}f
couplings.

The vertex structures for couplings of scalars and vector bosons to
fermions are defined in (
39
)
and (
38
), respectively.
Using the Dirac equations (
31
) for the
spinors
\bar{v}_{k}(p_{2})
and

with on-shell momenta
p_{2}

and

and the relation
\gamma_{\mu}\omega_{\sigma}=\omega_{-\sigma}\gamma_{\mu}
for the chirality projection matrix (
