),
we get

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{k}p_{1}^{\mu}G^{\underline{V_{a}\bar{f}_{k}f_{l}}}_{0,\mu}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})u_{l}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{f^{\sigma}_{k}f^{\sigma}_{l}}\bar{v}_{k}\not{p}_{1}\omega_{\sigma}u_{l}=\mathrm{i}eI^{V_{a}}_{f^{\sigma}_{k}f^{\sigma}_{l}}\bar{v}_{k}\left(-\not{p}_{2}\omega_{\sigma}-\omega_{-\sigma}\not{p}_{3}\right)u_{l}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}e\bar{v}_{k}\left(m_{f_{k}}I^{V_{a}}_{f^{\sigma}_{k}f^{\sigma}_{l}}-m_{f_{l}}I^{V_{a}}_{f^{-\sigma}_{k}f^{-\sigma}_{l}}\right)\omega_{\sigma}u_{l},

(393)

\displaystyle\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}\bar{v}_{k}G^{\underline{G_{a}\bar{f}_{k}f_{l}}}_{0}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})u_{l}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}e\eta_{V_{a}}M_{V_{a}}C^{\sigma}_{G_{a}\bar{f}_{k}f_{l}}\bar{v}_{k}\omega_{\sigma}u_{l},

(394)

where
\sigma=\pm
is summed over.
Plugging this into the
WI
(
46
) yields the
relation (
60
) between

G\bar{f}f
and
V\bar{f}f

couplings.

Appendix C
Alternative treatment of the mass-singular factor in

\boldsymbol{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}}

In this appendix,
we support our derivations from
Section
4
by presenting an
alternative, but closely related strategy for the treatment of the
contributions
\propto p^{\mu}/M_{V}
from longitudinal polarization
vectors
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}^{\mu}
for a vector boson
V
.
This approach follows a more “pedestrian” procedure.
In Section
3.2
, we detailed how the
GBET
implies the cancellation of
mass-singular “gauge contributions”
from diagrams
of type I that are not of collinear origin
(i.e. without factors of the collinearly enhanced propagator)
against counterparts from the remaining
diagrams of type II.
After performing this compensation manually, the residual contributions
from diagrams of types I and II are separately free
of such mass-singular gauge terms.
The diagrams of type II can now completely be considered
subleading.
In practice, this procedure requires the knowledge of the
compensating mass-singular gauge terms.
Conveniently, their structure was precisely and universally described
in Section
3.2
, allowing for their systematic
identification and removal from the diagrams of type I.
Below, we explicitly demonstrate this approach for the derivation of

V^{\ast}\to VV
and
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}
splitting functions.
An approach similar to this has been employed in
Kleiss:2020rcg
.

C.1
\boldsymbol{V^{\ast}\to VV}

splitting functions

For the LT configuration
of
V^{\ast}\to VV
splittings,
we consider the tHF contributions that arise
from replacing
\varepsilon^{\mu}(p_{i})
with
p_{i}^{\mu}/m_{i}
in the first
two lines of (
106
).
Denoting the contraction with
p_{i}^{\mu}/m_{i}
as “auxiliary
polarization”
\lambda_{i}=\mathrm{P}
, this yields

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{P}\mathrm{T}}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{i}}\frac{eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\bigg\{-\big(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}\big)\Big(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}(p_{j})T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle+\big(m_{j}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}\big)\Big(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}(p_{j})T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)+\Big(p_{ij}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)\big(p_{i}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}(p_{j})\big)\bigg\},

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{P}\mathrm{T}}={}

\displaystyle\frac{eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\Bigg\{\frac{\big(m_{i}^{2}-m_{j}^{2}\big)\Big(p_{ij}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)\big(p_{i}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}(p_{j})\big)}{m_{i}m_{ij}^{2}}\Bigg\}.

(395)

In the first term of the first line, the factor
(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2})

cancels with the propagator denominator, rendering it of the same
structure as corresponding mass-singular
gauge terms from the unconsidered
diagrams of type II.
As argued in Section
3.2
, the
GBET
requires these two contributions, though stemming from the two
distinct types of diagrams, to cancel.
This instructs us to identify and drop the term
\propto(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2})
from (
395
).
In other words, this manual but unambiguous procedure restores the gauge
cancellations tied to the mass-singular factor of

\varepsilon^{\mu}_{\mathrm{L}}(p_{i})
.
All other contributions from (
395
)
are seemingly also singular in
m_{i}
.
However, no further compensations with
diagrams of type II may occur, because
the latter terms are
proportional to a propagator factor.
In fact, the outlined procedure leads us to

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{P}\mathrm{T}}+\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{P}\mathrm{T}}

\displaystyle\to\frac{eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\bigg\{\frac{m_{j}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}{m_{i}}\Big(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}(p_{j})T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)+\frac{m_{i}^{2}+m_{ij}^{2}-m_{j}^{2}}{m_{i}m_{ij}}\frac{p_{ij}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})}{m_{ij}}\big(p_{i}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}(p_{j})\big)\bigg\},

(396)

which is well behaved in the massless limit
m_{i}\to 0
, following the
discussion from Section
2.6
.
Moreover, this expression directly agrees with

\displaystyle\eta_{V_{i}}^{\ast}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{G_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{T}}+\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{G_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\mathrm{T}}

(397)

from (
120
),
which means that we recover the LT splitting
function (
121
) after adding the corresponding
R-polarized contributions and following the remaining procedure from
Section
4.2.1
.
This illustrates two things:
First, selecting
would-be Goldstone-boson diagrams instead of vector-boson diagrams for
the P-polarized contributions
in Section
4.2.1

precisely removes the terms responsible
for incomplete gauge cancellations.
Second, the procedure from this section restores the exact
version (
63
) of the
GBET
at the considered
mass order, which gets broken by the naive diagram selection procedure.

For the LL configuration, we have to consider the polarization
contributions PP, PR, and RP, corresponding to the different
combinations of the terms
p^{\mu}/m
and
r^{\mu}

from
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}^{\mu}(p_{i})

and
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{L}}^{\mu}(p_{j})
.
For the PR and RP cases, we obtain results that are very similar
to (
395
)
and treat them accordingly.
For the PP part, on the other hand, we get

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(V_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{P}\mathrm{P}}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m_{i}m_{j}}\frac{eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\bigg\{\big(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}\big)\Big((p_{i}-p_{j})T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad{}-m_{i}^{2}\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})\Big((p_{i}-p_{j})T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad{}-2m_{i}^{2}\Big(p_{j}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)+2m_{j}^{2}\Big(p_{i}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)\bigg\},

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(G_{ij},\mathrm{tHF})}\Big]_{V_{i}V_{j}X}^{\mathrm{P}\mathrm{P}}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m_{i}m_{ij}^{2}m_{j}}\frac{eI_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\bigg\{\big(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}\big)\big(m_{i}^{2}-m_{j}^{2}\big)\Big(p_{ij}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad{}-m_{i}^{2}\rho(m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij})\big(m_{i}^{2}-m_{j}^{2}\big)\Big(p_{ij}T^{(n)}_{V_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\Big)\bigg\}.

(398)

Again, the first term in each of
these equations must cancel against
counterparts from neglected diagrams of type II and therefore
be removed.
Combining the modified PP, PR, and RP contributions with the RR counterparts, we
obtain (
127
) and eventually reproduce the LL
splitting function (
128
).

We note that the
procedure described in this section is based on
individual amplitudes
before interfering them with complex conjugate amplitudes,
since the multiplications
in (
74
) obscure the structure
in which the problematic mass-singular gauge
terms contribute.
More specifically, consider the product of two diagrams of
type I.
Terms
\propto 1/M_{V}/(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2})
may be produced in two
distinct ways:
first, by multiplication of propagator-enhanced terms from one diagram
with terms
\propto 1/M_{V}
from
another diagram, in which the
propagator denominator explicitly canceled against the numerator;
second, via products
of two propagator-enhanced terms, where a
propagator factor is only canceled after combining
the numerators.
27
27

27

In the above example, this happens for the product of two terms
proportional to
p_{i}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}(p_{j})
.
Inserting the polarization sum (
77
) into

\big(p_{i}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}(p_{j})\big)\big(p_{i}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{T}}(p_{j})\big)

produces terms that are proportional to
(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2})
.
On the level of individual diagrams, the separate
factors
p_{i}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ast}(p_{j})
, however, cannot fully
cancel a propagator denominator by themselves.
The corresponding terms, thus, may not cancel with contributions from
neglected diagrams.

While these two types of contributions are indistinguishable from each
other, only the first kind cancels against counterparts that involve
neglected diagrams of type II.

C.2
\boldsymbol{\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}}

splitting functions

We now turn to the adaptation of this strategy to the
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}
case.
In
Kleiss:2020rcg
, this process is used for the explicit
discussion of the handling of mass-singular terms.
The corresponding manipulations are described
in (20) and (21) there.
However, the overall factorization method from
Kleiss:2020rcg
and thereby the precise
application of this treatment
differ significantly from our approach,
see also Section
5.3

and (
344
).

Replacing
\varepsilon^{\mu}(p_{i})
in the first line
of (
245
) by
p_{i}^{\mu}/m_{i}
yields

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\bar{f}_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\mathrm{P}\kappa_{j}}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m_{i}}\frac{e}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\bigg\{\big(m_{ij}-m_{j}\big)\bigg(I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}+I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\bigg)\not{p}_{i}

\displaystyle\quad{}-\big(m_{ij}+m_{j}\big)\bigg(I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}-I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\bigg)\not{p}_{i}\gamma^{5}

\displaystyle\quad{}-\Big(m_{ij}^{2}-m_{j}^{2}+\big(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}\big)\Big)\bigg(I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}+I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad{}+\Big(m_{ij}^{2}-m_{j}^{2}+\big(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}\big)\Big)\bigg(I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}-I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\bigg)\gamma^{5}\bigg\}v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j}),

(399)

where we simplified the Dirac algebra and employed the Dirac
equations (
31
).
Removing the terms
\propto(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2})
and rearranging the
residual expression, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\bar{f}_{ij})}\Big]_{V_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\mathrm{P}\kappa_{j}}\to{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{i}}\frac{e}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}(p_{ij})\bigg\{\bigg(m_{ij}I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}-m_{j}I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\bigg)\big(\not{p}_{i}\omega_{+}-m_{j}\omega_{-}-m_{ij}\omega_{+}\big)

\displaystyle\quad{}+\bigg(m_{ij}I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}-m_{j}I_{f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\bigg)\big(\not{p}_{i}\omega_{-}-m_{j}\omega_{+}-m_{ij}\omega_{-}\big)\bigg\}v_{\kappa_{j}}(p_{j}).

(400)

Using the Dirac equations (
31
) once more, this
expression can be identified with the partial matrix element

\displaystyle\eta_{V_{i}}^{\ast}\Big[\mathcal{M}^{(n+1)}_{(\bar{f}_{ij})}\Big]_{G_{i}\bar{f}_{j}X}^{\mathrm{G}\kappa_{j}}

(401)

from (
245
).
Thus, supplementing (
400
) with
the corresponding R-polarized contribution and completing the remaining
steps from Section
4.3.6
, we recover the
splitting functions for longitudinally polarized
V
from (
254
)
and (
258
) for
\kappa_{j}=\pm
,
respectively.

Appendix D
Reproduction of literature results for QED and QCD splitting functions

D.1
FS

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}^{\ast}\to\mathrm{g}\mathrm{g}}

splitting function

Taking the limit

\displaystyle m_{i},m_{j},m_{ij},m_{ij}^{\prime}\to 0\qquad\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}}{m_{ij}}\to 0,\qquad\frac{\bar{p}^{\nu}}{m_{ij}^{\prime}}\to 0,

(402)

and setting
V_{ij}=V_{ij^{\prime}}
,
the TT splitting function (
118
) becomes

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}\to\left|I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\right|^{2}\bigg\{4\bigg(\frac{1}{z^{2}}+\frac{1}{(z-1)^{2}}\bigg)k_{\perp}^{2}g^{\mu\nu}+8k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}\bigg\}.

(403)

Accounting for the difference between the
conventions (
80
) from this work
and (4.5) from
catani2002massivedipole
by multiplying with

\displaystyle G_{\mathrm{CDST}}\equiv\frac{e^{2}}{8\pi\alpha_{s}}\frac{1}{p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}}\to\frac{e^{2}}{8\pi\alpha_{s}}\frac{z(z-1)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}

(404)

and setting

\displaystyle e^{2}\left|I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\right|^{2}\to 4\pi\alpha_{s}C_{\mathrm{A}},

(405)

we recover the triple-gluon
QCD
splitting function for the
process
\mathrm{g}^{\ast}\to\mathrm{g}\mathrm{g}
,

\displaystyle G_{\mathrm{CDST}}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{V_{ij}}_{V_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}\to 2C_{\mathrm{A}}\bigg\{\bigg(\frac{z-1}{z}+\frac{z}{z-1}\bigg)g^{\mu\nu}+2z(z-1)\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\bigg\}

(406)

in
D=4
space–time dimensions,
given in (4.9) of
catani2002massivedipole

for arbitrary
D
.

D.2
FS

\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}^{\ast}\to q\bar{q}}

and

\boldsymbol{\gamma^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}}

splitting functions

Taking the limit

\displaystyle m_{ij},m_{ij}^{\prime}\to 0,\qquad\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}}{m_{ij}}\to 0,\qquad\frac{\bar{p}^{\nu}}{m_{ij}^{\prime}}\to 0

(407)

and setting

\displaystyle m_{i}=m_{j},\qquad V_{ij^{\prime}}=V_{ij},\qquad I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{V_{ij}}=I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{V_{ij}}\equiv I_{f_{i}f_{j}}^{V_{ij}},

(408)

the unpolarized splitting function (
208
) becomes

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{U},\mathrm{U}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}\to\left|I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\right|^{2}\bigg\{\frac{2\big(m_{i}^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2}\big)}{z(z-1)}g^{\mu\nu}-8k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}\bigg\}.

(409)

Upon multiplication with the conventional factor

\displaystyle G_{\mathrm{CDST}}\to\frac{e^{2}}{8\pi\alpha_{s}}\frac{1}{p_{ij}^{2}}=\frac{e^{2}}{8\pi\alpha_{s}}\frac{z(z-1)}{k_{\perp}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}},

(410)

and setting

\displaystyle e^{2}\left|I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\right|^{2}\to 4\pi\alpha_{s}T_{\mathrm{R}}\delta_{f_{i}f_{j}},

(411)

we recover the unpolarized
\mathrm{g}^{\ast}\to q\bar{q}
splitting
function from (4.8) of
catani2002massivedipole
,

\displaystyle G_{\mathrm{CDST}}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{U},\mathrm{U}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}\to\delta_{f_{i}f_{j}}T_{\mathrm{R}}\bigg\{-g^{\mu\nu}-\frac{4k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}}{\big(p_{i}+p_{j}\big)^{2}}\bigg\}.

(412)

Similarly, we recover the unpolarized
\gamma^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}

splitting function (4.3) from
dittmaier2008polarized
by setting

\displaystyle e^{2}\left|I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\right|^{2}\to N_{\mathrm{c},f_{i}}Q_{f_{i}}^{2}\delta_{f_{i}f_{j}}

(413)

and multiplying with the conventional factor

\displaystyle G_{\mathrm{DKK}}\equiv\frac{1}{N_{\mathrm{c},{f_{i}}}Q_{f_{i}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\big(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}\big)^{2}}\to\frac{1}{N_{\mathrm{c},{f_{i}}}Q_{f_{i}}^{2}}\left(\frac{z(z-1)}{k_{\perp}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}}\right)^{2},

(414)

which is required to match
our (
80
) with (4.2)
from
dittmaier2008polarized
.
Here,
Q_{f}
denotes the electric charge of fermion
f

and
N_{\mathrm{c},f}

denotes its number of colors.

Finally, we obtain the results for polarized
\mathrm{g}^{\ast}\to q\bar{q}
splittings from
Czakon:2009ss
, by writing

\displaystyle\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\kappa_{i}+}\delta_{\kappa_{j}+}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,+\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}+\delta_{\kappa_{i}+}\delta_{\kappa_{j}-}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,-\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}+\delta_{\kappa_{i}-}\delta_{\kappa_{j}+}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},-,+\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}+\delta_{\kappa_{i}-}\delta_{\kappa_{j}-}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},-,-\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}.

(415)

We insert (
408
), contract (
415
)
with
\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda^{\prime},\nu}^{\ast}
,
and evaluate the different scalar products of the polarization vectors via

\displaystyle\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}=-s_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}\delta_{\lambda\mp},\qquad\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda^{\prime}}^{\ast}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\pm}=-\delta_{\lambda^{\prime}\pm},\qquad\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda^{\prime}}^{\ast}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}=-\delta_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}.

(416)

To match the convention from
Czakon:2009ss
, we set
s_{\varepsilon}\to-1
.
We reduce all occurring Kronecker deltas to a set involving
only
\lambda^{(\prime)}
,
\kappa_{i}
,
\kappa_{j}
and further minimize
their number by employing various algebraic relations.
Defining
\delta^{\prime}_{\kappa_{1}\kappa_{2}}=1-\delta_{\kappa_{1}\kappa_{2}}
, this finally yields

\displaystyle\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda^{\prime},\nu}^{\ast}\bigg[\mathcal{P}{}^{V_{ij}}_{f_{i}}{}^{V_{ij}}_{\bar{f}_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{i},\kappa_{j}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\to 2\left|I_{V_{i}\bar{V}_{j}}^{V_{ij}}\right|^{2}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\delta_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\bigg[m_{i}^{2}\bigg(\frac{\delta_{\kappa_{i}\lambda}-\delta^{\prime}_{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}}{z}+\frac{\delta^{\prime}_{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}-\delta_{\kappa_{j}\lambda}}{z-1}\bigg)+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}-m_{i}^{2}}{z(z-1)}\left(z\delta_{\kappa_{i}\lambda}-(z-1)\delta_{\kappa_{j}\lambda}\right)\delta^{\prime}_{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}\bigg]

\displaystyle-2\big(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}\big)\big(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda^{\prime}}^{\ast}\big)\delta^{\prime}_{\kappa_{i}\kappa_{j}}\bigg\}.

(417)

Multiplying with the factor
G_{\mathrm{CPW}}=G_{\mathrm{CDST}}
, which is
implied by (3.12) from
Czakon:2009ss
, and inserting the
couplings (
411
), we obtain (3.14)
from
Czakon:2009ss
.

D.3
FS

\boldsymbol{q^{\ast}\to\mathrm{g}q}

and

\boldsymbol{f^{\ast}\to\gamma f}

splitting functions

In the TU-polarized
f^{\ast}\to Vf
splitting function, which can be
obtained from the corresponding
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}

counterpart (

)
via (

), we set

\displaystyle m_{ij}=m_{ij}^{\prime}=m_{j},\qquad f_{ij^{\prime}}=f_{ij},\qquad I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{ij}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}=I_{f_{j}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{ij}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\equiv I_{f_{j}f_{ij}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}.

(418)

Taking the limit

m_{i}\to 0
,
only the contributions
\propto\not{\bar{p}}
survive,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{f_{ij}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{U}\big)\to\left|I_{f_{j}f_{ij}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\right|^{2}\left(\frac{2\big((z-1)^{2}+1\big)}{(z-1)z^{2}}\big(k_{\perp}^{2}-m_{j}^{2}z^{2}\big)-4m_{j}^{2}\right)\not{\bar{p}}.

(419)

Upon multiplication with the conventional factor

\displaystyle G_{\mathrm{CDST}}\to\frac{e^{2}}{8\pi\alpha_{s}}\frac{1}{2p_{i}p_{j}}=\frac{e^{2}}{8\pi\alpha_{s}}\frac{z(z-1)}{k_{\perp}^{2}-z^{2}m_{j}^{2}},

(420)

and setting

\displaystyle e^{2}\left|I_{f_{j}f_{ij}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\right|^{2}\to 4\pi\alpha_{s}C_{\mathrm{F}}\delta_{f_{ij}f_{j}},

(421)

we recover the unpolarized
q^{\ast}\to\mathrm{g}q
splitting function
from (4.7) of
catani2002massivedipole

for
D=4
dimensions,

\displaystyle G_{\mathrm{CDST}}\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{f_{ij}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\mathrm{U}\big)\to\delta_{f_{ij}f_{j}}C_{\mathrm{F}}\left(\frac{(z-1)^{2}+1}{z}-\frac{m_{j}^{2}}{p_{i}p_{j}}\right)\not{\bar{p}}.

(422)

To recover the polarized splitting functions for
f^{\ast}\to\gamma f

from (2.5) of
dittmaier1999photonrad
, we consider

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{f_{ij}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\kappa_{j}\big)

\displaystyle=\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\delta_{\kappa_{j}+}\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{f_{ij}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},+\big)+\delta_{\kappa_{j}-}\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{f_{ij}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},-\big)$\hss}

\displaystyle\to\left|I_{f_{j}f_{ij}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\right|^{2}\Bigg\{

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2\big(k_{\perp}^{2}-z^{2}m_{j}^{2}\big)}{z(z-1)}\frac{(z-1)^{2}+1}{z}-4m_{j}^{2}+\frac{2m_{j}^{2}z^{2}}{z-1}\right)\left(\delta_{\kappa_{j}+}\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{-}+\delta_{\kappa_{j}-}\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{+}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{2m_{j}^{2}z^{2}}{z-1}\left(\delta_{\kappa_{j}+}\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{+}+\delta_{\kappa_{j}-}\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{-}\right)\Bigg\},

(423)

where we inserted (
418
)
into
the
f^{\ast}\to Vf

splitting functions for
\kappa_{j}=+
and
\kappa_{j}=-
.
These two quantities are obtained from
the
\kappa_{j}=+
result
for
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}

branchings,
shown in (
252
),
via application of
(
269
)
and the combination of
(
258
) with
(
269
), respectively.
We also
took
m_{i}\to 0

and neglected terms
\propto\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}
and
\propto\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}
.
28
28

28

For more details regarding these structures, cf. Appendix
E.2
.

While all terms
\propto m_{j}^{2}
in the first parentheses of the third
line cancel, the following discussion becomes more transparent with this
form.
Replacing

\displaystyle e^{2}\left|I_{f_{j}f_{ij}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\right|^{2}\to Q_{f_{j}}^{2}\delta_{f_{ij}f_{j}}

(424)

and multiplying with the conventional
factor
G_{\mathrm{D}}

that is
implied by (2.4) from
dittmaier1999photonrad
,

\displaystyle G_{\mathrm{D}}\equiv\frac{1}{Q_{f_{j}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\big(p_{ij}^{2}-m_{ij}^{2}\big)^{2}}\to\frac{1}{Q_{f_{j}}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2p_{i}p_{j}}\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{Q_{f_{j}}^{2}}\left(\frac{z(z-1)}{k_{\perp}^{2}-z^{2}m_{j}^{2}}\right)^{2},

(425)

we get

\displaystyle G_{\mathrm{D}}\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{f_{ij}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\kappa_{j}\big)

\displaystyle\to\delta_{f_{ij}f_{j}}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{1}{p_{i}p_{j}}\bigg(\frac{(z-1)^{2}+1}{z}-\frac{m_{j}^{2}}{p_{i}p_{j}}\bigg)+\frac{m_{j}^{2}}{2(p_{i}p_{j})^{2}}\frac{z^{2}}{z-1}\bigg]\left(\delta_{\kappa_{j}+}\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{-}+\delta_{\kappa_{j}-}\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{+}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{m_{j}^{2}}{2(p_{i}p_{j})^{2}}\frac{z^{2}}{z-1}\left(\delta_{\kappa_{j}+}\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{+}+\delta_{\kappa_{j}-}\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{-}\right)\bigg\}.

(426)

In the conventions of
dittmaier1999photonrad
this corresponds to the
quantity
g^{\mathrm{(out)}}_{j,\pm}
, which turns into
g^{\mathrm{(out)}}_{i,\pm}

given in (2.5) there by
z\to 1-z
,
m_{j}\to m_{i}
.
According to (
438
), the
structures
\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{\pm}
represent, at leading order in
the quasi-collinear limit, outer products of spinors for
fermions with helicities
\mp\frac{1}{2}
, respectively.
These correspond to squared matrix elements for the hard process with
outgoing
fermions of helicities
\mp\frac{1}{2}
.
The “spin-preserving” splitting function
g^{\mathrm{(out)}}_{j,+}

from
dittmaier1999photonrad
can therefore be read off as the
coefficient in the second line
of (
426
), while the “spin-flipping”
splitting function
g^{\mathrm{(out)}}_{j,-}

appears as prefactor in the third line.

Finally, the results for polarized
q^{\ast}\to\mathrm{g}q
splittings
from (3.13) of
Czakon:2009ss
, summed over the two transverse
polarizations of the gluon, can be inferred from our results
as well.
For this, we identify in (
423
)

\displaystyle\not{\bar{p}}\omega_{\pm}\to\delta_{\kappa\kappa^{\prime}}\delta_{\kappa\mp}.

(427)

Here,
\kappa
and
\kappa^{\prime}
denote helicities of the
fermions
in the associated underlying matrix elements, as discussed
above.
Employing algebraic relations between Kronecker deltas, we eventually find

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{}^{f_{ij}}_{V_{i}}{}^{f_{ij}}_{f_{j}}\big(z,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\mathrm{T},\kappa_{j}\big)

\displaystyle\to\left|I_{f_{j}f_{ij}}^{\bar{V}_{i}}\right|^{2}\delta_{\kappa\kappa^{\prime}}\left\{\left(2\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}-z^{2}m_{j}^{2}}{z(z-1)}\frac{(z-1)^{2}+1}{z}+4m_{j}^{2}\frac{(z-1)^{2}+z}{z-1}\right)\delta_{\kappa\kappa_{j}}-\frac{2m_{j}^{2}z^{2}}{z-1}\right\}.

(428)

Multiplying this with the factor
G_{\mathrm{CPW}}=G_{\mathrm{CDST}}
,
inserting the couplings (
421
),
and setting
z\to 1-z
, we obtain
agreement with (3.13) from
Czakon:2009ss
, summed over the
transverse gluon polarizations.

D.4
IS

\boldsymbol{q\to q\mathrm{g}^{\ast}}

and

\boldsymbol{f\to f\gamma^{\ast}}

splitting functions

Since our procedure for the treatment of contributions involving
\gamma^{5}

follows the computation of the polarized
IS

f\to f\gamma^{\ast}
splitting

dittmaier2008polarized

results emerge as a special case from
our findings for general
IS

f\to fV^{\ast}
splittings.
The latter are obtained
via the crossing relation (
95
)
from their
FS

V^{\ast}\to\bar{f}f
counterparts
(similar to (
306
)),
which are themselves related to the corresponding
FS

V^{\ast}\to f\bar{f}

results of Section
4.3.1
as discussed in
Section
4.3.2
.
Specifically, we set

\displaystyle m_{a}=m_{i},\qquad V_{ai^{\prime}}=V_{ai},\qquad I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{L}}f_{a}^{\mathrm{L}}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}}=I_{f_{i}^{\mathrm{R}}f_{a}^{\mathrm{R}}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}}\equiv I_{f_{i}f_{a}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}},\qquad s_{\varepsilon}=1

(429)

in the
IS

f\to fV^{\ast}
splitting functions
and investigate the limit

\displaystyle m_{ai},m_{ai}^{\prime}\to 0,\qquad\frac{\hat{p}^{\mu}}{m_{ai}}=\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}-k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{m_{ai}}\to 0,\qquad\frac{\hat{p}^{\mu}}{m_{ai}^{\prime}}=\frac{\bar{p}^{\mu}-k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{m_{ai}^{\prime}}\to 0.

(430)

The last two
replacements correspond to the
WIs
for massless gauge bosons

V_{ai}
(such as
\gamma
or g).
Note that the limits
\hat{p}^{\mu}/m_{ai}\to 0
and
\bar{p}^{\mu}/m_{ai}\to 0
differ at leading mass order in the quasi-collinear limit.
In this way, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{a}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{a},\mathrm{U}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\delta_{\kappa_{a}+}\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{a}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,\mathrm{U}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}+\delta_{\kappa_{a}-}\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{a}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{a}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},-,\mathrm{U}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}$\hss}

\displaystyle\to\left|I_{f_{i}f_{a}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}}\right|^{2}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\frac{m_{a}^{2}x^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2}}{x-1}g^{\mu\nu}+\frac{4}{x^{2}}k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}

\displaystyle{}+\frac{\kappa_{a}}{x}\bigg((2-x)\frac{m_{a}^{2}x^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2}}{x-1}+2x^{2}m_{a}^{2}\bigg)\big(\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\nu}-\bar{\varepsilon}_{+}^{\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-}^{\nu}\big)\bigg\},

(431)

where the polarizations of the
FS
particle
i
have been summed
over.
Next, we replace

\displaystyle e^{2}\left|I_{f_{i}f_{a}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}}\right|^{2}\to N_{\mathrm{c},{f_{i}}}Q_{f_{i}}^{2}\delta_{f_{a}f_{i}}

(432)

and multiply with the factor

\displaystyle\bar{G}_{\mathrm{DKK}}\equiv\frac{1}{N_{\mathrm{c},{f_{i}}}Q_{f_{i}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\big(p_{ai}^{2}-m_{ai}^{2}\big)^{2}}\to\frac{1}{N_{\mathrm{c},{f_{i}}}Q_{f_{i}}^{2}}\frac{1}{p_{ai}^{4}}=\frac{1}{N_{\mathrm{c},{f_{i}}}Q_{f_{i}}^{2}}\left(\frac{x-1}{m_{a}^{2}x^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2}}\right)^{2},

(433)

which matches the conventions of (
80
)
and (5.2) from
dittmaier2008polarized
.
Swapping
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
, we obtain the splitting functions
from (5.3) of
dittmaier2008polarized
.
29
29

29

Equation (5.2) of
dittmaier2008polarized
associates the
index
\nu
to
T^{(n)}_{V_{ai}X}
, not the index
\mu
.

Similarly, we recover the polarized
q\to q\mathrm{g}^{\ast}
splitting

Czakon:2009ss
in the limit defined
by (
429

Specifically, we obtain
(setting
s_{\varepsilon}=-1
)

\displaystyle\bar{\varepsilon}^{\ast}_{\lambda,\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda^{\prime},\nu}\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{a}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},\kappa_{a},\kappa_{i}\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\bar{\varepsilon}^{\ast}_{\lambda,\mu}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda^{\prime},\nu}\Bigg\{\delta_{\kappa_{a}+}\delta_{\kappa_{i}+}\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{a}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,+\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}+\delta_{\kappa_{a}+}\delta_{\kappa_{i}-}\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{a}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},+,-\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}+\delta_{\kappa_{a}-}\delta_{\kappa_{i}+}\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{a}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},-,+\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}+\delta_{\kappa_{a}-}\delta_{\kappa_{i}-}\bigg[\bar{\mathcal{P}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{a}}{}^{V_{ai}}_{f_{i}}\big(x,k_{\perp}^{\mu},\bar{p}^{\mu},-,-\big)\bigg]^{\mu\nu}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\to\left|I_{f_{i}f_{a}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}}\right|^{2}\bigg\{\frac{4(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{\lambda^{\prime}})(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}^{\ast}_{\lambda})}{x^{2}}+\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2(x-1)}\delta_{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda}\left(4\delta^{\prime}_{\kappa_{a}\lambda}+\frac{4\left(2\delta_{\kappa_{a}\lambda}-1\right)}{x}\right)\bigg\}\delta_{\kappa_{a}\kappa_{i}},

(434)

similarly to the procedure described in Appendix
D.2
.
Replacing

\displaystyle e^{2}\left|I_{f_{i}f_{a}}^{\bar{V}_{ai}}\right|^{2}\to 4\pi\alpha_{s}C_{\mathrm{F}}\delta_{f_{a}f_{i}}

(435)

and multiplying with the conventional factor

\displaystyle\bar{G}_{\mathrm{CPW}}=-\frac{xe^{2}}{p_{ai}^{2}-m_{ai}^{2}}\to\frac{xe^{2}}{2p_{a}p_{i}}=e^{2}\frac{(x-1)x}{k_{\perp}^{2}},

(436)

we obtain (4.32) from
Czakon:2009ss

\displaystyle\frac{u_{i}(1-u_{i})}{p_{i}p_{j}}\left(\frac{p_{i}^{\mu}}{u_{i}}-\frac{p_{j}^{\mu}}{1-u_{i}}\right)\left(\frac{p_{i}^{\nu}}{u_{i}}-\frac{p_{j}^{\nu}}{1-u_{i}}\right)\to-2\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}}{k_{\perp}^{2}},

(437)

where the notation from
Czakon:2009ss
is used.

Appendix E
Further details on FS splittings of (anti-)fermions

E.1
Identification of matrix elements with definite (anti-)fermion polarizations

Here, we detail the relation of the
structures (
246
) to underlying
matrix elements of definite polarization, i.e. (
247
) and (
270
).
First, we focus on the forms
\not{\bar{p}}
and
\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}
.
The completeness relations (
78
) yield

\displaystyle u_{\pm}(p)\bar{u}_{\pm}(p)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\pm\gamma^{5}}{2}\not{p}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right)=\not{p}\frac{1\mp\gamma^{5}}{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right),

\displaystyle v_{\pm}(p)\bar{v}_{\pm}(p)

\displaystyle=\frac{1\mp\gamma^{5}}{2}\not{p}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right)=\not{p}\frac{1\pm\gamma^{5}}{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right).

(438)

For matrix elements with an external antifermion this implies,
assuming
m_{ij}=m_{ij}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\,\not{\bar{p}}\,T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+\,\ast}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-\,\ast}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right),

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\,\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\,T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+\,\ast}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}-\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-\,\ast}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right),

(439)

while for those with an external fermion

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}\,\not{\bar{p}}\,T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+}_{f_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+\,\ast}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-}_{f_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-\,\ast}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right),

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}\,\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\,T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-}_{f_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-\,\ast}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}-\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+}_{f_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+\,\ast}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right).

(440)

Note that the precise definition of the spin reference axes is
irrelevant for the considered approximation.

Next, we turn to the structures

\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}

and

\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}
.
For transverse polarization vectors
\varepsilon^{\mu}_{\pm}(p)
and
spinors
u_{\pm}(p)
,
v_{\pm}(p)
that are defined as helicity eigenvectors in
some arbitrary, but common frame,
we find the relations

\displaystyle\not{\varepsilon}_{\pm}(p)(\not{p}-m_{f})

\displaystyle=\pm\sqrt{2}u_{\pm}(p)\bar{v}_{\pm}(p),

\displaystyle\not{\varepsilon}_{\pm}(p)(\not{p}+m_{f})

\displaystyle=\pm\sqrt{2}v_{\mp}(p)\bar{u}_{\mp}(p),

\displaystyle\not{\varepsilon}_{\pm}(p)(\not{p}-m_{f})\gamma^{5}

\displaystyle=+\sqrt{2}u_{\pm}(p)\bar{u}_{\mp}(p),

\displaystyle\not{\varepsilon}_{\pm}(p)(\not{p}+m_{f})\gamma^{5}

\displaystyle=+\sqrt{2}v_{\mp}(p)\bar{v}_{\pm}(p),

(441)

where the conventions from
Dittmaier:1998nn
regarding
polarization vectors and Dirac matrices are followed (this involves

s_{\varepsilon}=+1
).
These identities
can be shown
either
with explicit parametrizations or by using the
Weyl--van-der-Waerden
spinor formalism.
30
30

30

The Weyl–van-der-Waerden
formalism expresses four-momenta, polarization vectors, and
Dirac spinors in terms of
Weyl–van-der-Waerden spinors
as common fundamental object
and thus provides a natural language
for this kind of relations.
In
Reyer:2024
, a proof of (
441
)
in terms of the spinor formalism from
Dittmaier:1998nn
is presented
(including some generalizations of the formalism to complex four-vectors).

Specifying the polarization vectors in (
441
) as
\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{\pm}

from (
190
), these
identities can be combined
into expressions for
\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}

and

\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}

via (
192
).
Using

\displaystyle u_{\pm}(\bar{p})=v_{\mp}(\bar{p})+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right),

(442)

and neglecting terms beyond leading mass order, we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\,\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\,T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}={}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-})\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+\,\ast}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}-\sqrt{2}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-\,\ast}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right),

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\,\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\,T^{(n)}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}={}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-})\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+\,\ast}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\sqrt{2}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+}_{\bar{f}_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-\,\ast}_{\bar{f}_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right)

(443)

and

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}\,\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}\,T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}={}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-})\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-}_{f_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+\,\ast}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}-\sqrt{2}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+}_{f_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-\,\ast}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right),

\displaystyle\bar{T}^{(n)}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}\,\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\,T^{(n)}_{f_{ij}X}={}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{-})\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-}_{f_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+\,\ast}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\sqrt{2}(k_{\perp}\bar{\varepsilon}_{+})\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,+}_{f_{ij}X}\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,-\,\ast}_{f_{ij^{\prime}}X}+\mathcal{O}\left(m_{f}\right).

(444)

With the conventions from
Cuomo:2019siu
,
there
is an extra sign in the relations
from (
441
) that involve

\varepsilon_{+}
.
31
31

31

The adaption of the formalism from
Dittmaier:1998nn
to the
conventions from
Cuomo:2019siu
is more involved than this
simple result suggests.
Specifically, this involves

\gamma^{i}_{\text{\cite[cite]{\@@bibref{Authors Phrase1YearPhrase2}{Dittmaier:1998nn}{\@@citephrase{(}}{\@@citephrase{)}}}}}=-\gamma^{i}_{\text{\cite[cite]{\@@bibref{Authors Phrase1YearPhrase2}{Cuomo:2019siu}{\@@citephrase{(}}{\@@citephrase{)}}}}}

for
i=1,2,3,5
and

\varepsilon^{\text{\cite[cite]{\@@bibref{Authors Phrase1YearPhrase2}{Dittmaier:1998nn}{\@@citephrase{(}}{\@@citephrase{)}}}}}_{\pm}(p)=\pm\exp\{\mp 2\mathrm{i}\phi\}\varepsilon^{\text{\cite[cite]{\@@bibref{Authors Phrase1YearPhrase2}{Cuomo:2019siu}{\@@citephrase{(}}{\@@citephrase{)}}}}}_{\pm}(p)
,
where
\phi
denotes the azimuthal angle of the momentum
p^{\mu}
w.r.t. the
z
-axis.
While the explicit representation of the
\gamma
-matrices is not
specified in
Cuomo:2019siu
, it may be reconstructed from the
parametrization of the spinors in (A.6)–(A.8) therein.

This is, however, compensated by
s_{\varepsilon}=-1

in (
192
), such that
that (
443
)
and (
444
) hold regardless.
We note that the precise definition of the fermion spin reference axis
is irrelevant for (
443
)
and (
444
), despite
u_{\pm}(p)
and

v_{\pm}(p)
in (
441
)
being defined as helicity spinors in frames where
\varepsilon_{\pm}^{\mu}

are transverse (which corresponds to frames where
\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}\parallel\boldsymbol{n}
for the choice
\varepsilon^{\mu}_{\pm}=\bar{\varepsilon}^{\mu}_{\pm}
made
in (
443
)
and (
444
)).
This is because helicity and chirality eigenspinors agree at leading
mass order
(up to the sign change for antifermions).

E.2
Suppression of terms involving

\boldsymbol{\not{k}_{\perp}}

In general, for
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to V\bar{f}
,
f^{\ast}\to Vf
,
\bar{f}^{\ast}\to H\bar{f}
, and
f^{\ast}\to Hf
splittings, the structures

\displaystyle\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}},\qquad\not{k}_{\perp}\not{\bar{p}}\gamma^{5}.

(445)

contribute to our unpolarized splitting functions if the couplings have
a non-trivial chiral structure or if the two fermion masses are not
equal, and to our polarized results even for non-chiral couplings and
equal fermion masses.
Similar terms have also been obtained and commented on
in
Kleiss:1986ct
for the computation of
FS

f^{\ast}\to\gamma f
splitting functions with polarized fermions, and
in
dittmaier2008polarized
for
IS

\gamma\to f\bar{f}^{\ast}

splittings with polarized photons.
These terms have, however, not been explicitly presented in the
literature so far.
32
32

32

The results from
Cuomo:2019siu
do contain corresponding
contributions, as discussed in Section
5.1
.
These results are, however, derived and presented in terms of
1\to 2
splitting amplitudes.
The relation to the
structures (
445
) that appear in
our factorization of squared amplitudes is not obvious,
see (
443
)
and (
444
).

This is due to the fact that the structures (
445
)
always appear with prefactors that vanish in the limit of massless
fermions, and that taking their azimuthal average around the collinear
axis provides further
suppression.
33
33

33

With the specific definition of the azimuthal integral from
Section
4.4.4
, these terms even vanish.

,
34
34

34

In
dittmaier2008polarized
, the
IS

\gamma\to f\bar{f}^{\ast}
splitting functions are considered in the context of the
application to the dipole subtraction formalism.
In principle, even subtraction terms that vanish for
m_{f}\to 0

may contribute to the massless limit of the integrated counterterms.
Due to the suppression from the azimuthal integral within the emission phase space,
this is not the case for the terms (
445
).
Thus, they are irrelevant for the construction of the dipole
subtraction formalism for light fermions.

Actually, it turns out that (
443
)
and (
444
) can be subject to
additional mass suppression, on top of the naive quasi-collinear scaling
used to obtain our results.
In that case, terms in (
445
) can be dropped even
before taking the azimuthal average.
As we discuss in the following, such a suppression mechanism indeed
exists in the
EWSM
and is due to the chirality structure of the
underlying amplitudes.

We first show that for any given hard process with an external
fermion, either the left-chiral amplitude

\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\mathrm{L}}_{fX}

is suppressed with respect to the right-chiral counterpart

\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\mathrm{R}}_{fX}
,
or vice versa.
This can be understood with the help of the following
argument from
Cuomo:2019siu
.
35
35

35

Note that the authors of
Cuomo:2019siu
use their
“mass–parity” argument in an entirely different context.
In fact, they do not consider the potential suppression of terms that
correspond to (
445
)
(via (
334
)).

The
EWSM
before SSB and gauge fixing is invariant under the
“mass–parity”
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetry

\displaystyle\Phi\to-\Phi,\qquad f^{\mathrm{L}}\to-f^{\mathrm{L}},

(446)

which is part of
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{\mathrm{w}}
.
In the spontaneously broken and
gauge-fixed theory, this transformation
corresponds to

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle\to-h,

\displaystyle G_{a}

\displaystyle\to-G_{a},

\displaystyle m

\displaystyle\to-m,

\displaystyle V_{a}

\displaystyle\to+V_{a},

\displaystyle f^{\mathrm{L}}

\displaystyle\to-f^{\mathrm{L}},

\displaystyle f^{\mathrm{R}}

\displaystyle\to+f^{\mathrm{R}},

(447)

where
m
collectively denotes all masses and the
vev

v
.
Invariance under this transformation implies that an amplitude
must scale with an even/odd power of
m
, respectively,
if it involves an even/odd number of external left-handed fermions, Higgs
bosons, and would-be Goldstone bosons.
Hence, for some
l\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\displaystyle\left.\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\mathrm{L}}_{fX}\middle/\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\mathrm{R}}_{fX}\right.\sim m^{2l+1},

(448)

such that one of the amplitudes is suppressed with respect to the other.
As a consequence, the products

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{R}\,\ast}

and

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{R}}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{L}\,\ast}

are mass suppressed w.r.t.
\left|\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{L}}\right|^{2}

or

\left|\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{R}}\right|^{2}
.
If either of the amplitudes vanishes, these statements become trivial.
Our factorization procedure implicitly assumes that one of them is non-zero.
Finally, this implies that the
contributions (
444
) are suppressed
w.r.t. (
440
), because the
amplitudes of definite polarization can, at leading order in
m
, be
substituted by amplitudes of definite chiralities,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\mathrm{L}/\mathrm{R}}_{fX}

\displaystyle=\big(\bar{u}_{\mp}(\bar{p})+\mathcal{O}\left(m\right)\big)T^{(n)}_{fX}=\mathcal{M}^{(n)\,\mp}_{fX}+\mathcal{O}\left(m\right).

(449)

The same arguments apply for amplitudes with an external antifermion and
generalize to more general gauge theories, as long as they exhibit a
symmetry like (
446
).
This concludes our claim that the Dirac structures
involving
\not{k}_{\perp}
are subleading.

Note, however, that the above arguments require all particle masses
m
to be
small compared to the relevant energy scales.
This is generally assumed in the computation of our splitting functions.
In
dittmaier2008polarized
and
Kleiss:1986ct
, however, only
the mass of the fermion that participates in the considered splittings
needs to be small.
This limits the applicability of our
statements in the corresponding situations.
